Conclusion: The compact implicit high-resolution scheme is effective for multicomponent transport problems with nonlinear Langmuir sorption, offering second-order accuracy and the ability to use large Courant numbers, making it advantageous over explicit methods for problems with steep fronts and discontinuities.

Abstract: We present a compact implicit high-resolution finite volume scheme for one-dimensional multicomponent transport problems with nonlinear Langmuir sorption. The method combines a second order compact implicit discretization with WENO reconstruction and a time limiter for the treatment of steep gradients and discontinuities. A special attention is given to the Courant number used in the limiter, where local characteristic Courant numbers based on the eigenvalues of the inverse retardation matrix are considered. The compact implicit schemes are compared with analogous explicit first and second order methods. Numerical experiments show second order convergence for smooth solutions and demonstrate that the implicit approach can be used with significantly larger Courant numbers. The method is also applied to a three-component displacement chromatography problem with sharp concentration fronts.

</details>


### [50] [A Discontinuous Galerkin Method for the Intrinsic Beam Model with Kelvin-Voigt Damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08397)
*Shivam Sundriyal,Christian Bleffert,Lukas Dreyer,Melven Röhrig-Zöllner,Gregor Gassner,Vadym Aizinger*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper presents an energy-stable discontinuous Galerkin (DG) spatial discretization for intrinsic beam equations with Kelvin-Voigt damping, reformulated by splitting sectional resultants into elastic and viscous parts, implemented in Trixi.jl.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a spatially discretized method for intrinsic beam equations with Kelvin-Voigt damping that is provably energy-stable, addressing challenges posed by mixed space-time derivatives in the original formulation.

Method: The governing equations are reformulated by splitting total sectional resultants into elastic and viscous components, producing a mixed system without mixed space-time derivatives. The DG discretization uses characteristic upwinding and alternating auxiliary traces to achieve semidiscrete energy stability for homogeneous cantilever split boundary closure.

Result: The method is implemented in Trixi.jl. Convergence is assessed via a physically compatible manufactured solution with all state and viscous components active. A rotating-beam test verifies the analytic constant-spin branch and the actuated energy balance.

Conclusion: The proposed energy-stable DG discretization provides a robust numerical framework for the intrinsic beam equations with Kelvin-Voigt damping, validated through both manufactured-solution convergence tests and rotating-beam physical tests.

Abstract: We present an energy-stable discontinuous Galerkin (DG) spatial discretization of the intrinsic beam equations with Kelvin-Voigt damping. We reformulate the governing equations by splitting the total sectional resultants into elastic and viscous parts, thereby exposing a mixed system without mixed space-time derivatives. For homogeneous cantilever boundary data, the continuous system obeys an energy dissipation identity. With characteristic upwinding and alternating auxiliary traces the DG discretization is energy stable at the semidiscrete level for the stated homogeneous split boundary closure. The method is implemented in the Julia-based simulation framework Trixi.jl. A physically compatible manufactured solution with all state and viscous components active assesses convergence, while a rotating-beam test verifies the analytic constant-spin branch and the actuated energy balance.

</details>


### [51] [Selective boundary condition reduction via learned error gating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08461)
*Daniel Fernández,Dominik Penk,Dominik Riedelbauch*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper introduces a framework for learning when a simpler reduced boundary condition can replace a more complex one in parametric PDEs. Paired solutions train a neural network to predict boundary and domain errors, and the simplified condition is only used when predicted errors meet tolerance thresholds. The method is evaluated on galvanic corrosion and other nonlinear problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Parametric PDEs can have multiple boundary condition formulations with different trade-offs between accuracy and computational cost. There is a need to automatically decide when a cheaper reduced boundary condition (e.g., a limiting Dirichlet form) can safely replace a more expensive stiff Robin or nonlinear boundary law.

Method: Paired solutions (using both the full and reduced boundary conditions) are used to train a neural network that estimates the domain error and boundary error introduced by the replacement. The reduced condition is applied only when both predicted errors fall below prescribed tolerances.

Result: The framework is evaluated on a galvanic corrosion problem and other nonlinear stationary and evolution problems, demonstrating its effectiveness.

Conclusion: The proposed learning framework enables adaptive selection of reduced boundary conditions in parametric PDEs, balancing accuracy and computational cost by only substituting simpler conditions when error predictions confirm they are acceptable.

Abstract: Parametric PDEs can admit different boundary conditions with different accuracy and computational cost. We introduce a framework for learning when one reduced boundary condition can replace another: paired solutions train a neural network to estimate the resulting domain and boundary errors, and the simpler condition is used only when both predicted errors meet prescribed tolerances. We focus on singular limits in applications, in which a stiff Robin or nonlinear boundary law is replaced by its limiting Dirichlet form. We evaluate the method on a galvanic corrosion problem and other nonlinear stationary and evolution problems.

</details>


### [52] [Cahn-Hilliard phase-field modeling of tumor growth via locally adaptive isogeometric analysis with THB-splines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08479)
*Dhiraj S. Bombarde,Carlotta Giannelli,Alessandro Reali,Guillermo Lorenzo*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper presents a tumor growth model based on the Cahn-Hilliard phase-field equation, solved using isogeometric analysis (IGA) with locally adaptive truncated hierarchical B-splines (THB-splines). The model is validated on benchmark cases and applied to a patient-specific MRI-reconstructed breast geometry, successfully reproducing tumor morphologies from spheroidal to fingered growth patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Predicting tumor dynamics under physiologically relevant conditions using mathematical models is challenging. The Cahn-Hilliard-based tumor growth model involves a fourth-order differential operator requiring high continuity in approximation spaces, which standard finite element methods struggle with and need mixed or auxiliary-variable approaches.

Method: The authors use isogeometric analysis (IGA) with spline-based basis functions to naturally satisfy the continuity requirements of the fourth-order CH operator, eliminating the need for mixed formulations. A locally adaptive IGA scheme using truncated hierarchical B-splines (THB-splines) reduces computational cost while maintaining accuracy. The model is validated on benchmarks and applied to an organ-scale, patient-specific breast geometry from MRI data.

Result: The model reproduces known tumor morphologies ranging from spheroidal patterns to fingered growth. Numerical experiments demonstrate diversity in tumor dynamics across different parameter choices, and the approach works effectively on a patient-specific breast model reconstructed from MRI.

Conclusion: The CH-based phase-field tumor growth model integrated with a locally adaptive IGA framework demonstrates strong predictive potential for simulating tumor dynamics in realistic, patient-specific geometries.

Abstract: Predicting tumor dynamics in biological systems under physiologically relevant conditions using mathematical and computational models remains a challenging problem. Continuum models based on phase-field (diffuse-interface) formulations have proven to be an effective modeling strategy to govern tumor dynamics and interactions of multiple species. Within this framework, the present work investigates a tumor growth model based on the Cahn-Hilliard (CH) equation. The formulation involves a fourth-order differential operator that imposes higher continuity requirement on approximation spaces for a well-defined primal variational formulation. To address this challenge, we use isogeometric analysis (IGA), which inherently satisfies this requirement through spline-based basis functions and eliminates the need for mixed or auxiliary-variable approaches commonly used in standard finite element discretizations. Additionally, a locally adaptive IGA scheme with truncated hierarchical B-splines (THB-splines) is used to reduce computational cost while maintaining accuracy. The model is first evaluated on standard benchmark cases and then applied to an organ-scale, patient-specific geometric model of the breast reconstructed from magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) data. Our results show that the model reproduces known tumor morphologies, ranging from a spheroidal pattern to fingered growth. A series of numerical experiments further shows the diversity of tumor dynamics produced by different model parameter choices. Our findings demonstrate the predictive potential of the CH-based phase-field tumor growth model integrated with a locally adaptive IGA framework.

</details>


### [53] [A Fully Dual Domain Decomposition Method for Extreme Learning Machines Using GenEO Spectral Coarse Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08630)
*Chang-Ock Lee,Byungeun Ryoo*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel nonoverlapping domain decomposition method (DDM) for Extreme Learning Machines (ELMs) that treats continuity and flux conditions equally, and constructs additive Schwarz preconditioners with GenEO coarse spaces to accelerate training of ELM-based PDE solvers, achieving weak scalability up to 4,096 subdomains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: ELMs are effective for solving PDEs but solving large least squares problems is computationally expensive, limiting their use in large-scale problems or high-accuracy applications. Existing DDMs for ELMs, even those with coarse spaces, struggle with scalability, so a more efficient parallel approach is needed.

Method: The authors develop a nonoverlapping DDM for ELMs where continuity and flux conditions across subdomain interfaces are treated and enforced equally. They construct additive Schwarz preconditioners and GenEO (Generalized Eigenvalues problems in the Overlaps) coarse spaces to accelerate convergence of the method.

Result: Numerical experiments demonstrate that the new method outperforms previous DDMs for ELMs in both iteration count and accuracy, particularly for Stokes and wave equations. Weak scalability is observed up to 4,096 subdomains when using GenEO coarse spaces.

Conclusion: The proposed nonoverlapping DDM with GenEO coarse spaces significantly improves scalability and accuracy of ELM-based PDE solvers, addressing key limitations of prior approaches and enabling large-scale parallel computations.

Abstract: Extreme learning machines (ELMs) are a class of machine learning algorithms that randomly intialize and subsequently fix the hidden layer parameters, and solve for the last layer coefficients using a least squares method. ELMs have been successfully applied to the solution of various partial differential equations, but the cost of solving large least squares problems limits their use in large scale problems or when high accuracy is desired. To address this limitation, domain decomposition methods (DDMs) have been developed for ELMs, reducing training times via parallel computation. Yet these methods, even those incorporating a coarse space, have struggled with scalability. This paper introduces a novel nonoverlapping DDM for ELMs that treats and enforces continuity and flux conditions equally. Additive Schwarz preconditioners and GenEO (Generalized Eigenvalues problems in the Overlaps) coarse spaces are constructed to accelerate the method. Numerical experiments show the new method outperforms previous DDMs for ELMs in both iteration count and accuracy, especially for the Stokes and wave equations. Furthermore, weak scalability has been observed in up to 4,096 subdomains when employing GenEO coarse spaces.

</details>


### [54] [Off-Grid Point-Scatterer Localization from Sparse Limited-Aperture Data via Hankel Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08665)
*Jingzhi Li,Xiliang Lu,Juntao You*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper studies localizing s point scatterers from sparsely sampled single-incidence, single-frequency acoustic far-field data over a limited receiver aperture under the first Born approximation. It introduces a unified framework for structured completion and off-grid localization using aperture-admissible Hankel pencils, establishes exact and stable recovery guarantees with explicit localization bounds, and provides quantitative criteria for selecting sample-efficient Hankel pencils.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Limited receiver apertures generally admit multiple aperture-admissible Hankel pencils with different lifted dimensions, sampling multiplicities, and Vandermonde conditioning. The choice of Hankel pencil affects both data completion and localization performance, yet no unified framework or rigorous recovery guarantees existed for this setting.

Method: The authors introduce a unified framework for structured completion and off-grid localization based on general aperture-admissible Hankel pencils. They establish exact recovery guarantees in the noiseless case and stable recovery under bounded noise with sample complexity linear in s, deriving explicit localization bounds in terms of completion error. The analysis separates pencil geometry effects from source-configuration-induced conditioning and provides verifiable sufficient conditions for stable completion and MUSIC resolution.

Result: The framework achieves exact recovery in the noiseless case and stable recovery under bounded noise from a number of random aperture samples linear in s (up to factors from pencil geometry, source conditioning, and logarithms), with an explicit localization bound. Quantitative criteria are derived for selecting sample-efficient Hankel pencils under prescribed physical apertures, validated through numerical experiments on connected and disconnected apertures.

Conclusion: The paper provides quantitative guidelines for Hankel pencil selection in limited-aperture acoustic localization, demonstrating that pencil geometry significantly impacts both completion accuracy and localization resolution, with the analysis cleanly separating geometry-induced from source-induced conditioning factors.

Abstract: We study the localization of $s$ distinct planar point scatterers from sparsely sampled single-incidence, single-frequency acoustic far-field data over a limited receiver aperture. Under the first Born approximation, the coherent data form a two-dimensional off-grid exponential sum and admit a low-rank Hankel representation. A limited aperture generally admits multiple aperture-admissible Hankel pencils with different lifted dimensions, sampling multiplicities, and Vandermonde conditioning. Hence, the choice of the Hankel pencil affects both data completion and subsequent localization.
  In this work, we introduce a unified framework for structured completion and off-grid localization based on general aperture admissible Hankel pencils. We establish exact recovery in the noiseless case and stable recovery under bounded noise from a number of random aperture samples that is linear in $s$, up to factors determined by the pencil geometry, source conditioning, and logarithmic terms, together with an explicit localization bound in terms of the completion error. The analysis separates the contribution of the pencil geometry from the conditioning induced by the source configuration and provides verifiable sufficient conditions for stable completion and MUSIC resolution. These results yield quantitative criteria for selecting sample efficient Hankel pencils under a prescribed physical aperture. Numerical experiments on connected and disconnected apertures demonstrate the effects of pencil geometry, sampling, and noise.

</details>


### [55] [Strong convergence rates of tamed exponential Euler schemes for superlinear hyperbolic SPDEs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08872)
*Katharina Klioba*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper proves pathwise uniform convergence of order up to 1/2 for tamed exponential Euler schemes applied to semilinear hyperbolic SPDEs with superlinearly growing nonlinearities and multiplicative noise, extending prior results from globally to locally Lipschitz settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish convergence guarantees for numerical schemes applied to hyperbolic stochastic evolution equations that lack parabolic smoothing, where nonlinearities may grow superlinearly and only local Lipschitz conditions hold—an extension beyond existing results for parabolic SPDEs and globally Lipschitz non-parabolic cases.

Method: The authors employ tamed exponential Euler schemes with stopped and fractionally tamed variants. They impose local Lipschitz, polynomial growth, coercivity, and monotonicity conditions on nonlinearities, and work within a stochastic Kato framework to establish well-posedness and a priori estimates before proving the strong error bound (E max_{0≤j≤N} ||U(t_j)-U^j||_X^p)^{1/p} ≲ √k on a Hilbert space X for p ∈ [2,∞).

Result: Pathwise uniform strong error estimates of order O(√k) are established for mildly increasing step sizes, covering both drift and diffusion with polynomial growth. Applications include nonlinear stochastic transport, Airy, wave-type, and damped nonlinear Schrödinger equations, with the Klein-Gordon equation with cubic velocity damping treated as a complement to prior additive-noise results.

Conclusion: The work demonstrates that tamed exponential Euler schemes achieve optimal-order strong convergence for hyperbolic SPDEs even under superlinear growth of nonlinearities, thereby broadening the applicability of numerical methods to a wider class of stochastic PDEs without parabolic smoothing.

Abstract: In this paper, we prove pathwise uniform convergence at rates up to $1/2$ for tamed exponential Euler schemes for semilinear hyperbolic stochastic evolution equations with superlinearly growing nonlinearities and multiplicative noise. We take the term hyperbolic to mean that the leading operator generates a contractive $C_0$-semigroup but no parabolic smoothing occurs. Under local Lipschitz, polynomial growth, coercivity, and monotonicity conditions on the nonlinearities, we establish pathwise uniform strong error estimates of the form \begin{equation*}
  \Big(\mathbb{E}\max_{0\le j \le N} \|U(t_j)-U^j\|_X^p\Big)^{1/p} \lesssim \sqrt{k} \end{equation*} on a Hilbert space $X$ for $p\in [2,\infty)$. Here, $U$ is the mild solution and $U^j$ is the tamed exponential Euler approximation at time $t_j=jk$ with step size $k>0$. This extends previous convergence results for non-parabolic SPDEs from globally to locally Lipschitz nonlinearities, allowing both drift and diffusion to grow polynomially. In a stochastic Kato framework, we further establish local and global well-posedness as well as uniform a priori estimates for the mild solution and its approximation. Applications to nonlinear stochastic transport, Airy, wave-type, and dissipatively damped nonlinear Schrödinger equations are included, covering different nonlinearities-stopped and fractionally tamed schemes. For the Klein-Gordon equation with cubic velocity damping, this complements previous results obtained for additive noise.

</details>


### [56] [High-Order Discontinuous Cut Finite Element Methods for Scalar Hyperbolic Conservation Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08880)
*Pei Fu,Gunilla Kreiss,Zelin Xin,Sara Zahedi*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper presents a family of high-order cut discontinuous Galerkin (DG) finite element methods for scalar hyperbolic conservation laws on complex domains, combining macro-element stabilization with flux limiting to preserve the maximum principle while remaining robust against arbitrary boundary cuts of the background mesh.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cut cell methods can produce arbitrarily small cells that cause severe time step restrictions and instability. The authors aim to develop a robust DG-based cut finite element method (CutFEM) that maintains stability, accuracy, and physical properties (maximum principle, no spurious oscillations) even with challenging boundary cuts.

Method: The physical domain is embedded in a regular background mesh. Ghost penalty stabilizations on interior facets of macro-elements handle arbitrarily small cut cells. Macro-element-based parameterized flux limiter plus adaptations of Zhang-Shu bound-preserving limiter and Barth-Jespersen slope limiter enforce the maximum principle. An L²-stability result is derived for the semi-discrete scheme under periodic and inflow-outflow boundary conditions.

Result: Numerical experiments in 2D and 3D demonstrate optimal convergence orders, preservation of the maximum principle, and accurate shock capturing without spurious oscillations, even for challenging cut configurations with very small cut-cell intersections.

Conclusion: The macro-element-stabilized cut DG method with integrated flux limiters achieves stability and accuracy comparable to standard DG on fitted meshes, robustly handling arbitrary boundary cuts while preserving physical solution properties.

Abstract: In this paper, we present a family of high-order cut finite element methods based on the discontinuous Galerkin (DG) framework for scalar hyperbolic conservation laws on complex domains. Building on our previous work, we develop a multidimensional formulation that combines macro-element stabilization with flux limiting to obtain a scheme that preserves the maximum principle and remains robust with respect to arbitrary boundary cuts of the background mesh. The physical domain is embedded in a regular background mesh, which may produce arbitrarily small cut cells. To avoid the severe time step restrictions typically associated with such cells, ghost penalty stabilization terms are added on interior facets of macro-elements. The resulting method exhibits stability and accuracy properties similar to those of standard DG methods on fitted meshes. An $L^2$-stability result is derived for the semi-discrete scheme under both periodic and inflow-outflow boundary conditions. To enforce the maximum principle and suppress nonphysical oscillations, we adapt limiter techniques from standard DG methods to the CutFEM setting by defining limiting parameters on macro-elements. In particular, we present a macro-element-based parameterized flux limiter together with adaptations of the Zhang-Shu bound-preserving limiter and the Barth-Jespersen slope limiter. Numerical experiments in two and three spatial dimensions demonstrate optimal convergence orders, preservation of the maximum principle, and accurate shock capturing without spurious oscillations, even for challenging cut configurations involving very small-cut cell intersections.

</details>


### [57] [On the well-posedness and efficient approximation for the classical Melan equation in suspension bridges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08912)
*Jinxiang Wang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the classical Melan equation for suspension bridges, establishing existence and uniqueness of solutions under explicit load conditions, and develops an efficient iterative approximation method with proven geometric convergence demonstrated on two real bridges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical Melan equation governing suspension bridge deflection lacks rigorous analytical guarantees and efficient computational methods, motivating the need for a thorough study of solution properties and practical approximation schemes applicable in engineering settings.

Method: The authors first investigate the explicit analytical solution and global properties of a simplified "less stiff" model, from which they derive an a priori estimate for the original Melan equation. They establish uniqueness with nonnegative integral under downward live loads via an explicit load condition, then develop an iterative method using the simplified model's solution as the first iterate, proving geometric convergence with explicit error estimates.

Result: Numerical calculations for two actual bridges demonstrate the applicability and computational efficiency of the method, quantify the influence of the nonlinear nonlocal term, and clarify the relationship between simplified and original models. Several engineering observations are verified and explained.

Conclusion: The paper provides rigorous mathematical guarantees for the Melan equation's solution uniqueness and an efficiently convergent numerical scheme, while suggesting several related open problems for future research.

Abstract: The classical Melan equation modeling suspension bridges is considered. We first study the explicit expression and global properties of the analytical solution for the simplified ``less stiff'' model, based on which we derive an a priori estimate for the original classical Melan equation and establish, under an explicit load condition, that it has a unique solution with nonnegative integral under downward live loads, thereby showing the uniqueness of the corresponding deflection curve in the engineering setting. We also develop an efficient iterative approximation method by taking the solution of the simplified ``less stiff'' model as the first iterate, and prove its geometric convergence with explicit error estimates. The applicability and computational efficiency of the method are demonstrated through calculations for two actual bridges, which also quantify the influence of the nonlinear nonlocal term on the solution and clarify the relationship between the simplified and original models. Several engineering observations are verified and explained, and some related open problems are suggested.

</details>


### [58] [On the sample complexity of the active subspace method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08940)
*Fabio Nobile,Matteo Raviola,Raúl Tempone*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the empirical active subspace method directly in the projection-error metric relevant for ridge approximation, deriving improved non-asymptotic quasi-optimality bounds via a regularized inverse Christoffel function, and showing that gradient smoothness (RKHS membership) yields sharper coherence estimates, motivates kernel diagonal importance sampling, and provides a priori decay bounds for active subspace tail energy—enabling full a priori sample complexity characterization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical analyses of active subspaces control the covariance error in operator norm, leading to sample-complexity estimates that are substantially more pessimistic than the sampling rules used in practice. The paper seeks a more accurate theoretical account of why empirically observed sampling rates suffice.

Method: The authors analyze the empirical active subspace method in the projection-error metric for ridge approximation, using a regularized inverse Christoffel function associated with the gradient field. They derive non-asymptotic quasi-optimality bounds, and under an RKHS smoothness assumption on the gradient map, obtain sharper coherence estimates and propose importance sampling from kernel diagonal measures. They also derive a priori decay bounds for the population active subspace tail energy, which combine with finite-sample estimates to prescribe rank, regularization scale, and sample size.

Result: Under bounded-gradient assumptions, the derived estimates already improve upon sample-complexity bounds from operator-norm covariance analyses. RKHS smoothness yields tighter coherence estimates, tractable importance sampling schemes, and a priori decay of tail energy. The assumptions and theory are verified for lognormal Gaussian and affine uniform parametric elliptic PDEs using weighted summability of Hermite and Legendre series expansions.

Conclusion: The paper provides a fully a priori characterization of active subspace sample complexity—including rank, regularization scale, and sample size—under verifiable assumptions, substantially closing the gap between theoretical guarantees and computational practice.

Abstract: Active subspaces identify low-dimensional linear structure in high-dimensional parameter-to-output maps by estimating the dominant eigenspace of a gradient covariance operator. In practice this covariance is replaced by a Monte Carlo estimator built from a limited number of gradient evaluations. Classical analyses based on controlling the covariance error in operator norm lead to sample-complexity estimates that can be substantially more pessimistic than the sampling rules commonly used in computations. This paper studies the empirical active subspace method directly in the projection-error metric relevant for ridge approximation. We derive non-asymptotic quasi-optimality bounds governed by a regularized inverse Christoffel function associated with the gradient field. Under a bounded-gradient assumption, the resulting estimates already improve the sample-complexity estimates obtained from operator-norm covariance bounds. We then show that additional smoothness of the gradient map, expressed through membership in a reproducing kernel Hilbert space, yields sharper coherence estimates and motivates tractable importance sampling from kernel diagonal measures. Furthermore, the same smoothness assumption yields a priori decay bounds for the population active subspace tail energy, which can be combined with our finite-sample estimate to prescribe rank, regularization scale, and sample size, allowing to fully characterize the a priori sample complexity. The abstract assumptions are verified for lognormal Gaussian and affine uniform parametric elliptic PDEs using weighted summability of Hermite and Legendre series expansions.

</details>


### [59] [Sharp Computational Bounds for Spectral Types of Schrödinger Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08945)
*Matthew J. Colbrook,George Coote*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper proves sharp complexity bounds for computing the spectral-type decomposition (pure point, absolutely continuous, singular continuous) of Schrödinger operators. It shows that pure point and absolutely continuous spectra cannot generally be recovered by any single limiting procedure, while singular continuous spectrum requires at least two nested limits—and these bounds are provably optimal via matching wavelet-based constructive upper bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In the spirit of Smale's program on the foundations of computation, the authors ask whether the spectral-type decomposition of Schrödinger operators can be effectively computed from a finite description of the operator. This is important for computer-assisted proofs and certified scientific computing, where understanding what is fundamentally computable (and at what computational cost in terms of nested limit procedures) is essential.

Method: The paper combines two complementary strategies. For lower bounds (impossibility results), it constructs explicit analytic dichotomies: Gordon-type repeated-potential constructions for pure point spectrum, high-barrier potentials for absolutely continuous spectrum, and an inverse-spectral construction using Riesz products, moment-killing perturbations, and a computational Gelfand-Levitan scheme for singular continuous spectrum. For upper bounds (positive computability results), it uses wavelet-based certified computation for self-adjoint differential operators on R^d with locally bounded variation coefficients.

Result: A sharp hierarchy is established: two nested limiting procedures are necessary and sufficient for pure point and absolutely continuous spectral sets, while three nested limiting procedures are necessary and sufficient for the singular continuous part. Lower bounds are realized by explicit analytic constructions, and matching upper bounds are obtained via certified wavelet computation frameworks.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a complete, sharp computational complexity hierarchy for spectral-type decomposition of Schrödinger operators, connecting constructive analysis, spectral theory, and the theory of computation. The finite-description framework also implies fundamental limitations on what can be certified within fixed formal systems, such as those used in computer-assisted proofs.

Abstract: We prove sharp bounds for determining the spectral-type decomposition of Schrödinger operators in the spirit of Smale's program on the foundations of computation. For explicit one-dimensional self-adjoint Schrödinger operators $H=-{\mathrm d^2}/{\mathrm dx^2}+V$ on $L^2(\mathbb R)$, where $V\in C^\infty(\mathbb R;\mathbb R)$ is given by a finite description of all derivatives and derivative bounds, the pure point and absolutely continuous spectral sets cannot, in general, be recovered by any single limiting procedure. The singular continuous spectral set is strictly harder: it cannot, in general, be recovered by two nested limiting procedures. Analytic constructions of dichotomies realize the lower bounds: Gordon-type repetitions for pure point spectrum, high barriers for absolutely continuous spectrum, and an inverse spectral construction for singular continuous spectrum based on Riesz products, moment-killing perturbations, and a computational Gelfand--Levitan scheme. The finite-description framework also implies corresponding limitations on what can be certified in fixed formal systems (e.g., when used in computer-assisted proofs). Conversely, using wavelet-based certified computation, we prove matching upper bounds for broad classes of self-adjoint differential operators on $\mathbb R^d$ with coefficients of locally bounded variation and quantitative local variation control: two limits suffice for the pure point and absolutely continuous parts, and three for the singular continuous part. This provides a sharp hierarchy for spectral types.

</details>


### [60] [Quadratic Point Estimate Method for Uncertainty Quantification with Dependent Non-Gaussian Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09053)
*Minhyeok Ko,Konstantinos G. Papakonstantinou*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper extends the Quadratic Point Estimate Method (QPEM) by integrating copula theory, enabling it to model joint input probability density functions through marginals and dependence structures. The enhanced copula-based QPEM maintains analytical sample locations and weights while capturing up to fifth-order input moments, and is validated against sampling methods in two practical scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of Point Estimate Methods (PEM) whose sigma points grow linearly with problem dimensions, and to extend QPEM's capabilities to handle non-Gaussian dependence structures through copula integration for more accurate probabilistic moment estimation.

Method: The authors integrate copulas into the QPEM framework, allowing decomposition of joint input PDFs into marginal distributions and dependence structures. The method preserves QPEM's analytical expressions for sample locations and weights (representing up to fifth-order input moments in Gaussian space) while adding flexible dependence modeling.

Result: The copula-based QPEM demonstrates validity and efficient performance compared to numerous other sampling methods across examples involving: (i) joint dependence structures inferred from data, and (ii) scenarios where only marginal distributions and correlation matrices are available.

Conclusion: The enhanced QPEM with copula integration provides an accurate and computationally competitive method for evaluating probabilistic moments of QoI in high-dimensional problems with complex dependence structures, complementing the original QPEM's advantages over PEM-based approaches.

Abstract: As an extension of the Point Estimate Method (PEM) to evaluate probabilistic moments of quantities of interest (QoI) in general $n$-dimensional spaces, the Quadratic Point Estimate Method (QPEM) has been recently developed. This new method is defined to fully represent up to fifth-order input moments in the Gaussian space, providing general analytical expressions for sample locations and weights, without requiring any numerical optimization. The QPEM can significantly improve the estimation accuracy of the output QoI moments, in relation to PEM-based methods whose numbers of sigma points grow linearly with the problem dimension, while at the same time having an affordable and competitive computational cost up to a considerable number of dimensions. The QPEM is further enhanced in this work by enabling copula integration into the framework, which enables effective modeling of the joint input probability density function by estimating marginals and the dependence structure of the involved random variables. The validity and efficient performance of the copula-based QPEM are showcased against numerous other sampling methods in various examples considering two practical scenarios: (i) when the joint dependence structure can be inferred from data, and (ii) when only marginal distributions and correlation matrices are known.

</details>

### [61] [The stability conjecture for the Bernoulli problem in dimension four](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05669)
*Xavier Fernández-Real,Joaquim Serra*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves a stability conjecture for the one-phase Bernoulli problem in R^4: every global classical stable solution is one-dimensional, meaning its free boundary consists of one or two parallel hyperplanes. This leads to universal local Hessian and free-boundary curvature estimates, as well as axial symmetry results for finite Morse index solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the stability conjecture for the Bernoulli free boundary problem specifically in dimension four, establishing rigidity of stable global solutions and providing curvature and symmetry consequences.

Method: The paper employs rigorous mathematical analysis techniques—likely involving tools from nonlinear PDE, free boundary regularity theory, dimension reduction, and monotonicity or blow-down arguments—to classify stable solutions in R^4.

Result: Every global classical stable solution in R^4 is one-dimensional; if nonempty, the free boundary consists of one or two parallel hyperplanes. This yields universal local Hessian and free-boundary curvature estimates and axial symmetry of global solutions with finite Morse index.

Conclusion: The stability conjecture for the Bernoulli problem in dimension four is settled, with extensions to local estimates and symmetry classification for finite Morse index solutions.

Abstract: We prove that every global classical stable solution to the one-phase Bernoulli problem in $\mathbb R^4$ is one-dimensional. In particular, if nonempty, its free boundary consists of one or two parallel hyperplanes. This settles the stability conjecture for the Bernoulli problem in dimension four. As consequences, we obtain universal local Hessian and free-boundary curvature estimates for classical stable solutions in $\mathbb R^4$, as well as axial symmetry of global classical solutions with finite Morse index in $\mathbb R^4$.

</details>


### [62] [Global asymptotic stability of KdV-Burgers fronts in a weakly two-dimensional model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05697)
*Jared C. Bronski,Olivia Clifton,Vera Mikyoung Hur*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the stability of front-type solutions in a two-dimensional nonlinear dispersive-dissipative PDE system that generalizes the Korteweg-de Vries-Burgers (KdVB) equation with weak transverse motion. The authors prove that one-dimensional KdVB fronts are global asymptotic attractors under general two-dimensional perturbations, provided the channel is sufficiently narrow and the dispersion parameter lies in a stability range. The front is unique up to spatial translations, and the proof extends the energy method from the 1D setting to handle transverse dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the propagation and stability of undular bores in a real two-dimensional channel, since previous work was limited to one-dimensional settings despite physical phenomena occurring in two dimensions.

Method: Extends the energy method for temporally-modulated perturbed solutions, originally developed for one-dimensional fronts, to accommodate weak transverse dynamics in a two-dimensional channel. The analysis relies on the channel being sufficiently narrow in the transverse direction and the relative dispersion parameter being in a range that guarantees one-dimensional stability.

Result: It is proven that one-dimensional fronts are global asymptotic attractors under general two-dimensional perturbations when the channel is sufficiently narrow and the dispersion parameter lies in the 1D stability range. Additionally, the front is shown to be unique up to spatial translations.

Conclusion: The stability of one-dimensional undular bore fronts extends to weakly two-dimensional settings under appropriate narrow-channel conditions, confirming their role as robust attractors in the 2D PDE system.

Abstract: We study front-type solutions of nonlinear dispersive-dissipative PDEs modeling the propagation of undular bores in a channel in two dimensions. The system extends the Korteweg-de Vries-Burgers (KdVB) equation by incorporating weak transverse motion, and admits the KdVB fronts as one-dimensional solutions. We investigate their stability under general two-dimensional perturbations. We prove that a one-dimensional front is a global asymptotic attractor in the weakly two-dimensional setting when the channel is sufficiently narrow in the transverse direction and the relative dispersion parameter lies in a range for stability to one-dimensional perturbations. Particularly, the front is unique up to spatial translations. The proof extends the energy method for temporally-modulated perturbed solutions, developed previously in the one-dimensional setting, to accommodate the transverse dynamics.

</details>


### [63] [On Decay of the Local Mean Oscillations of the Vorticity Direction in Critical Navier-Stokes Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05720)
*Zoran Grujic*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper derives and analyzes the geometric PDE governing vorticity direction evolution in 3D incompressible Navier-Stokes equations near a critical spatial point singularity with O(|x|^-2) vorticity concentration in L^{3/2,∞}. The PDE combines Harmonic Map Heat Flow with fluid transport, cross-diffusion and tangential strain. A transfer theorem is established showing that BMO regularity of the direction at the core is controlled by a logarithmic time modulus of inner-scale direction plus physical strain, where strain enters only via its tangential component vanishing for strain eigenvectors — in contrast to classical criteria requiring weak stretching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the Navier-Stokes mechanics can propagate logarithmic decay of local mean oscillations of the vorticity direction — a condition preventing finite-time blow-up at critical singularities — thereby addressing regularity versus blow-up in a setting where classical criteria (requiring depletion of full vortex stretching) are restricted to weak stretching configurations.

Method: The authors isolate the geometric PDE for the vorticity direction governed by Harmonic Map Heat Flow plus transport, cross-diffusion and tangential strain. Key structural observations: (1) the O(|x|^-2) concentration produces an outward radial drift 4ν x/|x|^2 at the core; (2) the HMHF nonlinearity is harmless for the quantity ½|ξVec − e|², which is a subsolution on any hemisphere. These yield a transfer theorem relating core BMO regularity to a logarithmic time modulus and strain.

Result: A transfer theorem is proved: BMO_{1/|log r|} regularity of the vorticity direction at the core, uniform up to the singular time, is controlled by a logarithmic modulus in time of the direction at the inner scale together with physical strain. The strain's contribution enters exclusively through the tangential component P_{ξVec⟂}SξVec, which vanishes when the direction is an eigenvector of the strain tensor (including the maximal-stretching eigenvector).

Conclusion: The geometric structure of the direction equation differs fundamentally from classical vortex-stretching based criteria: since the tangential strain vanishes precisely at strain eigenvectors — including maximal stretching — the regularity mechanism can operate even in strong stretching regimes, unlike classical criteria that require weak stretching configurations. This suggests the direction equation's geometric regularization is independent of depleting full vortex stretching.

Abstract: We isolate and analyze the geometric PDE governing the evolution of the vorticity direction in the 3D incompressible Navier-Stokes equations (NSE), restricted to the case of a critical spatial point singularity where the vorticity magnitude concentrates as $O(|x|^{-2})$, inhabiting the critical Lorentz space $L^{3/2, \infty}$. The PDE consists of the Harmonic Map Heat Flow (HMHF) into the sphere supplemented with the fluid transport, cross-diffusion and tangential strain. The question is whether the NSE mechanics can propagate logarithmic decay of the local mean oscillations of the direction -- the condition $\xiVec \in \bmo_{1/|\log r|}$ which (in this setting) was shown in the companion paper to prevent finite time blow-up. The key observations are that the $O(|x|^{-2})$ concentration, factored out of the viscous cross-diffusion, generates an outward radial drift $4ν\, x/|x|^2$ at the core and that the HMHF nonlinearity is harmless for the quantity $\frac12|\xiVec - e|^2$, which is a subsolution on any hemisphere. This yields a transfer theorem: the $\bmo_{1/|\log r|}$ regularity of the direction at the core, uniformly up to the singular time, is controlled by a logarithmic modulus in time of the direction at the inner scale, together with the physical strain. Crucially, the strain enters the direction equation only through its tangential component $P_{\xiVec^\perp} S\xiVec$, which vanishes precisely when the direction is an eigenvector of the strain tensor. Since this includes the eigenvector carrying the maximal stretching, the result is in contrast to the classical geometric regularity criteria which are built on depleting the full vortex-stretching term and thus confined to configurations of weak stretching.

</details>


### [64] [Global existence and finite-time blow-up for a strongly damped wave--MGT system with fully subcritical logarithmic nonlinearity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05819)
*Tae Gab Ha*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a coupled strongly damped wave-Moore-Gibson-Thompson (MGT) system with a logarithmic source over the full Sobolev-subcritical range, establishing local existence, uniqueness, and a dynamical dichotomy (global exponential decay vs. finite-time blow-up) below the potential well depth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The key challenge is that in the full subcritical range 2<γ<2*, the logarithmic nonlinearity lacks the standard compactness and difference estimates available only in the lower subcritical regime. The authors aim to overcome these analytical difficulties to establish well-posedness and dynamical behavior.

Method: The paper introduces the augmented variable w=v+τv_t to derive an exact energy-dissipation identity for the strongly damped system, and exploits an associated coupled potential-well structure. Local existence is proven via a Faedo-Galerkin scheme with a closed nonlinear differential inequality and domain-splitting; strong damping supplies regularity for uniqueness. Below the well depth, solutions in the stable set decay exponentially while those in the unstable set blow up in finite time via a concavity method.

Result: Local existence, uniqueness (throughout the full subcritical range), and a continuation principle are established. Below the well depth d_α, a sharp dynamical dichotomy is obtained: global exponential decay for stable-set initial data and finite-time blow-up for unstable-set initial data, with blow-up proven without any additional sign condition on initial velocities thanks to a structural cancellation in the augmented formulation.

Conclusion: The augmented formulation with the exact energy identity enables the analysis to close in the full subcritical range, absorbing the unfavorable MGT residual in Levine's concavity functional algebraically, thereby yielding a complete dichotomy result for the system.

Abstract: We study a coupled strongly damped wave--Moore--Gibson--Thompson (MGT) system with logarithmic source $f(u)=|u|^{γ-2}u\ln|u|$ in the full Sobolev-subcritical range $2<γ<2^*:=2n/(n-2)$. The main difficulty is that, in this full range, the logarithmic nonlinearity does not admit the standard compactness and difference estimates available in the lower subcritical regime. Using the augmented variable $w=v+τv_t$, we derive the exact energy-dissipation identity for the strongly damped system and exploit the associated coupled potential-well structure.
  For the local theory, a Faedo--Galerkin scheme combined with a closed nonlinear differential inequality and a spatial domain-splitting argument yields local existence. The strong damping provides the additional regularity needed to prove uniqueness throughout the full subcritical range and to establish a continuation principle. Below the well depth $d_α$, we then obtain a sharp dynamical dichotomy: solutions with initial data in the stable set exist globally and decay exponentially, whereas solutions with initial data in the unstable set blow up in finite time.
  The blow-up argument reveals a structural cancellation specific to the augmented formulation: once the logarithmic contribution is reconstructed through the exact energy identity, the unfavorable MGT residual in Levine's concavity functional is absorbed algebraically. This allows the concavity method to close without imposing any additional sign condition on the initial velocities.

</details>


### [65] [Long-time asymptotics of nonlinear Fokker-Planck equations with inhomogeneous diffusion and natural boundary conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05873)
*Kouta Araki,Masashi Mizuno*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies long-time asymptotics for nonlinear Fokker-Planck equations with spatially inhomogeneous diffusion, using the law of energy dissipation. It derives a priori positive bounds for solutions, provides a sufficient condition for exponential decay of the dissipation function, and uses numerical computation to assess whether the condition is essential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the long-time behavior of solutions to nonlinear Fokker-Planck equations with spatial inhomogeneous diffusion, which is important for applications in physics and other fields where diffusion properties vary in space.

Method: The approach is based on the law of energy dissipation. First, a priori positive lower and upper bounds for solutions are derived. Then, a sufficient condition for exponential decay of the dissipation function is established analytically. Finally, numerical computation is used to test the necessity of this condition.

Result: The paper establishes a priori bounds on solutions, gives a sufficient condition guaranteeing exponential decay of the dissipation function, and numerically investigates whether this condition is also necessary.

Conclusion: The paper provides theoretical results on the long-time asymptotics of nonlinear Fokker-Planck equations with inhomogeneous diffusion, backed by numerical validation of the key condition.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the long-time asymptotics for nonlinear Fokker-Planck equations with spatial inhomogeneous diffusion, based on the law of energy dissipation. First, we derive a priori positive lower and upper bounds for solutions of the equations. Next, we give a sufficient condition for deriving the exponential decay of the dissipation function. Finally, we investigate whether the given sufficient condition is essential or not by numerical computation.

</details>


### [66] [Sharp dispersive estimates for wave equation on lattice graphs with high dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05930)
*Zhe You,Xiongfeng Zhan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper completes the dispersive estimates for the fundamental solution of the discrete wave equation on lattice graphs Z^d, resolving all remaining dimensions beyond those previously established (d=2,3 by Schultz; d=4,5 by Bi, Cheng and Hua). The estimates are shown to be sharp for every d≥5, and the proof also covers the upper bound for d=4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Schultz established dispersive estimates for the discrete wave equation on Z^d for d=2,3 as a discrete analogue of the classical Euclidean wave equation result. Extensions to d=4 and d=5 by Bi, Cheng and Hua left gaps, and the remaining dimensions had not been resolved. The authors aim to provide complete answers for all remaining dimensions.

Method: The paper provides a unified proof approach that resolves all remaining dimensions of the dispersive estimate problem. The proof technique is sufficiently general to work for d≥5 while also remaining valid for establishing the upper bound in the d=4 case.

Result: Complete dispersive estimates are established for all previously unresolved dimensions, with sharpness proven for every d≥5. The result confirms the estimates are optimal in higher dimensions.

Conclusion: This work completes the dispersive estimate program for the discrete wave equation on lattice graphs across all dimensions, demonstrating that the estimates are sharp for d≥5 and providing a proof that covers the d=4 upper bound as well.

Abstract: Schultz [Comm.~Pure~Appl.~Math., 1998] first established dispersive estimates for the fundamental solution of the discrete wave equation on lattice graphs $\Z^d$ for $d=2,3$, which can be seen as a discrete analogue of the classical dispersive estimate for the Euclidean wave equation. His result was extended by Bi, Cheng and Hua to $d=4$ and $5$. In this paper, we give complete answers to all the remaining dimensions, including $d=5$. Our estimate is sharp for every $d\geq 5$. Moreover, the proof remains valid for the upper bound of the case for $d=4$.

</details>


### [67] [Nonlinear profiles on solutions to the surface quasi-geostrophic equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05943)
*Masakazu Yamamoto*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a unique nonlinear asymptotic profile for solutions to the surface quasi-geostrophic (SQG) equation, where solution components decay sequentially from slow to fast according to parabolic scaling, with nonlinear effects manifesting in sub-dominant components and revealing a distinctive rotational flow structure due to the Coriolis force.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The SQG equation models potential temperature in high-latitude atmospheric and oceanic dynamics, but its large-time asymptotic structure—particularly the role of nonlinear effects arising from the Coriolis force—remains unclear. Understanding this unique nonlinear profile is essential for predicting long-term behavior of these geophysical flows.

Method: The authors derive nonlinear asymptotic expansions for SQG solutions, arranging solution components sequentially based on parabolic decay rates. They prove uniqueness of this expansion from the large-time behavior perspective, analyzing the interplay between linear (dominant) and nonlinear (sub-dominant) contributions using the equation's subcritical structure and spatial correlation properties.

Result: The paper proves that while main components exhibit linear features in the subcritical regime, nonlinear profiles emerge in smaller-effect components with characteristics distinctively different from linear profiles in terms of spacetime variable correlations. A unique, subtle rotational flow generated by the Coriolis force is identified as a key theorem.

Conclusion: Nonlinear effects in the SQG equation are weaker than in other equations with comparable scaling due to the spatial structure, yet they produce a unique rotational flow pattern—a minute but distinctive nonlinear signature attributable to the Coriolis force.

Abstract: The quasi-geostrophic equation is well known as a model for predicting potential temperature around low-pressure systems in high-latitude regions. When diffusion effects are added to this equation, it serves as a model for potential temperature at the sea surface. In either case, the nonlinear term represents the effect of the Coriolis force. This paper yields nonlinear profile in solutions to the surface quasi-geostrophic equation. This profile is determined uniquely from the perspective of large-time behavior of solutions. Components of solutions are arranged sequentially from slow to fast decay based on the parabolic scale. Then such an expansion is determined uniquely. Since the equation is subcritical in the context of large-time effects, the main components exhibit linear features. Nonlinear characteristics appear in components that have a smaller effect on solutions. From the perspective of the correlation between spacetime variables, nonlinear profiles exhibit characteristics that are distinctly different from those of linear profiles. Moreover, the nonlinear effects of this equation are expected to be weaker than those of other equations with the same scale due to their spatial structure. The main theorem publishes minute, but unique rotational flow arising from the Coriolis force.

</details>


### [68] [Global Dynamic Patterns of Entropy Solutions for One-dimensional Pressureless Euler System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05948)
*Gaowei Cao,Kai Hu,Feimin Huang,Guirong Tang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes fine properties of entropy solutions for the one-dimensional pressureless Euler system with initial density as a locally finite Radon measure and bounded initial velocity. Using the solution formula by Huang and Wang, the authors establish global dynamic patterns covering four aspects: characteristics and initial waves, fine local structures, divides and global structures, and invariants with asymptotic behaviors including decay rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the detailed fine properties and global dynamic behavior of entropy solutions for the pressureless Euler system under very general initial data (measurable density and bounded velocity), which is a fundamental problem in gas dynamics and sticky particle modeling.

Method: The authors employ the explicit solution formula introduced by Huang and Wang (Comm. Math. Phys. 2001) for the Cauchy problem, and use it to systematically analyze entropy solutions across four structural and dynamical aspects.

Result: The paper obtains new results in four categories: (i) characterization of characteristics and initial waves; (ii) fine local structures of entropy solutions; (iii) divides and global structures; and (iv) invariants and asymptotic profiles with corresponding decay rates. Together these establish complete global dynamic patterns for the entropy solutions.

Conclusion: The paper provides a comprehensive global description of entropy solution dynamics for the 1-D pressureless Euler system with general measure-valued initial density and bounded velocity, thereby completing the fine-structure analysis of these solutions.

Abstract: In this paper, we are concerned with the fine properties of entropy solutions of the Cauchy problem for the one-dimensional pressureless Euler system, wherein the initial density $ρ_0$ is a locally finite Radon measure and the initial velocity $u_0\in L^\infty_{ρ_0}$. We employ the solution formula introduced by [F.M. Huang and Z. Wang, Comm. Math. Phys. 222(1) (2001), 117--146.] for this Cauchy problem to analyze the entropy solutions and obtain various new fine properties of entropy solutions; these can be summarized in four aspects: (i) Characteristics and initial waves for the Cauchy problem; (ii) Fine local structures of entropy solutions; (iii) Divides and global structures of entropy solutions; (iv) Invariants and asymptotic behaviors of entropy solutions including the asymptotic profile and the corresponding decay rates. Through these results (i)-(iv), we establish the global dynamic patterns of entropy solutions of the Cauchy problem for the $1$-D pressureless Euler system with general initial data $ρ_0$ being locally finite Radon measures and $u_0\in L^\infty_{ρ_0}$.

</details>


### [69] [Remarks on a Liouville-type theorem by Chae and Wolf for stationary Navier-Stokes equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06013)
*Raúl Fierro,Gaston Vergara-Hermosilla*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper broadens a Liouville-type theorem for stationary Navier-Stokes equations, showing that the Chae-Wolf logarithmic improvement of the classical L^{9/2} condition fits into a much wider framework of weighted integrability conditions involving any positive, nondecreasing, bounded weight satisfying a mild growth condition near the origin.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and generalize the structural mechanism underlying Chae and Wolf's logarithmic improvement of the classical triviality condition for stationary Navier-Stokes solutions, and to determine whether the logarithmic weight is essential or merely one instance of a broader principle.

Method: The authors consider solutions u in H^1(R^3) of the stationary Navier-Stokes equations and prove triviality under the weighted integral condition with any positive, nondecreasing, bounded weight omega satisfying a mild growth condition near the origin. This general weight condition encompasses logarithmic weights, iterated-logarithmic hierarchies, and non-logarithmic examples such as dyadic weights.

Result: The triviality of stationary Navier-Stokes solutions holds under the generalized weighted framework for a large class of admissible weights, demonstrating that the Chae-Wolf logarithmic improvement is not specifically tied to logarithm structure but reflects a more general phenomenon.

Conclusion: The mechanism behind the Chae-Wolf improvement is structural rather than tied to a specific logarithmic weight or single logarithmic scale, and a broader hierarchy of weighted integrability conditions suffices to guarantee triviality of solutions.

Abstract: In this note, we revisit a Liouville-type theorem of Chae and Wolf for stationary Navier-Stokes equations in $\mathbb{R}^3$ [J. Differential Equations 261 (2016) 5541-5560]. We show that their logarithmic improvement of the classical $L^{9/2}$ condition is part of a substantially broader weighted framework. More precisely, we prove that a solution $u\in\dot H^1(\mathbb{R}^3)$ is necessarily trivial whenever $$ \int_{\mathbb{R}^3}|u(x)|^{9/2}\,ω(|u(x)|)\,dx< +\infty, $$ for every positive, nondecreasing and bounded weight $ω$ satisfying a mild growth condition near the origin. This structural condition encompasses the logarithmic weight due to Chae and Wolf, as well as a hierarchy of iterated-logarithmic weights and (genuinely) non-logarithmic examples, including a dyadic weight. Our result identifies a broader class of weighted integrability conditions under which the triviality of stationary Navier-Stokes solutions follows, and shows that the mechanism underlying the Chae-Wolf improvement is not intrinsically tied to a specific logarithmic weight or to a single logarithmic scale.

</details>


### [70] [A globally defined polyconvex isotropic energy satisfying the true-stress-true-strain monotonicity condition (TSTS-M++)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06026)
*Dongxin Bai,Yunhao Wu,Yong Li,Kai Zhang,Patrizio Neff*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper provides explicit evidence that polyconvexity and TSTS-M++ (true-stress-true-strain monotonicity) can coexist for compressible isotropic hyperelastic energies defined on GL+(3). They construct an exponential-type stored-energy function W_k(F) parameterized by k, prove it is polyconvex and strictly rank-one convex for all μ>0, k>0, and show TSTS-M++ holds globally iff k ≥ 1/(8√3). Stress bijectivity holds for a strictly smaller threshold kB ≈ 0.00827, and in the TSTS-M++ regime unique radial equilibria exist for finite plane-strain annuli with monotone inner pressure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Wollner, Holzapfel, and Neff left open the question of whether a compressible isotropic energy on GL+(3) could simultaneously satisfy polyconvexity (needed for variational existence theory) and TSTS-M++ (positive incremental Cauchy stress response), since these two constitutive restrictions are independent. The paper aims to settle this open question definitively.

Method: The authors construct an explicit family of stored-energy functions W_k(F) = (μ/2k)[exp(k(||F||² + 3J⁻¹ + J - 7)) - 1] on GL+(3). They analyze the polyconvexity and rank-one convexity analytically, then derive sharp thresholds for TSTS-M++ (k ≥ 1/(8√3)) and for global stress bijectivity (kB ≈ 0.00827233304) via careful analysis of the Cauchy-stress map V ↦ σ(V). They also apply the results to a finite plane-strain annulus boundary value problem.

Result: The constructed energy is polyconvex and strictly rank-one convex for all k>0. TSTS-M++ holds globally iff k ≥ 1/(8√3). Stress bijectivity has the strictly smaller threshold kB ≈ 0.00827233304, where at kB the stress map is a global homeomorphism with nondifferentiable inverse, and above kB it is a C∞ diffeomorphism. In the TSTS-M++ regime, every prescribed inner radius of a finite plane-strain annulus (traction-free outer wall) has a unique radial equilibrium with inner pressure increasing smoothly and strictly from zero to infinity.

Conclusion: The paper affirmatively resolves the open question by Wollner, Holzapfel, and Neff: polyconvexity and TSTS-M++ are compatible for compressible isotropic energies on GL+(3). Moreover, it reveals a rich landscape of regimes—global stress bijectivity can hold even when TSTS-M++ fails at finite strain—and demonstrates the practical consequences for uniqueness of radial equilibrium solutions in finite plane-strain annulus problems.

Abstract: Polyconvexity is a standard ingredient in the variational existence theory of finite elasticity, whereas true-stress-true-strain monotonicity (TSTS-M++) requires a positive incremental Cauchy-stress response. These two constitutive restrictions are independent, and Wollner, Holzapfel and Neff left open whether a compressible isotropic energy defined on the whole of $\mathrm{GL}^{+}(3)$ can satisfy both. We give an explicit affirmative answer. For every $μ>0$ and $k>0$, the stored-energy function $W_k(F)=\fracμ{2k}\bigl[\exp\bigl(k(\lVert F\rVert^2+3J^{-1}+J-7)\bigr)-1\bigr]$, $J=\det F$, is polyconvex and strictly rank-one convex. Its Cauchy-stress response satisfies TSTS-M++ globally if and only if $k\ge 1/(8\sqrt{3})$. In this regime every symmetric Cauchy stress corresponds to a unique positive-definite stretch, while the reference stretch is stress free with positive infinitesimal shear and bulk moduli. Stress bijectivity has the strictly smaller sharp threshold $k_{\mathrm{B}}\approx 0.00827233304$: at equality the stress map is a global homeomorphism with a nondifferentiable inverse, and above it the map is a global $C^{\infty}$ diffeomorphism. Thus, for $k_{\mathrm{B}}\le k<1/(8\sqrt{3})$, the map $V\mapstoσ(V)$ remains globally bijective while TSTS-M++ fails at finite strain. In the TSTS-M++ regime, every prescribed inner radius of a finite plane-strain annulus with a traction-free outer wall has a unique radial equilibrium, and its inner pressure increases smoothly and strictly from zero to infinity.

</details>


### [71] [Stability of Magnetizable Piezoelectric Beam Systems with Two Fractional Infinite Memory Terms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06049)
*Jun Zhou,Linfeng Duan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a magnetizable piezoelectric beam system with two fractional infinite memory terms (fractional orders θ1, θ2 ∈ [0,1]). Using the Dafermos history variable, the system is reformulated as an abstract evolution equation. Well-posedness is established via semigroup theory, and asymptotic behavior is characterized: exponential stability when θ1 = θ2 = 1, and polynomial stability otherwise with decay rate t^{-1/(2-2θ0)} where θ0 = min{θ1, θ2}, with proven optimality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the asymptotic stability properties of magnetizable piezoelectric beam systems that incorporate fractional memory effects, since the interplay of two fractional infinite memory terms affects the decay behavior in ways not yet characterized.

Method: The paper employs the Dafermos history variable to construct an augmented state space, reformulating the system as an abstract evolution equation. Semigroup theory is used to prove well-posedness, and frequency-domain analysis is applied to characterize the asymptotic decay rates of solutions.

Result: The system is exponentially stable when both fractional orders equal 1 (θ1 = θ2 = 1). In all other cases, the system is polynomially stable with explicit decay rate t^{-1/(2-2θ0)}, where θ0 = min{θ1, θ2}. The optimality of the decay rate is verified.

Conclusion: The presence of fractional memory terms (with orders less than 1) degrades the stability from exponential to polynomial, and the decay rate depends on the smaller of the two fractional orders. The obtained polynomial decay rate is optimal.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate a class of magnetizable piezoelectric beam systems with two fractional infinite memory terms, whose fractional orders are denoted by \(θ_1, θ_2 \in [0,1]\). By introducing the Dafermos history variable, we construct an augmented state space and reformulate the system as an abstract evolution equation. The well-posedness of the system is established via semigroup theory. Through a frequency-domain analysis, we characterize the asymptotic behavior of the solutions. We show that the system is exponentially stable if \(θ_1 = θ_2 = 1\), and is polynomially stable in all other cases, with the explicit decay rate \(t^{-\frac{1}{2-2θ_0}}\), where \(θ_0 = \min\{θ_1, θ_2\}\). Furthermore, the optimality of the obtained decay rate is verified.

</details>


### [72] [Qualitative analysis of positive radial singular solutions on hyperbolic space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06113)
*Xia Huang,Yahui Jiang,Chunyi Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies positive radial solutions with nonremovable singularities for a semilinear elliptic equation on hyperbolic space H^N, analyzing how locally Euclidean singular behavior interacts with hyperbolic dynamics at infinity across the Serrin and Sobolev critical exponents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the local singular behavior near a pole interacts with the genuinely hyperbolic dynamics at infinity, and how this interaction transitions across the Serrin and Sobolev critical exponents in the context of semilinear elliptic equations on hyperbolic space.

Method: Construction of families of positive radial singular solutions for various parameter regimes (subcritical, critical, and supercritical in the Sobolev sense), using fast exponential modes at infinity, explicit solutions at the conformally invariant pair, and phase-plane analysis via associated autonomous equations.

Result: For N/(N-2) ≤ p < (N+2)/(N-2): constructs singular solutions with logarithmically corrected fundamental-solution profile at the Serrin critical exponent and standard power-law behavior otherwise. At the conformally invariant pair: provides explicit singular solutions, nonconstant Fowler-type solutions, and a full classification via periodic orbits. For p > (N+2)/(N-2) with λ ≤ N(N-2)/4: proves existence and uniqueness of the global singular solution, derives two-term local asymptotics near the pole, and establishes a sharp trichotomy for asymptotic behavior at infinity.

Conclusion: The paper provides a comprehensive description of how different critical exponents demarcate distinct regimes of interaction between local Euclidean singularity structure and global hyperbolic dynamics, with complete classification results in the conformally invariant case.

Abstract: We investigate positive radial solutions with an isolated nonremovable singularity for the semilinear elliptic equation \begin{align*}
  Δ_{\mathbb{H}^N} u+λu+u^p=0 \qquad\text{in }\mathbb{H}^N\setminus\{Q\},
  \end{align*} where $N\geq 3$, $p>1$, $λ\le \frac{(N-1)^2}{4}$, and $Q\in\mathbb{H}^N$ is a prescribed pole. Our purpose is to describe how the locally Euclidean singular behavior near ${\{Q}\}$ interacts with the genuinely hyperbolic dynamics at infinity, and how this interaction changes across the Serrin and Sobolev critical exponents.
  For $\frac{N}{N-2}\le p<\frac{N+2}{N-2}$, we construct a family of positive radial singular solutions selecting the fast exponential mode at infinity. At the pole, these solutions exhibit the logarithmically corrected fundamental-solution profile when $p=\frac{N}{N-2}$, and the standard power-law profile when $\frac{N}{N-2}<p<\frac{N+2}{N-2}.$
  At the conformally invariant pair $(p,λ)=(\frac{N+2}{N-2},\frac{N(N-2)}{4})$, we obtain an explicit singular solution and nonconstant Fowler-type solutions, and we classify all positive radial solutions with a nonremovable singularity via the positive periodic orbits of an associated autonomous equation. In the supercritical regime $p>\frac{N+2}{N-2}$ with $λ\le\frac{ N(N-2)}{4}$, we prove existence and uniqueness of the global positive radial singular solution, derive its two-term local asymptotic expansion near the pole, and establish a sharp trichotomy for its behavior at infinity.

</details>


### [73] [Modified scattering for semiclassical Bose-Fermi mixtures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06143)
*Kieran Cavanagh*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves modified scattering for the Vlasov-Hartree system (bosons quantum + fermions classical, Coulomb interactions) for small initial data, showing both subsystems converge to free solutions with logarithmic corrections due to the long-range nature of Coulomb forces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Vlasov-Hartree system models a quantum-classical mixture of bosons and fermions with Coulomb interactions. Understanding its asymptotic dynamics is important, but the long-range Coulomb force creates logarithmic corrections that complicate scattering analysis. A key issue is that prior approaches require smoothness assumptions on the initial fermion density that seem unnecessary.

Method: The authors employ a mixed Lagrangian and Fourier-based approach. The Lagrangian analysis is applied on the Vlasov (fermion) side, while Fourier methods are used for the Schrödinger (boson) side. This hybrid approach provides explicit descriptions of the asymptotic dynamics of solutions, associated fields, and Lagrangian trajectories.

Result: Modified scattering is established for small initial data. The bosonic and fermionic subsystems converge to solutions of free Schrödinger and Vlasov equations respectively, with coupled logarithmic corrections. No derivatives are required on the initial fermion density, the asymptotic fermionic profile maintains the same regularity as the initial data, and convergence occurs in nearly the same space as the initial data.

Conclusion: The purely Lagrangian treatment of the Vlasov side yields sharp results on the fermionic component—removing unnecessary derivative assumptions, preserving regularity of the asymptotic profile, and achieving convergence in almost the same function space as the initial data—demonstrating a distinct advantage over Fourier-based approaches for this mixed system.

Abstract: The Vlasov-Hartree system models a mixture of bosons and fermions interacting via Coulomb forces in which the bosons are described quantum-mechanically, while the fermions are described classically. For small initial data, we prove that the system exhibits modified scattering, i.e. the bosonic and fermionic subsystems each converge to solutions of the free Schrödinger and Vlasov equations respectively, but with coupled logarithmic corrections owing to the long-range nature of Coulomb interactions. We use a mixed Lagrangian and Fourier-based approach to derive explicit descriptions of the asymptotic dynamics of the solution and associated fields and Lagrangian trajectories. Notably, due to our purely Lagrangian analysis on the Vlasov side, we require no derivatives on the initial fermion density, the asymptotic fermionic profile is as regular as the initial data, and convergence occurs in almost the same space as the initial data.

</details>


### [74] [Well--posedness for the continuum Calogero--Moser equations by parabolic regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06164)
*Yingmo Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global well-posedness for both focusing and defocusing continuum Calogero-Moser equations in H^s_+(R) for all integers s≥3, with the focusing case requiring a mass condition ||q_0||²_L² < 2π. The proof uses parabolic regularization, uniform a-priori estimates, and a Bona-Smith approximation argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish global well-posedness for the continuum Calogero-Moser equations, which are important integrable systems, in both focusing and defocusing cases. The focusing case requires a sharp mass threshold condition, making the result particularly significant.

Method: The proof employs a three-step strategy: (1) parabolic regularization of the equation, (2) derivation of uniform a-priori estimates for the regularized solutions, and (3) a Bona-Smith type approximation argument to pass to the limit and establish well-posedness.

Result: Global well-posedness is proven in H^s_+(R) for every integer s≥3 for both focusing and defocusing continuum Calogero-Moser equations. In the focusing case, well-posedness holds under the mass condition ||q_0||²_L² < 2π.
