Conclusion: The continuum Calogero-Moser equations are globally well-posed in high-regularity Sobolev spaces of positive-index type, with a mass threshold (2π) determining global existence in the focusing regime—analogous to critical mass thresholds found in other focusing nonlinear dispersive equations.

Abstract: We prove that the focusing and defocusing continuum Calogero--Moser equations are globally well-posed in $H^s_+(\mathbb R)$ for every integer $s\geq 3$. In the focusing case, this requires the initial data to satisfy the mass condition $$\|q_0\|_{L^2}^2<2π.$$ Our proof is based on a parabolic regularization of the equation, uniform \textit{a--priori} estimates for the regularized solutions, and a Bona--Smith type approximation argument.

</details>


### [75] [Logarithmic capacity and torsion in planar shape optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06205)
*David Ziener*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a family of functionals that multiply logarithmic capacity by torsional rigidity, proving discs are the unique maximizers among planar convex sets for certain parameter values, and establishing regularity of maximizers in the non-critical case via a new shape-differentiability theorem for logarithmic capacity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and identify optimal shapes (maximizers) for a combined functional involving two fundamental geometric/physical quantities — logarithmic capacity and torsional rigidity — within the class of planar convex sets.

Method: The approach uses geometric inequalities to identify discs as the unique maximizers for specific parameter ranges, and proves a shape-differentiability theorem for logarithmic capacity over planar convex bodies to establish regularity of maximizers in the non-critical case.

Result: Discs are proven to be the only maximizers among planar convex sets for certain parameter values, and regularity of maximizers is established in the non-critical case using the newly proven shape-differentiability result for logarithmic capacity.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete characterization of maximizers for the studied functional in specific parameter regimes, along with regularity results, contributing new analytic tools (shape differentiability of logarithmic capacity on convex planar domains) to the field.

Abstract: We study a family of functionals involving a product of logarithmic capacity and torsional rigidity. Using several geometric inequalities, we identify the discs as the only maximizers among planar convex sets for certain values of the parameters. Finally, we prove regularity of maximizers in the non-critical case. This is done by establishing a shape-differentiability theorem for the logarithmic capacity in the class of planar convex bodies.

</details>


### [76] [Concavity Properties of Robin Solutions on $C^{3,1}$ Uniformly Convex Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06223)
*Dong Ye,Dekai Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves strict log-concavity of the first Robin eigenfunction and strict 1/2-concavity of the Robin torsion function on bounded uniformly convex C^{3,1} domains for large Robin parameters, confirming Conjecture 1.1 by Andrews, Clutterbuck and Hauer and settling an open problem by Crasta and Fragalà (for C^{3,1} domains).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to settle long-standing open questions about concavity properties of Robin eigenfunctions and torsion functions. Prior results (e.g., the Andrews-Clutterbuck-Hauer conjecture and the Crasta-Fragalà problem) required either higher-order boundary regularity or remained open; this work resolves these gaps by relaxing regularity assumptions to C^{3,1} and covering all dimensions.

Method: The proof uses maximum principle arguments to derive suitable uniform C^2-decay estimates near the boundary, which removes the previously required higher-order boundary regularity assumptions. These estimates serve as the key technical tool to establish the strict concavity properties.

Result: On bounded uniformly convex C^{3,1} domains in any dimension: (1) the first Robin eigenfunction is strictly log-concave, and (2) the Robin torsion function is strictly 1/2-concave, both for all sufficiently large Robin parameters. This confirms Conjecture 1.1 of Andrews-Clutterbuck-Hauer and answers the open problem posed by Crasta-Fragalà under C^{3,1} regularity.

Conclusion: The paper resolves an open conjecture and problem by proving strict concavity results with only C^{3,1} regularity via uniform C^2-decay estimates obtained from maximum principle arguments, thereby relaxing the previously imposed higher-order boundary regularity condition.

Abstract: We prove that, on a bounded uniformly convex domain of class $C^{3,1}$, the first Robin eigenfunction is strictly log-concave and the Robin torsion function is strictly $1/2$-concave for all sufficiently large Robin parameters. This means that Conjecture 1.1 of Andrews, Clutterbuck and Hauer holds for uniformly convex $C^{3,1}$ domains in any dimension, and settles an open problem posed by Crasta and Fragalà when the domain has $C^{3,1}$ regularity. Our proof derives suitable uniform $C^2$-decay estimates by maximum principle arguments, thereby removing the imposed higher-order boundary regularity assumption required previously.

</details>


### [77] [Determination of Wave Speed from interior sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06225)
*Joonas Ilmavirta,Teemu Saksala,Ashwin Tarikere,Gunther Uhlmann*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper addresses an inverse problem for the wave equation with variable wave speed in Euclidean space, where point sources are modeled by Dirac delta initial displacement data. The goal is to uniquely reconstruct the unknown wave speed from an unlabeled collection of waves measured at receivers, using propagation of singularities and finite speed of propagation to reduce measurements to geometric travel-time data. Three geometric scenarios are presented where unique determination of the wave speed is achieved.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper tackles the challenging inverse problem of reconstructing a variable wave speed from unlabeled (unordered/anonymous) waveform data collected at receivers from multiple point sources. Unlike classical inverse problems where source-receiver pairing is known, here the correspondence is unknown, making the problem more difficult and practically relevant (e.g., in seismic or medical imaging where triggers may not be identifiable).

Method: The method leverages two key tools from wave propagation theory: (1) propagation of singularities to trace wavefront arrivals to their generating point sources, and (2) sharp finite speed of propagation to restrict information flow. These tools are used to convert the raw wave measurements into geometric travel-time data. The reduced data structure depends on how the source set, unknown set, and receiver set are spatially related. Three specific geometric configurations are analyzed where this reduction yields unique recovery of the wave speed.

Result: The paper demonstrates that the unlabeled wave data can be successfully reduced to travel-time information under appropriate geometric configurations of the source, unknown, and receiver sets. For three identified scenarios, this procedure leads to the unique determination of the variable wave speed in the unknown region.

Conclusion: The authors establish uniqueness results for an inverse wave-speed reconstruction problem with unlabeled point-source data, providing a theoretical foundation based on singularity propagation and finite-speed arguments across three specific geometric configurations. This opens avenues for practical algorithms and broader geometric cases.

Abstract: We consider the wave equation with variable wave speed in Euclidean space, with point sources modeled by Dirac delta initial displacement data. There are three special subsets of the whole space: (1) the source set where the delta initial conditions are supported, (2) the unknown set where the wave speed is not known a priori, and (3) the receiver set where the waves are measured. The inverse problem is to reconstruct the wave speed uniquely in the unknown set from an unlabeled collection of waves generated by point sources and measured in the receiver set. We use propagation of singularities and sharp finite speed of propagation to reduce this data to geometric travel-time data, whose form depends on how the three sets lie in relation to each other. We give three scenarios where this procedure leads to unique determination of the wave speed.

</details>


### [78] [Notes on linear elliptic equations with $L^2$-gradient perturbations and singular zero-order coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06227)
*Haesung Lee*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes existence, uniqueness, and quantitative estimates for weak solutions to linear elliptic Dirichlet problems with rough coefficients: drift contains ∇φ ∈ L² only and zero-order coefficient c ∈ L¹ with c ≥ 0. Despite these low-regularity assumptions, well-posedness and quantitative energy/L² estimates hold. An interpolation result characterizes a trade-off between integrability of source f and coefficient c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study elliptic Dirichlet problems where the drift term contains a low-regularity gradient ∇φ (only in L²) and the zero-order coefficient is merely integrable (L¹), which goes beyond classical regularity assumptions. Understanding well-posedness under such rough data is important for applications where coefficients lack smoothness.

Method: The paper considers the linear elliptic equation −div(γ∇u) + ⟨∇φ+H, ∇u⟩ + (c+α)u = f with u=0 on the boundary, under assumptions φ ∈ H^{1,2}∩L^∞, H ∈ L^p (p > d), c ∈ L¹ with c ≥ 0. It proves well-posedness, quantitative energy and L² estimates under these rough assumptions, and uses a previously established interpolation result to characterize a trade-off between integrability of f and c.

Result: Well-posedness and quantitative energy/L² estimates hold even under the rough assumptions (∇φ ∈ L², c ∈ L¹). Additionally, using an interpolation result, it characterizes a trade-off between integrability of the source term f and the zero-order coefficient c, showing well-posedness with quantitative estimates under the interpolated assumptions.

Conclusion: The paper demonstrates that existence, uniqueness, and quantitative estimates for weak solutions to linear elliptic Dirichlet problems remain valid under surprisingly weak regularity assumptions on the coefficients, and provides an interpolated framework balancing source and coefficient integrability.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the existence, uniqueness, and quantitative estimates for weak solutions to linear elliptic Dirichlet problems of the form \[ -\operatorname{div}(γ\nabla u)+\langle \nablaφ+\mathbf{H},\nabla u\rangle+(c+α)u=f \quad\text{ in }U, \quad\; u=0 \quad\text{on }\partial U, \] where $U\subset \mathbb{R}^d$ is bounded, $γ\in[1,\infty)$ is a constant, $φ\in H^{1,2}(U)\cap L^\infty(U)$, $\mathbf{H}\in L^p(U,\mathbb{R}^d)$ for some $p \in (d, \infty)$, and $c\in L^1(U)$ with $c\ge0$. A key feature of this setting is that the drift contains the low-regularity term $\nablaφ$, which is only assumed to belong to $L^2(U,\mathbb{R}^d)$, while the zero-order coefficient is merely integrable. We prove that, even under these rough assumptions, well-posedness and quantitative energy and $L^2$ estimates remain valid. In addition, by using a previously established interpolation result, we characterize a trade-off between the integrability of the source term $f$ and that of the zero-order coefficient $c$, and show that well-posedness together with the corresponding quantitative estimates hold under these interpolated assumptions.

</details>


### [79] [A model of a multiphase medium based on the closure of moment chains for the Vlasov-Poisson equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06254)
*Olga S. Rozanova*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proposes a closure method for moment chains in the Vlasov-Poisson kinetic system (Landau fluid model), producing a hyperbolic system at each step. The system is interpreted as a multicomponent medium with components having individual densities, velocities, and pressures, coupled via an electric field. The closure principle assumes the final fluid component is pressureless.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the challenge of closing moment chains in kinetic plasma models like the Vlasov-Poisson system, which is necessary to obtain a tractable finite set of fluid-like equations that retain kinetic effects.

Method: The closure is achieved by assuming the last component in the moment hierarchy is pressureless (zero pressure), which yields a hyperbolic system of equations. The resulting system is interpreted as a multicomponent medium where each component possesses its own density, velocity, and pressure, with coupling mediated by an electric field.

Result: The approach produces a hyperbolic system of equations at each level of the moment hierarchy, providing a consistent and systematic closure scheme for the Landau fluid model.

Conclusion: A new closure principle for moment chains in the Vlasov-Poisson system is proposed, interpreting the hierarchy as coupled fluid components with the final component being pressureless, leading to hyperbolic governing equations.

Abstract: We propose a method for closing moment chains for the Vlasov-Poisson kinetic system (the Landau fluid model), which yields a hyperbolic system of equations at each subsequent step. This system is interpreted as a multicomponent medium, where each component has its own density, velocity, and pressure. The coupling between the components is mediated by an electric field. The principle of closing moment chains is that the final component of the fluid is assumed to be pressureless.

</details>


### [80] [Sharp spectral-scale stability for parabolic equations with measure-valued delay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06262)
*Lennon J. Shikhman*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how parabolic solution operators depend on a finite signed measure describing delay laws, establishing sharp Lipschitz stability results for semigroup-integrated memory perturbations, with the worst-case modulus on rough history balls scaling as d√log(e/d) for continuous L²-valued histories, and identifying exact weighted thresholds via reciprocal-square summability criteria over spectral bands.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how sensitively parabolic solution operators depend on the delay law encoded as a finite signed measure, particularly distinguishing sensitivity to delay-law perturbations from spatial approximation error, and to characterize sharp stability thresholds across different levels of spatial regularity of histories.

Method: The paper uses a weighted dyadic spectral sum for a positive self-adjoint generator with compact inverse to characterize the norm of semigroup-integrated memory perturbations. An exact realization identity transfers lower estimates between different delay configurations using common smooth finite-spectral histories. Logarithmic spatial regularity conditions are analyzed across different orders (γ>1/2, γ=1/2) with a reciprocal-square summability criterion over occupied spectral bands determining the exact weighted threshold.

Result: The sharp worst-case modulus on a bounded ball of continuous L²-valued histories is d√log(e/d) for the Dirichlet Laplacian on a bounded open set, where d is the bounded-Lipschitz distance between delay measures. Logarithmic spatial regularity of order γ>1/2 restores resolution-uniform Lipschitz stability, while γ=1/2 retains a square-root double-logarithmic loss. Consequences include sharp finite-resolution Lipschitz constants, worst-case errors for midpoint quadrature, and semilinear upper estimates.

Conclusion: The results establish sharp stability thresholds for delay-measure dependence in parabolic equations, distinguishing delay-law sensitivity from spatial approximation error while not precluding uniform approximation of positive-time states on rough history balls across a range of spectral and regularity regimes.

Abstract: We study the dependence of parabolic solution operators on a finite signed measure describing the delay law. For a positive self-adjoint generator with compact inverse, a weighted dyadic spectral sum characterizes the norm of the semigroup-integrated memory perturbation. An exact realization identity transfers the matching lower estimate to two positive point delays in a fixed linear equation, using a common smooth, finite-spectral history. For the Dirichlet Laplacian on a nonempty bounded open set, the sharp worst-case modulus on a bounded ball of continuous $L^2$-valued histories is $d\sqrt{\log(e/d)}$, where $d$ is the bounded-Lipschitz distance between the delay measures. This is a rough-history endpoint result: logarithmic spatial regularity of order $γ>1/2$ restores resolution-uniform Lipschitz stability in the linear model. The critical order $γ=1/2$ retains a square-root double-logarithmic loss. More generally, a reciprocal-square summability criterion over occupied spectral bands gives the exact weighted threshold, including sparse spectra. Consequences include sharp finite-resolution Lipschitz constants, worst-case errors for prescribed midpoint quadrature of the memory measure, and semilinear upper estimates under Hilbert-space-valued local Lipschitz assumptions. The results distinguish sensitivity to the delay law from spatial approximation error and do not preclude uniform approximation of positive-time states on rough history balls.

</details>


### [81] [Carleman Estimates for Wave Equations on the Half-Line: AI-Assisted Weights and Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06283)
*Chenyang An,Yu Wang,Qihao Ye,Zhongqiang Zhang,Qiao Zhuang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The authors develop an AI-assisted workflow for searching and certifying Carleman weights for wave equations on semi-infinite domains. They derive analytical screening conditions, use AI to propose symbolic candidate weights, and discover a logarithmic weight structure that is then rigorously certified by human verification. This leads to a global Carleman estimate on the half-line, a weighted conditional lateral Cauchy stability result, and applications to a finite-depth reconstruction problem for semilinear wave equations with numerical validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Constructing Carleman weights for wave equations on semi-infinite domains is non-trivial and typically requires substantial analytical insight. The authors aim to accelerate and systematize this process by combining AI-based symbolic search with rigorous mathematical verification, making the weight construction more efficient while preserving mathematical certifiability.

Method: The workflow starts from a weighted cross-term identity underlying the Carleman estimate, from which explicit analytical screening conditions are derived. An AI system proposes symbolic candidate weight functions satisfying these conditions, leading to discovery of a logarithmic weight structure. Human mathematicians then verify the candidate, determine the admissible parameter range, and rigorously certify the weight by checking all required quantitative conditions. The certified weight is subsequently used to prove a global Carleman estimate, a conditional lateral Cauchy stability result, and a finite-depth reconstruction result for semilinear wave equations on the half-line.

Result: The search identifies a logarithmic weight structure with concrete admissible parameter values. Using this weight, the authors establish a global Carleman estimate for the wave operator on the half-line and a weighted conditional lateral Cauchy stability result. The weight is applied to a finite-depth one-sided reconstruction problem where the initial-time flux yields an endpoint graph stabilizer and a contractive frozen nonlinear reconstruction map. Numerical experiments confirm contraction behavior and show that the Carleman weighting improves robustness to noisy data, with the endpoint graph term adding further stabilization at high noise levels.

Conclusion: The AI-assisted search-and-certification workflow successfully identifies a valid logarithmic Carleman weight that is rigorously certified and analytically useful. The resulting theorems and numerical evidence demonstrate the practical value of the discovered weight for stability and reconstruction problems for wave equations on semi-infinite domains, showcasing a productive human-AI collaboration in mathematical analysis.

Abstract: We develop an AI-assisted search-and-certification workflow for constructing Carleman weights for wave equations on semi-infinite domains. Starting from a weighted cross-term identity used to derive the Carleman estimate, we derive explicit analytical screening conditions and use an AI system to propose symbolic candidate weights subject to those conditions. The search identifies a logarithmic weight structure with concrete parameter values; the subsequent human verification by the authors then retains this structure, derives the admissible parameter range, and rigorously certifies the selected weight by verifying the required quantitative conditions. Based on the identified and certified Carleman weight, the subsequent proofs, analysis, and applications are carried out entirely by the authors: we establish a global Carleman estimate for the wave operator on the half-line and derive a weighted conditional lateral Cauchy stability result. The proposed weight is further used in a finite-depth one-sided reconstruction problem for semilinear wave equations on the half-line, where the initial-time flux induces an endpoint graph stabilizer and a contractive frozen nonlinear reconstruction map. Numerical experiments support the predicted contraction behavior and show improved robustness to noisy data from the Carleman weighting, with the induced endpoint graph term providing further stabilization at higher noise levels.

</details>


### [82] [The entropy production is not always monotone for the Kac equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06290)
*Weinan Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper disproves McKean's 1966 conjecture that entropy production in Kac's caricature of a Maxwellian gas is monotonically nonincreasing in time, providing smooth, strictly positive counterexamples that are even mixtures of two Gaussians at different temperatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: McKean conjectured in 1966 that the entropy production of Kac's model is nonincreasing in time, a property that would be desirable for understanding convergence to equilibrium and the structure of the Boltzmann equation. Testing this conjecture is important for validating theoretical understanding of this classical kinetic model.

Method: The authors construct explicit counterexamples using even mixtures of two Gaussians at different temperatures. They demonstrate that along these solution trajectories, entropy production increases at some times, and further show that every entropy derivative beyond the first also violates its expected sign at some point along the same solution.

Result: The paper provides smooth, strictly positive counterexamples (mixtures of two Gaussians with different temperatures) that disprove McKean's conjecture for the standard uniform-angle Kac equation. Additionally, all higher-order entropy derivatives beyond the first are shown to violate their expected signs at some time along the same solution trajectory.

Conclusion: McKean's conjecture is false for the standard uniform-angle Kac equation, meaning entropy production is not monotonically nonincreasing in time for this model. The counterexamples can be chosen with physically natural normalization (unit mass and second moment).

Abstract: In 1966, McKean conjectured that the entropy production of Kac's caricature of a Maxwellian gas is nonincreasing in time \cite{McKean66}. We disprove this conjecture for the standard uniform-angle Kac equation. Our counterexamples are smooth, strictly positive, even mixtures of two Gaussians at different temperatures. We also show that every entropy derivative beyond the first violates its expected sign at some time along the same solution. The initial densities can be chosen with unit mass and second moment. mass and second moment.

</details>


### [83] [Ill-Posedness of the Euler Equations Linearized around Homogeneous Steady States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06313)
*Matei P. Coiculescu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that the 2D incompressible Euler equations linearized around homogeneous steady states with rotational symmetry (but not radial power-law vortices) are ill-posed on the space L^2_m of square-integrable functions with m-fold rotational symmetry, for any m≥2, l≥1, and α∈(0,1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand stability/instability properties of homogeneous steady states of the 2D Euler equations with rotational symmetry, specifically whether linearization around such states yields well-posed dynamics on function spaces with symmetry constraints.

Method: The paper analyzes the linearized vorticity formulation of the incompressible Euler equations around homogeneous steady states of the form r^{-α}f(θ) with (m·l)-fold rotational symmetry, distinguishing between the radial power-law vortex case (f constant) and the general non-radial case, and examines well-posedness on L^2_m(ℝ²).

Result: It is proven that for any homogeneous steady state that is NOT a radial power-law vortex, the linearized Euler equations are ill-posed on L^2_m(ℝ²) for all m≥2, l≥1, and α∈(0,1).

Conclusion: Non-radial homogeneous steady states with rotational symmetry lead to ill-posed linearized dynamics on the symmetric square-integrable function space, highlighting a fundamental distinction from the radial power-law vortex case.

Abstract: Let $L^2_m(\mathbb{R}^2)$ be the space of square-integrable functions on $\mathbb{R}^2$ with $m$-fold rotational symmetry. Let $\overlineω(r,θ) = r^{-α}f(θ)$ be a homogeneous steady state of the two-dimensional incompressible Euler equations with $(m\cdot l)$-fold rotational symmetry. If $f(θ)$ is a constant function we say that $\overlineω$ is a radial power-law vortex. We prove that the incompressible Euler equations in vorticity form, linearized around any homogeneous steady state $\overlineω$ that is not a radial power-law vortex, are ill-posed on $L^2_m(\mathbb{R}^2)$ for any $m\geq 2$, any $l\geq 1$ and $α\in (0,1)$.

</details>


### [84] [An Inverse Problem for Determining the Piston Speed from a Given Lipschitz Leading Shock](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06317)
*Gui-Qiang G. Chen,Qianfeng Li,Yun Pu,Yongqian Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes an inverse problem for the isentropic Euler equations (p-system) in a shock tube, where the goal is to recover the piston speed and flow field from a prescribed leading shock trajectory and initial data. Using an Oleĭnik-type entropy condition, the authors develop a modified wavefront tracking scheme to construct the flow field behind the shock and determine the corresponding piston speed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The problem is inverse in nature: rather than giving the piston motion and computing the resulting shock, the authors seek to determine what piston motion produces a desired leading shock trajectory. This is practically relevant for shock tube design and control.

Method: The authors prescribe the leading shock trajectory as a given Lipschitz curve, impose an Oleĭnik-type entropy condition on this shock, and develop a modified wavefront tracking scheme to construct the flow field behind the shock and thereby determine the piston speed.

Result: The modified wavefront tracking scheme successfully constructs the flow field behind the prescribed shock, enabling determination of both the corresponding piston speed and the associated flow field.

Conclusion: The proposed constructive approach solves the inverse problem of determining piston motion from a prescribed leading shock under entropy conditions, providing a rigorous framework for this class of shock tube inverse problems.

Abstract: We analyze an inverse problem for determining the piston speed and the associated flow field from a prescribed leading shock and the initial data in a shock tube. The gas flow is described by the isentropic Euler equations (i.e., the $p$-system), while the trajectory of the leading shock is prescribed as a given Lipschitz curve. Under an Oleĭnik-type entropy condition on the leading shock, we develop a modified wavefront tracking scheme to construct the flow field behind the shock. This construction enables us to determine the corresponding piston speed and the associated flow field.

</details>


### [85] [Large-time behavior of solutions to bipolar Euler-Poisson equations with space-dependent damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06336)
*Chunpeng Wang,Jianing Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the Cauchy problem for one-dimensional bipolar Euler-Poisson equations with space-dependent damping, proving that small initial perturbations around a constant steady-state admit a unique global smooth solution converging to the steady-state at algebraic decay rates, matching those of the constant-coefficient damping case without requiring smallness of the damping coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the long-time behavior of solutions to bipolar Euler-Poisson equations with space-dependent damping, which arise in semiconductor hydrodynamic models, particularly when the pressure functions differ and the doping profile is nonzero.

Method: Time-weighted energy method with carefully chosen weights to prove global existence and convergence of solutions.

Result: The Cauchy problem admits a unique global smooth solution for small initial perturbations, converging to the constant steady-state at algebraic decay rates that coincide with those of the constant-coefficient damping case. No smallness of the space-dependent damping coefficients is required.

Conclusion: The influence of spatially dependent damping on decay rates is overcome, demonstrating that the decay rates match the constant-coefficient damping case even without smallness assumptions on the damping coefficients.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with the Cauchy problem for the one-dimensional bipolar Euler-Poisson equations with space-dependent damping, which arise in the hydrodynamic model of semiconductors. Notably, the two pressure functions are allowed to be different, and the doping profile is nonzero. By means of the time-weighted energy method with carefully chosen weights, we prove that if the initial perturbation around a constant steady-state is small enough, the Cauchy problem admits a unique global smooth solution, which converges to the constant steady-state at algebraic decay rates. We show that the decay rates coincide with those established for the constant-coefficients damping case, thereby overcoming the influence of spatially dependent damping on the decay rates of the solutions. A key feature is that no smallness of the space-dependent damping coefficients is required.

</details>


### [86] [Generalized Whiting transform and Flux Estimates for Wave Equations in Subextremal Kerr](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06344)
*Lili He,Sergiu Klainerman*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper presents a purely physical-space, generalized version of Whiting's integro-differential transformation for spin-$\sk$ wave equations on subextremal Kerr spacetimes. The transformation maps solutions to a secondary wave equation on a new metric manifold $\KKt$ with two stationary, asymptotically flat ends, enabling classical Fourier analysis to derive flux bounds at the future event horizon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: All recent advances on boundedness and decay for wave equations on subextremal Kerr spacetimes with non-small angular rotation rely on Whiting's groundbreaking transformation. The authors aim to generalize this transformation into a purely physical-space framework to extend its applicability and analytical power.

Method: The authors construct a generalized physical-space version of Whiting's integro-differential transformation that takes solutions of spin-$\sk$ wave equations on Kerr spacetime $\KK$ to solutions of a secondary wave equation on a new metric manifold $\KKt$ with two stationary, asymptotically flat ends. They identify a subdomain $\DDt$ corresponding to the exterior of a black hole region where the new metric is Lorentzian and the time translation is timelike, then apply classical asymptotic Fourier analysis.

Result: Using the geometric properties of the two asymptotically flat ends of $\KKt$, the authors deduce through classical asymptotic Fourier analysis the desired bound for the flux of $\psi$ at the future event horizon of the Kerr spacetime.

Conclusion: This flux estimate played a crucial role in the authors' companion paper \cite{He-K1}, providing a physical-space geometric framework that makes Whiting's transformation more accessible and applicable to the analysis of wave equations on Kerr spacetimes.

Abstract: All recent advances
  \cite{DRS}, \cite{S-Rita1}, \cite{S-Rita2}, \cite{Millet}, \cite{MaS}, \cite{MaS2} on the boundedness and decay for wave equations on subextremal Kerr spacetimes $\KK(a,m)$,
  with non-small angular rotation, rely in an essential way on Whiting's groundbreaking discovery of a powerful
  integro-differential transformation with the help of which he ruled out exponentially growing modes in \cite{W} (see also \cite{Yacov}, \cite{AMPW} and \cite{Rita} for further developments). We give a purely \textit{physical space}, generalized version, of the transformation which takes general solutions of {spin-$\sk$} wave equations in $\KK=\KK(a,m)$ to solutions of a secondary wave equation on a new metric manifold $\KKt=\KKt(a,m)$ with two stationary, asymptotically flat, ends. Moreover, for a certain subdomain $\DDt\subset\DDt_-$, which can be identified as the exterior of a black hole region, the new metric $\gt$ of $\KKt(a, m)$ is Lorentzian and the time translation $\T $ is timelike. Using the geometric properties of the two ends one can then deduce, by classical asymptotic Fourier analysis techniques, the desired bound for the flux of $ψ$ at the future event horizon.
  This estimate played a crucial role in our companion paper \cite{He-K1}.

</details>


### [87] [Quantitative Estimates of the Field Excited by an Emitter in the Narrow Region Between Two Strictly Convex Inclusions in Two Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06390)
*Saibo Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes electromagnetic field enhancement caused by a dipole-type emitter near two perfectly conducting inclusions with smooth (C^{2,γ}) boundaries in two dimensions. The authors derive enhancement estimates in the narrow region between inclusions, prove a factor of ε^{-1/2} field enhancement far from the emitter, and use a novel harmonic barrier approach (instead of Kelvin transformation) to control the dipole term. They also examine how emitter location affects the field along the shortest segment connecting the two inclusions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding field enhancement near plasmonic or conducting inclusions is important for applications like surface-enhanced spectroscopy and optical sensing. The presence of an emitter (dipole source) adds complexity, and previous techniques (Kelvin transformation) have limitations when studying dipole effects in narrow gaps between inclusions.

Method: The authors derive estimates on field enhancement in the narrow region between two perfectly conducting inclusions with smooth boundaries. They construct a harmonic barrier function to control the dipole term, replacing the Kelvin transformation approach. This allows analysis of field behavior in regions both near and far from the emitter, as well as on the shortest connecting segment between inclusions.

Result: The field is shown to be enhanced by a factor proportional to ε^{-1/2} in regions far from the emitter, where ε characterizes the gap width between the inclusions. The influence of emitter location on the field along the shortest line segment connecting the two inclusions is also characterized.

Conclusion: The paper provides rigorous quantitative estimates for dipole-driven field enhancement between two conducting inclusions, introduces a new harmonic barrier technique that generalizes beyond Kelvin transformation methods, and clarifies the role of emitter positioning in determining field behavior in the gap region.

Abstract: We consider the field enhancement due to the presence of an emitter of the dipole type near two perfectly conducting inclusions with $C^{2,γ}$ boundaries in two dimensions. We derive estimates of field enhancement in the narrow region and prove that the field is enhanced by a factor $\varepsilon^{-1/2}$ in the region far from the emitter. We construct a harmonic barrier to control the dipole term instead of using the Kelvin transformation. We also study how the location of the emitter affects the field on the shortest line segment connecting two inclusions.

</details>


### [88] [Threshold for the existence of scattering states for inhomogeneous nonlinear Schrödinger equations without gauge invariance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06404)
*Mourad Grira,Hayato Miyazaki,Slim Tayachi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes asymptotic behavior of solutions to inhomogeneous nonlinear Schrödinger equations with non-gauge-invariant nonlinearities, proving both absence of scattering below a Strauss-type threshold and small-data scattering above it using Lorentz space Strichartz estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the long-time asymptotic behavior of solutions to inhomogeneous nonlinear Schrödinger equations where the nonlinearity is not gauge-invariant and the spatial coefficient has varying singularity at the origin and decay at infinity.

Method: The authors establish a no-scattering result below a Strauss-type threshold under a coercivity condition, and prove a complementary small-data scattering result above the threshold using non-admissible Strichartz estimates in Lorentz spaces adapted to the singular coefficient.

Result: No scattering states exist below a Strauss-type threshold (determined by decay at infinity) under a coercivity condition, while small-data scattering is proven above this threshold.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete asymptotic picture for this class of inhomogeneous NLS with non-gauge-invariant nonlinearities, including cases with dominant non-oscillatory components, by establishing threshold behavior for scattering versus non-scattering.

Abstract: We consider the asymptotic behavior of solutions to inhomogeneous nonlinear Schrödinger equations with non-gauge-invariant nonlinearities. The spatial coefficient in the nonlinear term may have different orders of singularity at the origin and decay at infinity. Under a coercivity condition involving the coefficient and the nonlinearity, we show that no scattering states exist for the equation below a Strauss-type threshold determined by the decay at infinity. Our class includes nonlinearities with a dominant non-oscillatory component. We also prove a complementary small-data scattering result above this threshold, using non-admissible Strichartz estimates in Lorentz spaces adapted to the singular coefficient.

</details>


### [89] [Optimal Spectral Lower Bounds and Nonradial Nonlinear Asymptotic Stability of a Family of Three-Dimensional Keller--Segel Self-Similar Blow-Up Solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06479)
*Te Li,Yuwei Sun,Kaiqiang Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes the spectral properties and nonlinear asymptotic stability of a family of finite-time self-similar blow-up solutions to the 3D Keller-Segel system, constructed via matched asymptotic expansions. For each sufficiently large matching index n, the full linearized operator around the stationary state is analyzed, showing that after removing scaling/translation modes and finitely many unstable radial modes, the remaining spectrum is separated from the imaginary axis. The spectral gap is 1/4 for modes l≥2, and nonradial nonlinear asymptotic stability is proved via modulation equations, energy estimates, and Brouwer's no-retraction theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The goal is to establish rigorous stability of finite-time self-similar blow-up solutions of the 3D Keller-Segel chemotaxis system, which is a challenging problem due to the nonlocal nature of the linearized operator and the multiplicity of unstable modes.

Method: The authors analyze the full linearized operator on L²(R³) using several complementary techniques: Sturm zero counting for radial modes, a wave operator reducing the nonlocal l=1 equation to a local one, and a Mellin-Newton quadratic form for all l≥2. They then construct an exponentially decaying semigroup on the stable subspace with a modified energy equivalent to L² norm, and use modulation equations, H² energy estimates, control of the scaling derivative, and Brouwer's no-retraction theorem for the nonlinear stability analysis.

Result: After removing scaling/translation modes and finitely many genuinely unstable radial modes, the remaining spectrum is separated from the imaginary axis by a positive distance. The optimal lower bound on the real parts of the spectrum is 1/4 in every mode l≥2. The logarithmic asymptotic decay rate of the semigroup norm equals the stable spectral gap. Nonradial nonlinear asymptotic stability of the self-similar blow-up solutions is proved after suitably choosing initial coefficients in the finitely many unstable radial directions.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete spectral analysis and full nonlinear asymptotic stability result for a family of self-similar blow-up solutions to the 3D Keller-Segel system, resolving the challenge of treating the nonlocal linearized operator across all angular modes.

Abstract: This paper studies the spectral properties and nonlinear asymptotic stability of a family of finite-time self-similar blow-up solutions to the three-dimensional Keller--Segel system constructed by matching interior and exterior profiles within the framework of matched asymptotic expansions. For every sufficiently large matching index $n$, the full linearized operator around the stationary state $U_n$ in self-similar variables is analyzed on $L^2(\mathbb R^3)$. Sturm zero counting in the radial mode, a wave operator that reduces the nonlocal $l=1$ equation to a local equation, and a Mellin--Newton quadratic form for all $l\ge2$ show that, after the scaling and translation modes and the finitely many genuinely unstable radial modes are removed, the remaining spectrum is separated from the imaginary axis by a positive distance. In addition, the optimal lower bound on the real parts of the spectrum is $1/4$ in every mode $l\ge2$. On the stable subspace, an exponentially decaying semigroup and a modified energy equivalent to the $L^2$ norm are constructed, and the logarithmic asymptotic decay rate of the semigroup norm is proved to equal the stable spectral gap. Finally, modulation equations, $H^2$ energy estimates, control of the scaling derivative, and Brouwer's no-retraction theorem yield nonradial nonlinear asymptotic stability of the corresponding self-similar blow-up solutions after the initial coefficients in the finitely many unstable radial directions have been chosen suitably.

</details>


### [90] [On solutions of the modified Helmholtz equation in a planar domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06523)
*Alexander I. Nazarov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A criterion for convexity of 2D domains is established based on solutions to a boundary value problem for the modified Helmholtz equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To relate geometric properties (convexity) of a domain to solutions of elliptic PDEs, providing an analytic characterization of convexity.

Method: The paper proves a criterion for two-dimensional domain convexity using solutions of a boundary value problem for the modified Helmholtz equation (u_xx + u_yy - k^2 u = 0).

Result: A proven criterion linking the convexity of a 2D domain to properties of the modified Helmholtz equation's boundary value problem solution.

Conclusion: Convexity of two-dimensional domains can be characterized analytically through solutions of the modified Helmholtz equation boundary value problem.

Abstract: A criterion for the convexity of a two-dimensional domain is proved in terms of the solution of a boundary value problem for the modified Helmholtz equation.

</details>


### [91] [Rate of convergence of the $p$-torsion function to the distance function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06534)
*Farid Bozorgnia,Masoud Bayrami-Aminlouee,Khudoyor Mamayusupov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The Dirichlet p-torsion function u_p converges uniformly to the distance-to-boundary function d at rate O(1/p) on any bounded domain, with constants depending only on dimension and diameter. This rate is sharp on the ball. Under additional geometric conditions (uniform exterior ball, C² regularity), the paper derives explicit gradient bounds and L^q convergence rates for gradients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The p-torsion function plays a central role in nonlinear PDE theory and shape optimization. While it is known that u_p converges to the distance function d as p→∞, precise quantitative rates of convergence were lacking, especially in the uniform norm on general (possibly nonsmooth) bounded domains. The authors aim to close this gap by providing explicit rates and constants.

Method: The authors use: (1) a radial barrier function with pole at a boundary point as an admissible supersolution for p>n, requiring no boundary regularity; (2) an inscribed ball for the lower bound; (3) an annular barrier under a uniform exterior ball condition; (4) the classical gradient maximum principle for C² domains; and (5) measure-theoretic arguments (showing -Δd is a finite variation measure for C¹ domains with uniform exterior ball) to obtain L^q gradient convergence rates via duality/integration estimates.

Result: (1) Uniform convergence rate O(1/p) of u_p to d on every bounded domain, with constants in terms of dimension and diameter; sharp since on the ball the error is (1+log n)/p + O(p^{-2}). (2) Pointwise bound u_p ≤ K_Ω^{1/(p-1)} d on domains with uniform exterior ball condition, with explicit K_Ω. (3) Gradient sup-norm bound on C² domains: ||∇u_p||_{L^∞}^{p-1} ≤ K_Ω. (4) L^q gradient convergence rates O(p^{-1/2}) for q≤2 and O(p^{-1/q}) for q≥2 on C¹ domains with uniform exterior ball. (5) Demonstration that gradients do not converge uniformly, via the explicit ball profile.

Conclusion: The paper provides sharp or explicit quantitative convergence results for p-torsion functions to the distance function, covering both the uniform norm of the functions themselves and various norms of their gradients, across domains of varying regularity. The exponents for gradient L^q convergence are not claimed sharp, leaving open questions for future improvement.

Abstract: We prove that the Dirichlet $p$-torsion function $u_p$ converges to the distance to the boundary $d$ at the rate $O(1/p)$ in the uniform norm, on every bounded domain and with a constant depending only on the dimension and the diameter. The upper bound comes from a radial barrier with its pole at a boundary point, which is an admissible comparison function for $p>n$ and needs no boundary regularity; the lower bound comes from an inscribed ball. On the ball the error equals $(1+\log n)/p+O(p^{-2})$, so the order $1/p$ cannot be improved in general. Under a uniform exterior ball condition an annular barrier gives $u_p\le K_Ω^{1/(p-1)}d$ for every $p>1$, with $K_Ω$ explicit in the dimension, the diameter and the exterior radius; on convex sets the constant is the diameter, and a multiple of $d$ is a supersolution whenever $-Δd$ has a positive distributional lower bound. On $C^2$ domains, the same barrier and the classical gradient maximum principle give $\norm{\nabla u_p}_{L^\infty}^{p-1}\le K_Ω$. When $-Δd$ is a measure of finite total variation, which we prove for $C^1$ domains with a uniform exterior ball, the gradients converge in $L^q$ at the rate $O(p^{-1/2})$ for $q\le2$ and $O(p^{-1/q})$ for $q\ge2$; these exponents are not claimed to be sharp. The explicit ball profile shows that the gradients do not converge uniformly.

</details>


### [92] [The periodic Wulff problem in two dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06584)
*Marco Bonacini,Alberto Fiorini*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper characterizes minimizers of anisotropic perimeter on a flat torus under area constraint, showing two phase transitions: from a droplet (Wulff shape) to a lamellar phase (flat band), then to a bubble (complement of Wulff shape), with the lamellar phase potentially absent or coexisting with infinitely many minimizers depending on the surface tension properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how anisotropy affects the shape of periodic global minimizers of perimeter under volume constraint, and to determine the complete phase diagram of minimizers on the flat torus.

Method: A mathematical characterization of anisotropic perimeter minimizers on the flat torus with area constraint, analyzing how the structure of the surface tension determines which phases (droplet, lamellar, bubble) appear and their transitions.

Result: Two phase transitions occur as area varies: droplet → lamellar (flat band wrapping in a direction favored by anisotropy) → bubble. For non-symmetric surface tensions the lamellar phase disappears; for certain non-regular surface tensions, infinitely many minimizing configurations coexist with the lamellar phase.

Conclusion: The phase structure of anisotropic perimeter minimizers on a torus is fully characterized, revealing that anisotropy fundamentally determines the preferred orientation of lamellar bands, and that symmetry and regularity of the surface tension critically affect the existence and uniqueness of phases.

Abstract: We characterize the minimizers of the anisotropic perimeter in the flat torus under an area constraint. We show that, depending on the area, the periodic global minimizer undergoes two phase transitions, changing from a droplet (a rescaled Wulff shape) to a flat band wrapping around the torus in a preferred direction determined by the anisotropy (lamellar phase), and finally to a bubble (the complement of a rescaled Wulff shape). For certain non-symmetric surface tensions, the lamellar phase is absent altogether; for other non regular surface tensions, there exist infinitely many minimizing configurations coexisting with the lamellar phase.

</details>


### [93] [Singular Extremal Solutions on Thin Ellipsoids with Varying Nonlinearities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06673)
*Bao Yu,Yang Zhou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that on sufficiently thin ellipsoids in sufficiently large dimensions, one can construct a nonlinearity f_ε whose extremal solution is an unbounded H^1_0 solution. Combined with Dancer's result for the Gelfand nonlinearity (which gives a bounded extremal solution on the same domains), this resolves Brézis' Open Problem 6.1 in a two-part sense for all sufficiently large dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address Brézis' Open Problem 6.1, which concerns whether extremal solutions of semi-linear elliptic problems can be unbounded while still belonging to H^1_0 on certain domains, and to understand the interplay between domain geometry (thinness), nonlinearity, and regularity of extremal solutions.

Method: The authors consider the thin ellipsoid Ω_ε in R^N (N=m+1) with principal axes scaled by ε. They construct a specific smooth, positive, strictly increasing, strictly convex, superlinear nonlinearity f_ε for which the extremal solution on Ω_ε is an unbounded H^1_0 solution, using tools from large-dimension analysis. They combine this with Dancer's thin-domain regularity theorem for f(t)=e^t to contrast bounded versus unbounded extremal solutions on the same domains.

Result: For every sufficiently large dimension N and every sufficiently small ε>0, there exists a nonlinearity f_ε such that the extremal problem on the thin ellipsoid Ω_ε admits an unbounded H^1_0 extremal solution. Simultaneously, the Gelfand problem on the same thin ellipsoid admits a bounded extremal solution, illustrating both sides of the dichotomy.

Conclusion: Brézis' Open Problem 6.1 is resolved in a two-part sense in all sufficiently large dimensions: unbounded H^1_0 extremal solutions exist (for suitably chosen nonlinearities), while bounded extremal solutions persist for the canonical Gelfand nonlinearity on the same thin domains.

Abstract: Let $N=m+1$ and consider the thin ellipsoid \[ Ω_\varepsilon=\{(y,x_N)\in\mathbb R^m\times\mathbb R:\ |y|^2+\varepsilon^{-2}x_N^2<1\}. \] We prove that in every sufficiently large dimension, for every sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$, there exists a smooth positive, strictly increasing, strictly convex, superlinear nonlinearity $f_\varepsilon$ for which the extremal solution in $Ω_\varepsilon$ is an unbounded $H^1_0$ solution. Combining this result with Dancer's thin-domain regularity theorem for the Gelfand nonlinearity $f(t)=e^t$, we obtain on the same sufficiently thin ellipsoids a bounded Gelfand extremal solution and an unbounded extremal solution for another nonlinearity. Thus, in this two-part sense, Brézis' Open Problem~6.1 is resolved in every sufficiently large dimension.

</details>


### [94] [Gradient estimates for the Lagrangian mean curvature equation with Lipschitz critical phase](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06692)
*Ruosi Chen,Xingchen Zhou,Ruixuan Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves an a priori interior gradient estimate for solutions to the Lagrangian mean curvature equation under critical and supercritical phase conditions, assuming only bounded Lipschitz norm of the prescribed phase function θ(x).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The gradient estimate for the Lagrangian mean curvature equation is a fundamental a priori estimate needed for existence and regularity theory. Previous results required stronger assumptions on the phase function, and this work aims to extend these estimates to the critical/supercritical phase regime (θ ≥ (n-2)π/2) under the minimal assumption that the phase has bounded Lipschitz norm.

Method: The paper proves a priori interior gradient estimates using analytic techniques adapted to the Lagrangian mean curvature equation. The key innovation appears to be handling the critical and supercritical phase condition θ ≥ (n-2)π/2 while only assuming the prescribed phase θ(x) has bounded Lipschitz norm, rather than stronger regularity assumptions.

Result: The authors establish the gradient estimate for n ≥ 3 solutions to the Lagrangian mean curvature equation under the phase condition θ ≥ (n-2)π/2, with the prescribed phase only required to have bounded Lipschitz norm.

Conclusion: The result extends the known gradient estimates for the Lagrangian mean curvature equation to the critical and supercritical regime under minimal regularity assumptions on the phase function, which is significant for tackling existence and regularity questions in these cases.

Abstract: Let $n\ge3$. We prove a priori interior gradient estimate for the Lagrangian mean curvature equation \[
  \sum_{i=1}^n\arctanλ_i(D^2u)=θ(x) \] under the critical and supercritical phase condition $θ\ge (n-2)π/2$, assuming only that the Lipschitz norm of the prescribed phase is bounded.

</details>


### [95] [Global Strong Solutions for Maxwell-Stefan Diffusion with Additive Friction Coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06732)
*Dieter Bothe*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies Maxwell-Stefan diffusion with additive friction coefficients, showing that in mass fractions the system reduces to a constrained pair-friction block, while in mole fractions it is the classical ideal Maxwell-Stefan system. The additive structure yields species-diagonal dissipation, explicit spectral/interlacing results with global real-analytic root coordinates on the simplex, and diagonal principal parts with invariant rectangles. The authors prove global strong solvability on bounded C^{2+α} domains for all N≥2, d≥2, with solutions being classical for positive times and converging exponentially to equilibrium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous mathematical theory (global strong solvability, regularity, and long-time behavior) for the Maxwell-Stefan diffusion system under the physically relevant assumption of additive friction coefficients, which renders the dissipation structure tractable and enables entropy-based analysis tools.

Method: Key techniques include: (1) reducing the constrained pair-friction system in mass fractions and connecting to the classical MS system in mole fractions; (2) analyzing the constrained resolvent as a scalar shift of a compression of a diagonal operator, yielding explicit inverse and interlacing spectral roots that serve as global real-analytic coordinates on the simplex; (3) introducing entropy-stabilized one-sided multi-EPD truncations with Euler-Poisson-Darboux entropies to cancel mixed quadratic production, plus truncation-weighted mixing-entropy corrections for transverse coercivity (N≥4); (4) applying Caccioppoli/logarithmic estimates, shrinking arguments, and critical mass to get Hölder continuity; (5) symmetrizing the moment system via mixing entropy and using frozen conormal estimates for spatial Lipschitz bounds, combined with time Hölder control and maximal regularity.

Result: The paper proves global strong solvability on bounded C^{2+α} domains (0<α<1) for every N≥2 and d≥2 and p>d+2, for all uniformly positive, compatible initial concentrations in the natural trace class. Solutions become classical for positive times and converge exponentially to equilibrium in relative entropy, L², and C¹(Ω̄).

Conclusion: Additive friction coefficients make the Maxwell-Stefan system amenable to a complete global regularity and solvability theory via entropy-dissipation and spectral-coordinate analysis; the proposed one-sided multi-EPD truncations overcome high-dimensional technical obstructions (transverse coercivity for N≥4) and yield strong solutions with exponential stabilization.

Abstract: We study Maxwell-Stefan diffusion with additive friction coefficients $f_{ij}=g_i+g_j$. In mass fractions, the system isolates the constrained pair-friction block; in mole fractions, it is the classical ideal isothermal/isobaric Maxwell-Stefan system at constant total molar concentration. Additivity makes the constrained pair-friction dissipation species-diagonal; conversely, species-diagonality on one interior barycentric constraint space forces a pair-sum representation. At operator level, the positive constrained relaxation operator is a scalar shift of a compression of $G=diag[g_1,\ldots,g_N]$. Its scalar resolvent yields both an explicit constrained inverse and interlacing spectral roots, which form global real-analytic coordinates on the open simplex and whose differentials are left eigen-covectors. In root coordinates the principal part is diagonal, no self-square gradient term occurs, and scalar comparison yields invariant rectangles and separation from the simplex boundary. For regularity we introduce entropy-stabilized one-sided multi-EPD truncations: Euler-Poisson-Darboux entropies cancel mixed quadratic production, while for $N\ge4$ a truncation-weighted mixing-entropy correction supplies transverse coercivity. Caccioppoli and logarithmic estimates, shrinking, and critical mass yield Hölder continuity up to the Neumann boundary. The mixing entropy also symmetrizes the moment system; frozen conormal estimates give spatial Lipschitz bounds. Together with time Hölder control and short-interval maximal regularity, this yields global strong solvability on bounded $C^{2+α}$ domains with $0<α<1$, for each $N\ge2$, $d\ge2$, and $p>d+2$, for all uniformly positive, compatible initial concentrations in the natural trace class. Solutions become classical for positive times and converge exponentially to equilibrium in relative entropy, $L^2$, and $C^1(\barΩ)$.

</details>


### [96] [Uniform bounds for a thermo-diffusive system beyond exponential growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06741)
*Weinan Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes a global-in-time uniform bound for solutions of a thermo-diffusive system in the whole space with bounded nonnegative initial data, extending previous results to allow exponential and certain superexponential reaction rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend existing results on global-in-time uniform bounds for thermo-diffusive systems, which were previously limited to strictly subexponential reaction rates, into a broader class including exponential and superexponential rates.

Method: The authors prove the uniform bound analytically for the thermo-diffusive system with bounded nonnegative initial data, handling exponential reaction rates and a range of superexponential rates that depend on the diffusivity parameters of the system.

Result: A global-in-time uniform bound is proven for the thermo-diffusive system in the whole space, valid for exponential reaction rates and a range of superexponential rates determined by the diffusivities.

Conclusion: The result generalizes and extends the prior work of La, Roquejoffre, and Ryzhik and of Leontev and Ryzhik beyond the strictly subexponential regime.

Abstract: In this paper, we prove a global-in-time uniform bound for solutions to a thermo-diffusive system in the whole space with bounded nonnegative initial data. The result allows exponential reaction rates and a range of superexponential rates determined by the diffusivities. This extends the recent results of La, Roquejoffre, and Ryzhik \cite{LRR24} and of Leontev and Ryzhik \cite{LR26} beyond their strictly subexponential range.

</details>


### [97] [Higher order KdV-type equations with Robin boundary conditions on $\mathbb{R}^+$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06762)
*Fernando. A. Gallego,Chulkwang. Kwak*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves local well-posedness for the higher-order KdV-type equation on the right half-line with Robin boundary conditions, extending the low regularity theory from Dirichlet to Robin data. The key innovation is a unified transform representation where the Robin parameter dependence is exactly factored into (k-iγ)^(-1), showing the only possible pole generates a residue mode e^{-γx+γ^{2j+1}t} when j is odd and γ>0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend low regularity well-posedness theory for higher-order KdV-type equations from Dirichlet boundary conditions to Robin boundary conditions, which introduce coupling of adjacent boundary traces and a nontrivial coupled system in each spectral sector.

Method: The paper develops an explicit unified transform representation for the higher-order IBVP with Robin boundary conditions. Rather than computing the inverse of the coupled system for unknown boundary transforms, they use Lagrange interpolation at rotated spectral points to resolve the required linear combination. This yields an exact factorization concentrating the Robin parameter dependence into a single factor (k-iγ)^(-1), combined with modified Fourier restriction spaces and higher-order KdV bilinear estimates.

Result: Local well-posedness for u_0 ∈ H^s(R^+) and φ_ℓ ∈ H^{(s+j-ℓ)/(2j+1)}(0,T) in the range -j+1/4 < s < 3/2, extending the Dirichlet IBVP low regularity theory to Robin conditions. The representation recovers classical KdV formulas for j=1 and is consistent with the Dirichlet case under reciprocal Robin limit.

Conclusion: The unified transform approach successfully handles Robin boundary conditions for higher-order KdV equations, with the only possible Robin pole at k=iγ generating a residue mode e^{-γx+γ^{2j+1}t} exactly when j is odd and γ>0. This establishes a robust framework connecting Robin and Dirichlet boundary data regimes.

Abstract: We study the initial boundary value problem (IBVP) for the higher order Korteweg--de Vries type equation \[\partial_tu+(-1)^{j+1}\partial_x^{2j+1}u+\frac12\partial_x(u^2)=0,\qquad j\in\mathbb{N},\] on the right half line, subject to the Robin boundary conditions \[(\partial_x+γ)\partial_x^{\ell-1}u(t,0)=\varphi_\ell(t),\qquad 1\le\ell\le j,\] where $γ\in\mathbb{R}$ is common to all boundary conditions. We prove local well posedness for \[u_0\in H^s(\mathbb{R}^+),\qquad \varphi_\ell\in H^{\frac{s+j-\ell}{2j+1}}(0,T),\qquad -j+\frac14<s<\frac32.\] In particular, the regularity range extends the low regularity theory for the higher order Dirichlet IBVP to Robin boundary conditions.
  The main ingredient is an explicit unified transform representation for the higher order IBVP with Robin boundary conditions. The Robin hierarchy couples adjacent boundary traces and leads, in each spectral sector, to a nontrivial system for the unknown boundary transforms. Rather than computing the inverse of this system, we resolve precisely the linear combination required by the unified transform representation through Lagrange interpolation at the rotated spectral points. This yields an exact factorization in which the dependence on the Robin parameter is concentrated in the single factor $(k-iγ)^{-1}$. Consequently, the only possible Robin pole is $k=iγ$, which contributes the residue mode \[e^{-γx+γ^{2j+1}t}\] exactly when $j$ is odd and $γ>0$. Combining this representation with linear estimates in modified Fourier restriction spaces and higher order KdV bilinear estimates yields the low regularity well posedness result. The representation also recovers the classical KdV formulas with Robin and Neumann boundary data when $j=1$ and is formally consistent with the higher order Dirichlet representation under the reciprocal Robin limit.

</details>


### [98] [Sharp Hessian integrability for fully nonlinear elliptic supersolutions in low dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06817)
*Thialita M. Nascimento,Eduardo V. Teixeira*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper settles the planar sharp Hessian-integrability conjecture of Armstrong-Silvestre-Smart for fully nonlinear uniformly elliptic equations, proving the optimal W^{2,ε} exponent in the plane is ε₂(κ)=2/(κ+1), and establishing ε₃(κ)=3/(2κ+1) for 1≤κ≤4 in dimension three via a new spectrally resolved continuum-in-opening mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To close the long-standing optimal W^{2,ε} regularity bounds for viscosity supersolutions of fully nonlinear uniformly elliptic equations, resolving the sharp planar conjecture proposed by Armstrong, Silvestre, and Smart in 2012, and extending precision to dimension three where gaps remained.

Method: Introduces a spectrally resolved continuum-in-opening mechanism for contact geometry. Unlike classical pointwise reductions (which discard critical spectral information), the approach keeps track of the vertex Jacobian retaining the negative index of the Hessian and its mean negative curvature throughout contact evolution. These variables are optimized only after integration over all contact openings, reducing the problem to a finite family of explicit one-dimensional kernels.

Result: Proves the planar optimal exponent is exactly ε₂(κ)=2/(κ+1). In dimension three, the same mechanism recovers the known upper obstruction throughout 1≤κ≤4, giving ε₃(κ)=3/(2κ+1). Beyond this threshold it yields a closed algebraic lower bound and identifies the precise spectral configuration causing the remaining gap.

Conclusion: The sharp planar conjecture is resolved, exact three-dimensional exponents are identified in the range κ≤4, and a broader principle is exposed: second-order regularity can be extracted from the spectral geometry of contact sets via explicit kernel analysis, offering a pathway for higher dimensions.

Abstract: We settle the planar sharp Hessian-integrability conjecture of Armstrong, Silvestre, and Smart [\emph{Comm. Pure Appl. Math.} \textbf{65} (2012), 1169--1184] for viscosity supersolutions of fully nonlinear uniformly elliptic equations. If $κ=Λ/λ$, then the optimal exponent in the $W^{2,ε}$ regularity theory in the plane is exactly $$
  ε_2(κ)=\frac{2}{κ+1}. $$ The same mechanism reaches the known upper obstruction in dimension three throughout the full range $1\le κ\le 4$ and therefore gives $$
  ε_3(κ)=\frac{3}{2κ+1}
  \qquad(1\leκ\le4). $$ Beyond this threshold, it yields a closed algebraic lower bound and identifies the precise spectral configuration responsible for the remaining gap. The proof introduces a spectrally resolved continuum-in-opening mechanism for contact geometry. Throughout the contact evolution, the vertex Jacobian retains the negative index of the Hessian and its mean negative curvature---data erased by the classical pointwise reduction---and these variables are optimized only after integration over all openings. This reduces the analysis to a finite family of explicit one-dimensional kernels whose critical exponents recover the sharp planar threshold, identify the exact three-dimensional regime above, and expose a broader principle for extracting second-order regularity from the spectral geometry of contact sets.

</details>


### [99] [Non-decaying weak solutions to the 2D quasi-geostrophic equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06836)
*David M. Ambrose,Ryan Aschoff,Elaine Cozzi,James P. Kelliher*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global existence of weak solutions to the two-dimensional quasi-geostrophic equations without dissipation, for bounded temperature lacking spatial decay and velocity in L²_ul(ℝ²), using a spectral Serfati identity and a zero-dissipation limit argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to establish global well-posedness/weak solution existence for the two-dimensional quasi-geostrophic (QG) equations in the inviscid case, under very mild assumptions on the initial data (bounded temperature without spatial decay and velocity in L²_ul). This extends prior results that required stronger decay or regularity assumptions on the data.

Method: The authors introduce a spectral Serfati identity to obtain uniform L²_ul bounds on a sequence of velocities satisfying dissipative (viscous) QG equations. Combined with a maximum principle on the scalar temperature, these uniform bounds allow passing to the zero-dissipation limit, yielding global-in-time weak solutions.

Result: Global existence of weak solutions is established for the inviscid 2D quasi-geostrophic equation with bounded temperature (lacking spatial decay) and velocity in the uniformly local L² space L²_ul(ℝ²).

Conclusion: The spectral Serfati identity provides a key technical tool enabling uniform velocity estimates that, together with the temperature maximum principle, permit a rigorous zero-dissipation limit to construct global weak solutions in very general data spaces.

Abstract: We investigate weak solutions to the two-dimensional quasi-geostrophic equations without dissipation. We establish global existence of weak solutions for temperature bounded and lacking spatial decay and velocity in the space $L^2_{ul}(\mathbb{R}^2)$. Our methods rely on a spectral Serfati identity, which we use to establish uniform $L^2_{ul}$ bounds on a sequence of velocities satisfying the dissipative equations. These bounds, combined with a maximum principle on the scalar temperature, allow us to pass to the zero-dissipation limit, giving global-in-time weak solutions.

</details>


### [100] [Gradient estimates for the fractional $p$-Laplacian in the supercritical regime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06924)
*Ying Li,Chao Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves a pointwise gradient estimate (involving truncated Wolff potentials) for weak solutions of the fractional p-Laplace equation with measure data, showing Fréchet differentiability and a linear bound on the gradient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish pointwise gradient estimates for the fractional p-Laplace equation with measure data, extending classical potential-theoretic estimates to the nonlocal, nonlinear setting.

Method: The proof combines comparison with fractional p-harmonic replacements and an affine decay estimate for homogeneous solutions, with constants independent of affine slope. In the large slope regime, a Schauder estimate for the linear nonlocal equation satisfied by the affine remainder is used.

Result: Under the condition that a truncated Wolff potential is finite, the solution u is Fréchet differentiable at x0 and satisfies |∇u(x0)| ≤ C[𝒜 + 𝒲], giving a sharp pointwise gradient bound in terms of the solution's local oscillation and the Wolff potential of the measure.

Conclusion: The paper establishes optimal pointwise gradient estimates for fractional p-Laplace equations with measure data, demonstrating Fréchet differentiability and providing a quantitative bound via truncated Wolff potentials and local oscillation terms.

Abstract: We prove a pointwise gradient estimate for the fractional $p$-Laplace equation with measure data. Let $n\ge 2$, $p>1$ and $\max\{n/p,1/p'\}<s<1$, where $p'=p/(p-1)$. Suppose $u\in W^{s,p}(\mathbb R^n)$ is a weak solution of
  \[
  (-Δ_p)^s u=μ\quad\text{in }Ω,
  \] with $μ\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{loc}}(Ω)$. Set $γ=s-(p-1)/p$. If $B_{2R}(x_0)\SubsetΩ$ and $\mathcal{W}_{γ,p}^{|μ|}(x_0,2R)<\infty$, then $u$ is Fréchet differentiable at $x_0$, and
  \[|\nabla u(x_0)|\le C\bigl[\mathcal A(u;x_0,2R)+\mathcal{W}_{γ,p}^{|μ|}(x_0,2R)\bigr], \] where $\mathcal{W}_{γ,p}^{|μ|}$ denotes the truncated Wolff potential, and $\mathcal A$ depends on the local oscillation of $u$ and its nonlocal tail. The proof uses a comparison with fractional $p$-harmonic replacements and an affine decay estimate for homogeneous solutions, with constants independent of the affine slope. In the large slope regime, the affine decay estimate follows from a Schauder estimate for the linear nonlocal equation satisfied by the affine remainder.

</details>


### [101] [Local Well-Posedness of the Boundary Layer Equations for Dilatant Power-Law Fluids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06944)
*Mingxue Zhang,Zhonger Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves local-in-time existence and uniqueness of monotone solutions to the 2D nonstationary boundary-layer equations for a dilatant power-law fluid in a periodic half-space for exponent range 1 < n < 7/3, partially resolving an open problem by Oleinik and Samokhin.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to address the eleventh open problem posed by Oleinik and Samokhin concerning the existence and uniqueness of solutions to nonstationary boundary-layer systems for dilatant (shear-thickening) power-law fluids, which had remained unresolved.

Method: The proof combines several techniques: (1) constructing solutions through tangential regularization; (2) deriving uniform a priori estimates with weighted Sobolev spaces; (3) introducing a suitable "good unknown" to compensate for the loss of one tangential derivative caused by the normal velocity; (4) using weighted energy estimates, the Faà di Bruno formula, and maximum/minimum principles to control the nonlinear degenerate diffusion term; and (5) propagating weighted monotonicity of vorticity under Oleinik's monotonicity condition.

Result: The paper establishes local-in-time existence and uniqueness of monotone solutions for 1 < n < 7/3 in a periodic half-space, partially resolving the eleventh open problem of Oleinik and Samokhin for dilatant fluids.

Conclusion: The nonlinear degenerate diffusion term ∂²_y(ω^n) is effectively controlled within the exponent range 1 < n < 7/3, where the effective diffusion coefficient vanishes as vorticity decays at infinity, yielding well-posedness in this regime.

Abstract: We establish the local-in-time existence and uniqueness of monotone solutions to the two-dimensional nonstationary boundary-layer equations for a dilatant power-law fluid in a periodic half-space for $1<n<\frac{7}{3}$. Under Oleinik's monotonicity condition and suitable weighted Sobolev assumptions on the initial data and outer flow, we construct solutions through tangential regularization and derive uniform a priori estimates. A suitable good unknown compensates for the loss of one tangential derivative caused by the normal velocity. By combining weighted energy estimates, the Faà di Bruno formula, and the maximum and minimum principles, we control the nonlinear degenerate diffusion term $\partial_y^2(ω^n)$, whose effective diffusion coefficient vanishes as $ω=\partial_yu$ decays at infinity, and propagate the weighted monotonicity of the vorticity. Within this exponent range, our result partially resolves the eleventh open problem posed by Oleinik and Samokhin \cite{OAO} on the existence and uniqueness of solutions to nonstationary boundary-layer systems for dilatant fluids.

</details>


### [102] [Modified Scattering and Asymptotics for Perturbed One-Dimensional Cubic NLS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07044)
*Vinh Nguyen,Avy Soffer,Truong Vu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the long-time dynamics of small solutions to a 1D nonlinear Schrödinger equation with cubic nonlinearity (long range), a spatially localized quintic term, and a first-order derivative term (γi∂_x v). The authors construct global forward modified wave operators, prove global existence, sharp t^{-1/2} decay, and modified scattering for small data. A novel finding is that the Duhamel tail from the localized quintic term exhibits a universal inner scaling limit at the distinguished frequency ζ = -γ/2 on scale ~ t^{-1/2}, with a shape that is C^{2,1} but not C^3 at the center. Higher-order outer expansions are also constructed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the long-time behavior of small solutions to an NLS equation with both long-range (cubic) and short-range (localized quintic) nonlinearities, going beyond the standard leading-order modified scattering to capture subleading corrections and universal scaling phenomena.
