Method: The analysis combines: (1) global forward modified wave operator construction via Duhamel tail analysis and weighted energy estimates; (2) two-level separation of long-range (cubic) versus short-range (localized quintic) contributions; (3) analysis of the Duhamel tail's inner scaling limit at a distinguished frequency with explicit universal limiting shape; (4) rigorous construction of higher-order outer expansions to all integer orders (or finite order for rapidly decaying W).

Result: Global existence, sharp t^{-1/2} decay, and forward modified scattering are established for small data. The principal new phenomenon: the localized quintic Duhamel tail has a universal inner scaling limit at ζ = -γ/2 on scale |ζ+γ/2| ~ t^{-1/2}, which is explicit, optimal in C^{2,1}_{loc}, and fails to be C^3 at the center when both the scattering profile value on the distinguished ray and the zeroth moment of W are nonzero. Higher-order outer expansions are constructed through all integer orders allowed by W's decay.

Conclusion: The paper fully characterizes the long-time asymptotics of the system, revealing that beyond the leading modified scattering law, localized nonlinearities produce universal inner-layer structures at distinguished frequencies whose regularity is precisely characterized (C^{2,1} optimal), demonstrating a richer asymptotic structure than previously known for such equations.

Abstract: We study the long-time dynamics of small solutions to the one-dimensional nonlinear Schrödinger equation \[ i\partial_t v+\partial_x^2v-β\abs{v}^2v +\cW(x)\abs{v}^4v+γi\partial_xv=0, \] where $\cW$ is spatially localized. The cubic nonlinearity is long range and produces the logarithmic phase correction, whereas the localized quintic term is short range at leading order. We construct a global forward modified wave operator for small complex asymptotic profiles and prove quantitative final-state estimates. For small data in the weighted energy space, we also establish global existence, sharp $t^{-1/2}$ decay, and forward modified scattering with a unique asymptotic profile. The principal new phenomenon occurs beyond this leading law. The exact Duhamel tail generated by the localized quintic term admits a quantitative inner scaling limit at the distinguished frequency $ζ=-γ/2$ on the scale $\abs{ζ+γ/2}\sim t^{-1/2}$. Its universal shape is explicit and depends on the value of the scattering profile on the distinguished ray and on the zeroth moment of $\cW$. When both quantities are nonzero, the limit is nontrivial, belongs optimally to $C^{2,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}$, and is not $C^3$ at the center. Away from the corresponding self-similar ray $ξ=-γ$, we construct rigorously defined higher-order outer expansions through every integer order allowed by the decay of $\cW$, and to each fixed finite order when $\cW$ is rapidly decreasing.

</details>


### [103] [Asymptotic stability of viscous shock for the outflow problem of 3D Navier-Stokes system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07071)
*Moon-Jin Kang,Hyeonseop Kim,Hobin Lee*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves the asymptotic stability of planar viscous shock waves for the outflow problem of the 3D barotropic compressible Navier-Stokes equations in a half-space with periodic transverse directions, establishing the first shock-stability result for the multidimensional outflow problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish stability of viscous shock waves for multidimensional outflow problems, which had not been previously proven despite existing results for inflow problems and one-dimensional cases.

Method: Combines the a-contraction method with higher-order energy estimates adapted to the half-space boundary. Boundary trace control is achieved via tangential differentiation and the favorable outflow flux sign; highest-order normal derivatives are recovered from time and tangential derivatives through the momentum equation; dynamical shift boundary terms are controlled using exponential decay of the viscous shock tail.

Result: Under small H² perturbations, the outflow problem admits a unique global-in-time solution that converges uniformly to the viscous shock up to a dynamical shift whose velocity decays time-asymptotically, for weak shocks sufficiently far from the boundary.

Conclusion: This provides the first shock-stability result for the multidimensional outflow problem, extending the stability theory of viscous shocks to the half-space outflow setting in three dimensions.

Abstract: We establish the asymptotic stability of planar viscous shock waves for the outflow problem of the three-dimensional barotropic compressible Navier--Stokes equations in a half-space, with periodic boundary conditions imposed in the transverse directions. For a weak shock located sufficiently far from the boundary, we prove that, under small perturbations in $H^{2}$, the outflow problem admits a unique global-in-time solution and that the solution converges uniformly to the viscous shock, up to a dynamical shift, whose velocity time-asymptotically decays. This provides the first shock-stability result for the multidimensional outflow problem. Our proof combines the $a$-contraction method and higher-order energy estimates adapted to the half-space boundary. The boundary trace remaining in the zeroth-order estimate is controlled by differentiating the perturbation system in the tangential directions and exploiting the favorable sign of the outflow flux. The highest-order normal derivatives are then recovered from the time and tangential derivatives through the momentum equation, while the boundary terms generated by the dynamical shift are controlled using the exponential decay of the viscous shock tail.

</details>


### [104] [The capillary $L_p$ dual Minkowski problem for $p>q$ in higher dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07158)
*Ya Gao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves existence and uniqueness for the capillary L_p dual Minkowski problem when p>q with contact angle θ ∈ (0, π/2) in R^(n+1) for n≥3, by reducing it to a Monge-Ampère type equation with Robin boundary condition on a unit spherical cap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish well-posedness (existence and uniqueness) of solutions to the capillary L_p dual Minkowski problem under specific parameter conditions (p>q, contact angle in (0, π/2)), which extends prior results to the higher-dimensional case n≥3.

Method: The problem is reduced to a Monge-Ampère type equation with a Robin boundary condition on the unit spherical cap. A novel auxiliary function is constructed to derive the C^2 estimate for the case p>q in R^(n+1) with n≥3.

Result: Existence and uniqueness of a smooth solution are proven for the capillary L_p dual Minkowski problem under the conditions p>q and contact angle θ ∈ (0, π/2) in R^(n+1), n≥3.

Conclusion: The capillary L_p dual Minkowski problem admits a unique smooth solution for p>q with contact angle θ in (0, π/2) in dimensions n≥3, established via a Monge-Ampère type equation reduction with Robin boundary conditions and a new auxiliary function for C^2 estimates.

Abstract: We prove existence and uniqueness for the capillary $L_p$ dual Minkowski problem for $p>q$ and contact angle $θ\in (0,\fracπ{2})$ in $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$, $n\geq 3$. We reduce it to a Monge-Ampère type equation with a Robin boundary condition on the unit spherical cap, by building a new auxiliary function to obtain the $C^2$ estimate for $p>q$ in $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$ ($n\geq 3$), we then prove that there exists a unique smooth solution that solves this problem provided $θ\in (0, \fracπ{2})$.

</details>


### [105] [Strong maximum principle for nonlocal diffusion in measure spaces: new characterization and population dynamics applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07191)
*Ana Casado-Sanchez,Monica Molina-Becerra,Antonio Suarez*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the existence of nontrivial nonnegative solutions to nonlocal semilinear diffusion equations on general measure spaces with symmetric kernels. The authors provide a complete characterization of the strong maximum principle via a kernel-induced connectivity structure, develop sub- and supersolution methods yielding existence, uniqueness, nonexistence, and regularity results, and demonstrate applicability across discrete, continuous, and hybrid models with numerical experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The primary motivation is to establish the strict positivity of solutions to nonlocal diffusion equations on abstract measure spaces—a major challenge in this general framework. Working on a general measure space unifies diverse settings (discrete, continuous, hybrid, systems across different dimensions), making the results broadly applicable.

Method: The authors provide a novel complete characterization of the strong maximum principle in terms of a connectivity structure induced by the symmetric kernel, which leads to a decomposition of the domain into independent components. They then develop the method of sub- and supersolutions within this setting to obtain existence, uniqueness, and nonexistence results, plus a regularity result ensuring continuity under suitable assumptions.

Result: The paper achieves: (1) a complete characterization of the strong maximum principle via kernel-induced connectivity, (2) existence, uniqueness, and nonexistence results through sub-/supersolution methods, (3) a regularity result guaranteeing continuity of solutions, and (4) numerical experiments illustrating the theoretical findings across various model frameworks.

Conclusion: The abstract measure-space framework successfully unifies and extends a wide range of models (discrete, continuous, hybrid, cross-dimensional systems). The theoretical results, particularly the strong maximum principle characterization and the sub-/supersolution framework, are validated numerically, demonstrating the practical potential of the approach.

Abstract: We study the existence of nontrivial solutions to nonlocal semilinear diffusion equations of the form
  \[
  a_0(x)u(x)-\int_Ωk(x,y)\big(u(y)-u(x)\big)\,dμ(y)=F(x,u), \quad u\geq 0,
  \]
  on a general measure space $(Ω,M,μ)$, where the kernel $k$ is nonnegative and symmetric. In this framework, a major challenge is establishing the strict positivity of solutions. To this end, we provide a novel complete characterization of the strong maximum principle in terms of a connectivity structure induced by the kernel, which leads to a decomposition of the domain into independent components. We also develop the method of sub- and supersolutions in this setting, obtaining existence, uniqueness, and nonexistence results, as well as a regularity result that ensures the continuity of solutions under suitable assumptions. The main interest of working within an abstract measure space lies in its rich applicability, as it unifies and extends a wide variety of frameworks, including discrete, continuous, and hybrid models, or systems interacting across different dimensions. Finally, numerical experiments are presented to illustrate the potential of our theoretical results.

</details>


### [106] [Liouville Theorem for the Constant $Q/R$-Curvature Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07256)
*Han Lu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies positive entire solutions to the flat constant Q/R-curvature equation in dimension n≥5, proving that the weak superharmonicity condition (-Δu≥0) forces all nonconstant solutions to be admissible, which leads to a full classification as positive constants or standard bubbles. The authors also show this condition is structurally necessary, as removing it yields a one-parameter family of positive radial entire solutions near every positive constant that converge to a constant at infinity while both -Δu and -Δ(u^((n-2)/(n-4))) oscillate in sign infinitely many times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To classify positive entire solutions of the flat constant Q/R-curvature equation in high dimensions (n≥5) by establishing that a natural weak superharmonicity condition provides automatic admissibility, and to demonstrate the structural necessity of this condition by analyzing what happens when it is removed.

Method: The approach combines admissibility analysis under the weak superharmonicity condition -Δu≥0 with classification arguments showing all such solutions are constants or standard bubbles. Additionally, the authors construct a one-parameter family of positive radial entire solutions near every positive constant when the superharmonicity condition is removed, analyzing their asymptotic behavior and sign-changing properties.

Result: Under -Δu≥0, all nonconstant solutions are automatically admissible and are classified as positive constants or standard bubbles. When the condition is removed, there exists a one-parameter family of positive radial entire solutions near any positive constant, which converge to the constant at infinity but -Δu and -Δ(u^((n-2)/(n-4))) change sign infinitely often.

Conclusion: The weak superharmonicity condition is both sufficient for a clean classification (constants or bubbles) and structurally necessary, since without it a rich family of oscillatory radial solutions emerges. This delineates exactly where the classification boundary lies for the flat constant Q/R-curvature equation in dimensions n≥5.

Abstract: We study positive entire solutions of the flat constant $Q/R$-curvature equation in dimension $n\geq5$. We prove that the weak superharmonicity condition $-Δu\geq0$ already forces every nonconstant solution to be admissible. With this automatic admissibility, we classify all such solutions as positive constants or standard bubbles. We also show that the weak superharmonicity condition is structurally necessary. Once it is removed, there is a one-parameter family of positive radial entire solutions near every positive constant. These solutions converge to a positive constant at infinity, while both $-Δu$ and $-Δ(u^{(n-2)/(n-4)})$ change sign infinitely many times.

</details>


### [107] [Generalized functions in a Non-Archimedean field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07279)
*Vieri Benci*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces an improved notion of ultrafunctions defined on a non-Archimedean field extending R, which provides a more flexible functional framework than previous constructions and enables proving existence of ultrafunction solutions for several classes of ill-posed evolution problems in PDEs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical distribution theory is insufficient for treating certain problems, and previous ultrafunctions constructions had limitations. There is a need for a more flexible functional framework to handle ill-posed evolution problems in partial differential equations.

Method: The authors refine previous ultrafunctions constructions by introducing an improved notion of ultrafunctions on a non-Archimedean field extending R, yielding a more flexible functional setting than used in prior work.

Result: The improved ultrafunction framework allows the authors to prove the existence of ultrafunction solutions for several classes of ill-posed evolution problems arising in partial differential equations.

Conclusion: The refined ultrafunction approach provides a more flexible functional framework that extends beyond classical distribution theory and can handle ill-posed evolution problems in PDEs.

Abstract: Ultrafunctions are generalized functions defined on a non-Archimedean field extending R. They provide a framework for treating problems that fall outside the classical theory of distributions. In this paper we introduce an improved notion of ultrafunctions which refines the previous constructions. This new approach allows a more flexible functional framework than the one used in other papers and yields a more flexible functional setting. In particular, this approach allows us to prove the existence of ultrafunction solutions for several classes of ill-posed evolution problems arising in partial differential equations.

</details>


### [108] [Asymptotic behavior for the damped Schrödinger equation with nonlinear dissipation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07322)
*Takahisa Inui,Shun Takizawa*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the large-time asymptotic behavior of solutions to the damped Schrödinger equation with nonlinear dissipation in the mass-subcritical regime, proving that the optimal L²-decay rate matches that of the linear solution, and establishing optimal convergence rates for scattering across all powers in this regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear dissipation affects the long-time decay and scattering properties of solutions to the damped Schrödinger equation, specifically whether the presence of nonlinear dissipation degrades the decay rate compared to the linear case.

Method: Analytical techniques involving large-time asymptotic analysis of the damped Schrödinger equation with nonlinear dissipation, focusing on the mass-subcritical case and comparing nonlinear solutions to their linear counterparts.

Result: The optimal L²-decay rate of the nonlinear solution coincides with that of the corresponding linear solution despite the nonlinear dissipation. Additionally, optimal convergence rates for scattering are obtained for all powers in the mass-subcritical regime.

Conclusion: Nonlinear dissipation in the mass-subcritical regime does not alter the optimal L²-decay rate relative to the linear equation, and sharp scattering convergence rates can be fully characterized in this setting.

Abstract: We consider large time asymptotics of solutions to the damped Schrödinger equation with the nonlinear dissipation in the mass-subcritical case. We prove that the optimal $L^2$-decay rate of the nonlinear solution coincides with that of the corresponding linear solution even in the presence of nonlinear dissipation. Moreover, we give the optimal convergence rate for scattering for any power in the mass-subcritical regime.

</details>


### [109] [Low Mach number limit for a compressible two-fluid model with algebraic closure and ill-prepared initial data in critical Besov spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07366)
*Sai Li,Yang Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves the low Mach number limit for a compressible two-fluid model with algebraic closure in d-dimensional tori (d≥2). For large and ill-prepared initial data in critical Besov spaces, the rescaled compressible two-fluid flow exists and converges to the incompressible Navier-Stokes flow as the Mach number tends to zero.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to establish (almost) global convergence in the low Mach number limit for compressible two-fluid models with large, ill-prepared initial data in critical Besov spaces—a result not previously achieved in the critical framework for such models.

Method: The proof employs a high-middle-low frequency decomposition of densities and velocity field, combined with a filtering technique using wave operators. A key novelty is deriving new a priori estimates for the high-middle frequency component of the solution, with estimates depending explicitly on time, frequency parameter, and Mach number.

Result: Provided the Mach number is sufficiently small, the rescaled compressible two-fluid flow exists in critical Besov spaces for any finite time not exceeding the lifespan of the corresponding incompressible flow, and the flow converges to the incompressible Navier-Stokes solution as the Mach number goes to zero.

Conclusion: This is the first work proving (almost) global convergence for large and ill-prepared initial data in the low Mach number limit for compressible two-fluid models within the critical framework.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the low Mach number limit for a compressible two-fluid model with algebraic closure in the $d$-dimensional torus with $d \geq 2$. For large and ill-prepared initial data in critical Besov spaces, we prove that, provided that the Mach number is sufficiently small, the rescaled compressible two-fluid flow exists in critical Besov spaces for any finite time not exceeding the lifespan of the incompressible flow. Moreover, the rescaled compressible two-fluid flow converges to the incompressible Navier--Stokes flow as the Mach number tends to zero. The proof is based on a high-middle-low frequency analysis of the densities and velocity field, combined with a filtering technique involving wave operators. The main novelty is the derivation of new a priori estimates for the high-middle frequency part of the solution to the compressible two-fluid model, depending explicitly on time, the frequency parameter and the Mach number. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first work that proves (almost) global convergence for large and ill-prepared initial data in the low Mach number limit for compressible two-fluid model in critical framework.

</details>


### [110] [Threshold phenomena for least energy solutions of a doubly critical Neumann problem with a critical absorption term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07411)
*Yinbin Deng,Yulin Shi,Pengyan Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the existence and nonexistence of least energy solutions for a doubly critical Neumann problem with a critical absorption term on a smooth bounded domain in R^n (n≥5). The authors prove a sharp threshold phenomenon: there exists a critical value α₀ such that least energy solutions exist when α<α₀, do not exist when α>α₀, and exist at α=α₀ under an additional mean curvature condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The problem combines two critical exponents (the Sobolev critical exponent 2* in the interior and the critical trace exponent 2♯ on the boundary) together with a critical absorption term that carries the same trace exponent but with opposite sign and acts in the interior. This creates a novel competition between the absorption term and the boundary mean curvature correction at the same asymptotic order in the energy expansion, requiring a careful analysis of competing concentration mechanisms.

Method: The authors combine several analytic techniques and geometric tools to analyze the energy expansion and competing concentration mechanisms. They prove the existence of a threshold value α₀ = α₀(λ,Ω) ∈ (0,+∞), and establish existence/nonexistence of least energy solutions on either side of this threshold, with a special condition involving the mean curvature H on ∂Ω for the critical case α=α₀.

Result: The problem admits a least energy solution if α<α₀ and no least energy solution if α>α₀. Additionally, a least energy solution exists at α=α₀ provided α₀ > C(n)·max_{∂Ω} H, where C(n) is a positive constant depending only on the dimension n and H is the mean curvature of the boundary.

Conclusion: The simultaneous presence of interior and boundary critical exponents with a critical absorption term produces a sharp threshold phenomenon in the energy landscape, governed by the parameter α₀ which depends on λ and the domain Ω. The existence at the threshold depends on a delicate balance between the absorption strength and the boundary mean curvature.

Abstract: We investigate the existence and nonexistence of least energy solutions for a doubly critical Neumann problem with a critical absorption term. We consider a positive function $u$ defined on a smooth bounded domain $Ω\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ with $n\ge 5$, satisfying $-Δu + λu = u^{2^* - 1} -αu^{2^\sharp-1}$ inside $Ω$, with Neumann boundary condition $\nabla u\cdot ν= u^{2^\sharp -1}$ on $\partialΩ$, where $λ>0$, $α\ge0$, $2^*=\frac{2n}{n-2}$ is the critical Sobolev exponent and $2^\sharp=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}$ denotes the critical trace exponent.
  The simultaneous presence of the interior and boundary critical exponents together with this critical absorption term creates a new interaction between competing concentration mechanisms. In particular, the absorption term has the same critical trace exponent as the boundary nonlinearity, but with the opposite sign and acting in the interior of $Ω$. Consequently, it competes with the boundary mean curvature correction at the same asymptotic order in the energy expansion, leading to a sharp threshold phenomenon.
  Combining several analytic techniques and geometric tools, we prove the existence of a threshold value $α_{0}=α_{0}(λ,Ω)\in(0,+\infty)$ such that the problem admits a least energy solution if $α<α_{0}$, and no least energy solution if $α>α_{0}$. Moreover, the problem also has a least energy solution at $α=α_0$ provided $α_0> C(n)\max_{\partial Ω} H$, where $C(n)$ is a positive constant depending only on $n$ and $H$ is the mean curvature on $\partialΩ$.

</details>


### [111] [Fully discrete stochastic maximal regularity and $H^\infty$-calculus for second-order elliptic operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07436)
*Foivos Evangelopoulos-Ntemiris*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves fully discrete stochastic maximal L^p-regularity and associated sharp maximal estimates for numerical approximations of parabolic SPDEs, using finite element spatial discretization combined with broad temporal schemes, underpinned by a uniform bounded H^∞-calculus for the discrete operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain optimal discrete regularity results for numerical approximations of stochastic partial differential equations, analogous to the continuous stochastic maximal L^p-regularity theory.

Method: Establishes a bounded H^∞-calculus for the discrete spatial operator A_h (finite element discretization of a general second-order elliptic operator on a smooth bounded convex domain in R^3), uniformly in mesh size h. This underpins the fully discrete stochastic maximal L^p-regularity for broad temporal schemes including rational approximations and the exponential Euler method.

Result: Fully discrete stochastic maximal L^p-regularity and sharp maximal estimates are obtained for the numerical schemes, with a byproduct of discrete-in-space stochastic maximal regularity for the spatial semi-discretizations.

Conclusion: The uniform bounded H^∞-calculus approach yields optimal discrete regularity results for parabolic SPDE approximations, covering both spatial and temporal discretization frameworks.

Abstract: This paper establishes the fully discrete stochastic maximal $L^p$-regularity and the accompanying sharp maximal estimate for numerical approximations of parabolic stochastic partial differential equations. We consider the spatial finite element discretization $A_h$ of a general second-order elliptic operator $A=-\nabla \cdot a\nabla +b\cdot \nabla +c$ with Dirichlet boundary conditions on a smooth, bounded, convex domain in $\mathbb{R}^3$, coupled with a broad class of temporal schemes, including rational approximations and the exponential Euler method. To obtain these optimal discrete regularity results, we establish a bounded $H^\infty$-calculus for the discrete spatial operator $A_h$, uniformly in the mesh size $h$. As a direct byproduct, we also establish the discrete-in-space stochastic maximal regularity for the corresponding spatial semi-discretizations.

</details>


### [112] [Geometric regularity for semi-convex viscosity solutions to the special Lagrangian equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07458)
*Zhenyu Fan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that the gradient graph of W^{2,1} semi-convex viscosity solutions to the 3D subcritical special Lagrangian equation is area-minimizing and smooth, along with smoothness of W^{2,1}∩C^{1,1/3+} semi-convex viscosity solutions, making progress on the Wang-Yuan conjecture. Higher-dimensional analogues are also established.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance the Wang-Yuan conjecture regarding the regularity and area-minimizing property of gradient graphs of viscosity solutions to the special Lagrangian equation, building on previous results that required stronger regularity assumptions.

Method: The authors prove regularity and area-minimizing properties for semi-convex viscosity solutions in W^{2,1} and W^{2,1}∩C^{1,1/3+} spaces, extending results to higher dimensions.

Result: Gradient graphs of W^{2,1} semi-convex viscosity solutions in 3D are shown to be area-minimizing and smooth; smoothness is also proven for W^{2,1}∩C^{1,1/3+} semi-convex viscosity solutions; higher-dimensional results follow.

Conclusion: The results constitute significant progress on the Wang-Yuan conjecture, with the area-minimizing and smoothness properties established under weaker regularity assumptions than previously known.

Abstract: We prove that the gradient graph of any $W^{2,1}$ semi-convex viscosity solution to the three-dimensional subcritical special Lagrangian equation is area-minimizing and smooth. Furthermore, we prove the smoothness of $W^{2,1}\cap C^{1,1/3+}$ semi-convex viscosity solutions. This yields progress on the conjecture proposed by Wang--Yuan [WY13]. The corresponding results in higher dimensions are also established.

</details>


### [113] [Gradient flow approach to Landau equation: A cross-product structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07466)
*Manh Hong Duong,Zihui He*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper introduces a cross-product Landau gradient that commutes with Gaussian mollification, providing a variational characterization of the gradient-flow structure for the spatially homogeneous Landau equation with hard-potential interaction kernels. The construction yields the same geometry as a prior work but offers a different representation requiring only weighted L1-control, and extends to the GENERIC structure of fuzzy Landau equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to provide a new formulation of the gradient-flow structure of the Landau equation that commutes with Gaussian mollification and requires weaker (weighted L1) control conditions, overcoming limitations of existing variational characterisations.

Method: The paper constructs a cross-product Landau gradient that commutes with Gaussian mollification for hard-potential kernels A(|v-v_*|)~⟨v-v_*⟩^γ|v-v_*|^2 with γ∈(-∞,1], yielding a variational characterisation of the gradient-flow structure. This provides an alternative representation of the Landau gradient compared to the Carrillo-Delgadino-Desvillettes-Wu (2024) framework, while preserving the same gradient-flow geometry. The approach is also extended to discuss variational characterisation of the GENERIC structure for fuzzy Landau equations.

Result: The cross-product Landau gradient commutes with Gaussian mollification and provides a variational characterisation of the gradient-flow structure requiring only weighted L1-control, offering an advantage over existing formulations. The same geometric structure as prior work is recovered, and a similar characterisation is discussed for the GENERIC structure of fuzzy Landau equations.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a new gradient-flow formulation for the Landau equation that requires less stringent regularity assumptions while preserving the same underlying geometry as existing approaches, with extensions to fuzzy Landau equations.

Abstract: We introduce a cross-product Landau gradient that commutes with Gaussian mollification. For the hard-potential interaction kernels of the form $A(|v-v_*|)\sim \langle v-v_*\rangle^γ|v-v_*|^2$ with $γ\in(-\infty,1]$, this construction yields a variational characterisation of the gradient-flow structure of the spatially homogeneous Landau equation. The cross-product gradient induces the same gradient-flow geometry as that introduced by Carrillo, Delgadino, Desvillettes and Wu (2024), while providing a different representation of the Landau gradient. The main advantage of this formulation is that it requires only weighted $L^1$-control. We also discuss a similar variational characterisation for the GENERIC structure of the fuzzy Landau equations.

</details>


### [114] [On Analyticity of Solitons for the Periodic Dispersion-Managed Nonlinear Schrödinger Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07535)
*Sebastian Herr,Dirk Hundertmark,Jeremy L. Marzuola,Tim Van Hoose*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper investigates the regularity properties of energy-maximizing solutions for a periodic dispersion-managed fiber optic system with vanishing average dispersion on a torus. The authors prove polynomial then exponential decay of Fourier coefficients (implying analyticity for large torus length L), establish non-triviality via comparison to real-line optimizers, and numerically explore a symmetry conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the regularity and structure of maximizers in dispersion-managed fiber optic systems is important for both mathematical theory and practical applications, yet these properties are not fully understood, particularly in the periodic setting with vanishing average dispersion.

Method: The authors analyze Fourier coefficient decay rates (progressing from polynomial to exponential), employ asymptotic comparison arguments against real-line optimizers to show non-triviality, and conduct numerical experiments including investigation of a symmetry conjecture for the maximizers.

Result: Fourier coefficients decay polynomially and then exponentially (for sufficiently large L), implying the maximizers are analytic. Non-triviality is established via comparison to the real-line case. Numerical experiments support all findings and address the symmetry conjecture.

Conclusion: Energy maximizers for the periodic dispersion-managed fiber optic system are analytic for large torus lengths, non-trivial, and numerically exhibit an inherent symmetry—all contributing to a more complete characterization of these solutions.

Abstract: We explore the regularity of energy maximizers for the Lagrangian of a periodic dispersion managed fiber optic at fixed intensity, on a torus of length $L$ and with vanishing average dispersion. We show that the Fourier coefficients decay at a polynomial rate, and then upgrade this to exponential decay, so that the maximizers are analytic in space for large enough $L$. In addition, by an asymptotic comparison to the optimizers on the real line, we prove that the solutions are non-trivial. We also consider a conjecture that the maximizer necessarily has an underlying symmetry inherent to both the energy functional and the resulting Euler-Lagrange equation. All of our results are supported with illustrative numerical experiments.

</details>


### [115] [The equivalence of solutions to fractional and logarithmic Helmholtz equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07553)
*A. Biswas,J. Groszkiewicz,P. R. Stinga*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves that if a function u solves a fractional Helmholtz equation A^s u = λ^s u for some fixed 0<s<2 and some λ>0, then it solves the same equation for ALL s≠0, and equivalently solves the logarithmic Helmholtz equation log(A)u = (log λ)u, for general nonnegative linear operators A on Banach spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize known results about the fractional Laplacian Helmholtz equation to broader classes of fractional power operators where the Fourier transform is not available, proving an equivalence result between solutions for different fractional orders.

Method: The authors consider a nonnegative linear operator A on a Banach space X, which includes cases where A = -L with L being the infinitesimal generator of a C₀-semigroup. They establish equivalence results between fractional Helmholtz equations across all orders s and the logarithmic Helmholtz equation.

Result: They show that u solving A^s u = λ^s u for some fixed 0<s<2 and some λ>0 is equivalent to u solving it for all s≠0, and equivalently solving log(A)u = (log λ)u. They recover known fractional Laplacian results and provide new applications to elliptic operators in bounded domains, Laplace-Beltrami operators, space-time master equations, the discrete Laplacian, and fractional derivatives.

Conclusion: The equivalence between fixed fractional order solutions and all-orders solutions holds for a very general class of operators beyond the fractional Laplacian, making the results applicable in settings without Fourier transform tools.

Abstract: We show the following equivalence of solutions to general fractional Helmholtz equations: $u$ is a solution to $$A^su=λ^su$$ for \textit{some} fixed $0<s<2$ and some $λ>0$ if and only if $u$ is a solution to the same equation for \textit{all} $s\neq0$. Furthermore, we show as well that $u$ equivalently solves the logarithmic Helmholtz equation $$\log(A)u=(\logλ)u.$$ Here, $A$ is a nonnegative, linear operator on a Banach space $X$. In particular, our results hold whenever $A=-L$, where $L$ is the infinitesimal generator of a $C_0$-semigroup in a Banach space $X$ satisfying mild assumptions that are typical in applications.
  As a particular case, we recover known results for the fractional Laplacian $(-Δ)^s$ in $\mathbb{R}^n$. More importantly, we provide a list of examples of other fractional power operators in settings where the Fourier transform is not available and for which our theorems apply. These include fractional powers and logarithms of second order elliptic operators in bounded domains, Laplace--Beltrami operators on Riemannian manifolds, space-time master equations, the discrete Laplacian, and fractional derivatives.

</details>


### [116] [Strong and Weak-Type Dispersive Estimates for the Energy-Critical Nonlinear Schrödinger Equation with an Inverse-Square Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07585)
*Tiesong Jiang,Kexue Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves dispersive estimates for the 3D defocusing energy-critical nonlinear Schrödinger equation with inverse-square potential La = -Δ + a|x|^{-2}. It extends finite-p theory to the endpoint L^1→L^∞ for nonnegative potentials, and establishes strong decay rates for negative potentials with sharp restrictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the dispersive estimate theory for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation with inverse-square potentials, filling gaps in the endpoint and negative potential regimes where previous results were incomplete or restrictive.

Method: Combines a finite-interval bootstrap argument, nonlinear real interpolation on bounded energy sets, and endpoint Sobolev–Lorentz estimates specially adapted to the operator L_a.

Result: For nonnegative potentials, endpoint L^1→L^∞ dispersive estimates are established. For negative potentials in the global well-posedness range, the free strong decay rate is obtained for 2<p<3/σ, with a limiting Lorentz estimate from L^{(3/σ)',1} to L^{3/σ,∞}. The restriction p<3/σ is shown to be sharp for strong Lebesgue decay.

Conclusion: The paper achieves sharp dispersive estimates for the energy-critical NLS with inverse-square potentials, establishing that the decay restriction p<3/σ is optimal for strong Lebesgue-type decay while providing a complementary Lorentz space result at the limiting endpoint.

Abstract: We prove dispersive estimates for the three-dimensional defocusing energy-critical nonlinear Schrödinger equation associated with $\mathcal L_a=-Δ+a|x|^{-2}$. For nonnegative potentials, we extend the known finite-$p$ theory to the endpoint $L^1\to L^\infty$. For negative potentials in the global well-posedness range, set $σ=\frac12-\sqrt{\frac14+a}$. We obtain the free strong decay rate for $2<p<3/σ$ and the limiting Lorentz estimate from $L^{(3/σ)',1}$ to $L^{3/σ,\infty}$. The restriction $p<3/σ$ is sharp for strong Lebesgue decay. The proof combines a finite-interval bootstrap, nonlinear real interpolation on bounded energy sets, and endpoint Sobolev--Lorentz estimates adapted to $\mathcal L_a$.

</details>


### [117] [Littlewood--Paley operators and semigroup maximal operators on CMO spaces associated to Schödinger operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07593)
*Wanjun Li,Qingze Lin,Liang Song*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies Littlewood-Paley operators s_L and S_L, and the semigroup maximal operator T*_L on the space CMO_L(R^n) associated with Schrödinger operators L = -Δ + V, where V satisfies a reverse Hölder inequality. The authors prove these operators map CMO_L into itself, extending previous boundedness results on BMO_L.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous work established boundedness of these operators on BMO_L(R^n), but their behavior on the subspace CMO_L(R^n) (functions with vanishing mean oscillation) was not understood. This is a natural and important question since CMO is the closure of compactly supported functions in BMO, and establishing that operators map CMO into itself is essential for understanding their compactness and duality properties.

Method: The authors develop new equivalent characterizations of CMO_L(R^n) and employ a refined decomposition that partitions the parameter interval at r_B·ρ(x_B) (where ρ is the critical radius function associated with the potential), instead of the customary r_B² or ρ(x_B)². This new decomposition strategy overcomes a key technical obstacle that prevented existing BMO_L methods from being adapted to the CMO_L setting.

Result: The main result establishes that the Littlewood-Paley operators s_L and S_L, as well as the semigroup maximal operator T*_L, are mappings from CMO_L(R^n) into itself.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete extension of previous boundedness results from BMO_L to the CMO_L space for these Schrödinger operator-associated operators, achieved through novel characterizations and a refined decomposition technique.

Abstract: Let $L=-Δ+V$ be a Schrödinger operator on $\mathbb{R}^n$, where $Δ$ is the Laplacian and $V$ satisfies the reverse Hölder inequality ${\rm RH}_q$ for some $q>n/2$. In this paper, we study the behavior of the Littlewood--Paley operators $s_L$ and $S_L$, as well as the semigroup maximal operator $T^*_L$, on the space ${\rm CMO}_L(\mathbb{R}^n)$ associated with the Schrödinger operator $L$. It is known from previous work that these operators are bounded on ${\rm BMO}_L(\mathbb{R}^n)$. Our main result shows that they are, in fact, mappings from ${\rm CMO}_L(\mathbb{R}^n)$ into itself. To prove this, we develop several equivalent characterizations of ${\rm CMO}_L(\mathbb{R}^n)$ and employ a refined decomposition that partitions the parameter interval at $r_Bρ(x_B)$, instead of the customary $r_B^2$ or $ρ(x_B)^2$. The new strategy allows us to overcome a key technical obstacle that arises when applying existing methods to the ${\rm CMO}_L$ setting.

</details>


### [118] [Solutions of Duhamel's formula with a sum of mappings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07642)
*Radosław Czaja,Maria Kania*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a general framework for solving semilinear evolution equations using the variation of constants formula, with smoothing linear operators and multiple nonlinearities between Banach spaces. It determines admissible initial condition spaces, analyzes solution existence, uniqueness, maximal existence time, blow-up profiles, global extendibility, and regularity, and applies the theory to the modified viscous Cahn-Hilliard equation, cell population evolution, and the semilinear Schrödinger equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified theoretical framework for semilinear evolution equations with multiple nonlinearities acting between different Banach spaces, enabling analysis of existence, uniqueness, blow-up, and regularity across diverse applications.

Method: The method uses the variation of constants formula under smoothing linear operators and finite nonlinearities among a family of Banach spaces. The framework establishes admissible initial condition spaces for existence/uniqueness, analyzes time profiles including blow-up and global extendibility, and verifies the theory on an extrapolated fractional power scale of Banach spaces.

Result: The framework successfully determines admissible spaces for initial conditions, characterizes maximal existence time, short-time and blow-up profiles, global extendibility, and regularity. The theory is validated through three applications: the modified viscous Cahn-Hilliard equation in R^N, cell population evolution with varying genotype, and the semilinear Schrödinger equation.

Conclusion: A general existence and uniqueness theory for semilinear evolution equations is established, with demonstrated applicability to a wide range of concrete PDE problems from different fields.

Abstract: Semilinear evolution equations are solved by means of the variation of constants formula under the action of smoothing linear operators and a finite number of nonlinearities operating between chosen Banach spaces of a given family. Admissible spaces of initial conditions are determined for the existence and uniqueness of a suitable notion of solution. Its maximal time of existence, short time and blow up time profiles, global extendibility and regularity are analyzed. For extrapolated fractional power scale of Banach spaces the fulfillment of the corresponding Cauchy problem is shown. Chosen applications to the modified viscous Cahn-Hilliard equation in R^N, the evolution of cell population of varying genotype and the semilinear Schrödinger equation are presented.

</details>


### [119] [Well-posedness and a sign-graph limit for an active Cahn--Hilliard equation on Riemannian manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07669)
*Darko Mitrovic,Andrej Novak,Ajla Sukurica*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a weak solution theory for a non-variational Cahn-Hilliard evolution on compact Riemannian manifolds (with possible boundary), featuring an active correction term sensitive to the sign of the Laplace-Beltrami operator and a monotone anchoring mechanism. Weak solutions are constructed in arbitrary dimensions via a Galerkin scheme with strong second-order compactness, yielding uniqueness and continuous dependence in two dimensions. A singular constitutive limit (steep classifier at fixed interface thickness) is proven, converging to a weak solution of a differential inclusion governed by the maximal monotone sign graph.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The active correction breaks the passive gradient flow structure of classical Cahn-Hilliard and introduces nonlinear dependence on second derivatives not identifiable through natural weak compactness estimates, requiring new analytical tools to develop a well-posedness and limit theory.

Method: A Galerkin approximation combined with a level-one-sided comparison argument exploiting monotonicity of the classifier and anchoring law, along with biharmonic coercivity, yields strong convergence of approximate Laplacians and second-order compactness. Estimates depend only on boundedness and monotonicity of classifiers (not slope), enabling a singular limit via maximal monotone sign graph.

Result: Weak solutions exist for square-integrable initial data in arbitrary dimensions; in two dimensions uniqueness and continuous dependence hold. Subsequential strong second-order convergence to the differential inclusion solution is obtained in arbitrary dimensions, with full convergence in 2D due to uniqueness. The limit is a constitutive steepness limit at fixed diffuse interface thickness.

Conclusion: A rigorous weak solution framework and constitutive singular limit are established for a non-variational, active Cahn-Hilliard model on manifolds, with a solution-dependent classifier steepness limit shown to converge to a unique limit (in 2D) governed by the sign graph of the Laplacian.

Abstract: We establish a weak solution theory and a singular constitutive limit for a non variational Cahn Hilliard evolution on compact Riemannian manifolds, possibly with boundary. The model combines the classical double well chemical potential with an active correction concentrated in diffuse transition layers and sensitive to the sign of the Laplace Beltrami operator, together with a monotone anchoring mechanism toward a prescribed reference state. The active correction breaks the passive gradient flow structure and introduces a nonlinear dependence on second derivatives not identified by the natural weak compactness estimates. For square integrable initial data and sufficiently integrable reference data, we construct weak solutions in arbitrary dimension. A Galerkin level one sided comparison argument, using monotonicity of the classifier and anchoring law together with biharmonic coercivity, yields strong convergence of the approximate Laplacians and hence strong second order compactness. This identifies the nonlinear active term in an ordinary weak formulation, without a second derivative defect. In two dimensions we prove uniqueness and continuous dependence. The estimates depend only on the bound and monotonicity of the classifier, not on its slope, and are therefore uniform for increasingly steep arctangent classifiers. Their singular limit is governed by the maximal monotone sign graph acting on the Laplace Beltrami operator. In arbitrary dimension we obtain subsequential strong second order convergence to a weak solution of the resulting differential inclusion. In two dimensions the limiting state is unique, so the entire steep classifier family converges; uniqueness of the constitutive multiplier on the zero Laplacian set is not asserted. This is a constitutive steepness limit at fixed diffuse interface thickness, rather than a sharp interface limit.

</details>


### [120] [The Dirichlet problem for a non-commutative elliptic operator in the ball](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07770)
*A. Moreno García,D. Alfonso Santiesteban,R. Abreu Blaya*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a necessary and sufficient condition for the solvability of the Dirichlet problem in the unit ball for a second-order system involving a Clifford-algebra valued Dirac operator. Solutions exist when boundary data are C^1, with explicit counterexamples showing failure for less regular data. Notably, solutions exhibit one order less regularity than the boundary data in the C^{k,ν} case, contrasting with the standard commutative setting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize exactly when the Dirichlet problem for the second-order Dirac-type system ∂_x̄ f ∂_x̄ = 0 in the unit ball is solvable, determining the minimal smoothness requirements on boundary data and understanding how regularity is lost in the Clifford (non-commutative) setting.

Method: The authors derive a necessary and sufficient condition for solvability, prove existence of solutions for C^1 boundary data on the sphere, and construct explicit counterexamples demonstrating failure of solvability when the smoothness assumption is weakened. They also analyze the regularity of solutions relative to boundary data in Hölder spaces C^{k,ν}.

Result: The Dirichlet problem is solvable if and only if boundary data belong to C^1(∂B). Explicit counterexamples show solvability can fail below this regularity. Solutions corresponding to boundary data in C^{k,ν} exhibit regularity one order lower than the data, a phenomenon absent in the standard commutative setting.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete characterization of solvability for this Clifford-valued Dirac system, highlighting a sharp C^1 threshold for boundary data and revealing a systematic loss of one degree of regularity in the non-commutative setting—a key departure from commutative analogues.

Abstract: In this paper we establish a necessary and sufficient condition for the solvability of the Dirichlet problem in the unit ball $\mathbb{B}$ for the second order system $\partial_{\underline{x}} f\partial_{\underline{x}}=0$, where $\partial_{\underline{x}}$ stands for the Clifford-algebra valued Dirac operator in $\mathbb{R}^m$. We prove that the problem admits a solution whenever the boundary data belong to $C^1(\partial\mathbb{B})$. Conversely, we construct explicit counterexamples showing that solvability may fail if this smoothness assumption on the boundary is not satisfied. Moreover, in contrast with the standard commutative setting, we show how that solutions exhibit a regularity one order less than the boundary data, when the latter belong to $C^{k,ν}(\partial\mathbb{B})$.

</details>


### [121] [On the effective sharp stability of the Faber-Krahn inequality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07772)
*André Guerra,João Miguel Machado,João P. G. Ramos*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves the sharp quantitative Faber-Krahn inequality with an explicit, computable dimensional constant, resolving Open Problem 2 posed by Brasco and De Philippis. Unlike prior proofs, it avoids compactness arguments and instead uses a level-set proof of the Saint-Venant inequality, where the torsional deficit controls both the average isoperimetric deficit and gradient oscillation along level-set boundaries, combined with a local strong isoperimetric estimate for confined sets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve an open problem (Brasco-De Philippis) by establishing the sharp quantitative Faber-Krahn inequality with a constant that is explicit and computable in any dimension, avoiding the non-constructive compactness/selection arguments used in previous proofs.

Method: The proof proceeds through: (1) a new level-set proof of the Saint-Venant inequality, (2) showing the torsional deficit controls the average isoperimetric deficit and the oscillation of the gradient magnitude along level-set boundaries, and (3) applying an effective local strong isoperimetric estimate for confined sets to conclude the result.

Result: A sharp quantitative Faber-Krahn inequality is established with a computable dimensional constant, using a fully constructive argument rather than indirect compactness methods.

Conclusion: The sharp quantitative Faber-Krahn inequality with an explicit constant is proven, providing a constructive resolution to the open problem while contributing new level-set techniques that link torsional, isoperimetric, and gradient oscillation quantities.

Abstract: We prove the sharp quantitative Faber--Krahn inequality with a computable dimensional constant, resolving a problem posed by Brasco and De Philippis~\cite[Open Problem~2]{brasco2017spectral}.
  In contrast with the known proof of the sharp estimate, our argument avoids indirect compactness arguments and selection principles. It runs through a new level-set proof of the Saint--Venant inequality, using that the torsional deficit controls both the average isoperimetric deficit and the oscillation of the gradient magnitude along the boundaries of level sets. An effective local strong isoperimetric estimate for confined sets then yields the result.

</details>


### [122] [Nonlinear Schrödinger systems with all attractive forces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07813)
*Jaeyoung Byeon,Junyoung Heo,Zhi-Qiang Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper systematically investigates the solution structure of nonnegative solutions for coupled nonlinear Schrödinger systems in the all attractive regime, analyzing Morse indices, kernels of linearized systems, and synchronization phenomena for positive vector solutions, with results linking the interaction matrix's spectral properties and domain geometry to the solution structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand the solution structure of coupled nonlinear Schrödinger systems in the all attractive regime, particularly concerning Morse index properties, kernel structure, and synchronization of positive vector solutions, as well as how these depend on the spectral information of the interaction matrix and domain geometry.

Method: The authors conduct a systematic analysis using variational methods, examining synchronized positive vector solutions and nonnegative semi-vector solutions for equal frequencies. They establish variational characterizations and analyze Morse indices and kernels of linearized systems. They also study synchronization phenomena through spectral analysis of the interaction matrix, using examples to demonstrate when synchronization holds or fails.

Result: They obtain Morse index results and kernel properties for synchronized positive vector and semi-vector solutions in the equal frequency case. They establish a variational characterization leading to new existence results for general systems with arbitrary frequencies. They prove that if the interaction matrix has exactly one positive eigenvalue, all positive vector solutions are synchronized, while providing counterexamples showing synchronization may fail when there are at least two positive eigenvalues.

Conclusion: The results reveal the significant influence of the spectral information of the interaction matrix and domain geometry on the solution structure of coupled nonlinear Schrödinger systems, showing precise conditions under which synchronization occurs and how the structure changes based on these properties.

Abstract: In this paper we investigate in a systematic way the solution structure of nonnegative solutions for coupled nonlinear Schrödinger systems in the all attractive regime. For all frequencies equal case we obtain results on Morse index of synchronized positive vector solutions and nonnegative semi-vector solutions as well as their kernel of linearized systems at these solutions. We establish a variational characterization of synchronized positive vector solutions and as applications we give a new existence result about positive vector solutions for the general systems with arbitrary frequencies. We also examine the synchronization phenomenon of positive vector solutions, and prove that if the interaction matrix has exactly one positive eigenvalue, any positive vector solution is synchronized. Finally we provide examples of domains for which synchronization of positive solutions fails to hold if the interaction matrix has at least two positive eigenvalues. Our results reveal the effect of the spectral information of the interaction matrix of the couplings and geometry of a domain on the solution structure.

</details>


### [123] [An improved strong comparison principle for singular $p$-Laplace equations, with applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07877)
*Phuong Le*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper removes the dimensional restriction $\frac{2N+2}{N+2}<p<2$ and proves Harnack-type inequalities, the strong maximum principle for the linearized operator, critical set description, and strong comparison principle for positive weak solutions of $-Δ_p u=f(u)$ in the singular case $1<p<2$, establishing these results for $\frac32<p<2$ independent of dimension. This improvement propagates to qualitative theory results including Gibbons' conjecture and monotonicity theorems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical results of Damascelli and Sciunzi (2006) for the singular p-Laplacian case were only valid under the restriction $\frac{2N+2}{N+2}<p<2$, a range that shrinks to the empty set as the dimension $N$ grows. This severely limits applicability in high dimensions, motivating the removal of the dimension dependence.

Method: The authors develop new techniques to extend Harnack inequalities, the strong maximum principle for the linearized operator, critical set analysis, and strong comparison principles from the restricted range to the dimension-independent range $\frac32<p<2$.

Result: All the key qualitative properties (Harnack-type inequalities, strong maximum principle for the linearized operator, critical set description, strong comparison principle) are established for $\frac32<p<2$ without dimensional restrictions. This improvement propagates to subsequent results, replacing the old assumption in Gibbons' conjecture resolution and monotonicity theorems for changing-sign nonlinearities.

Conclusion: The paper significantly extends the validity of fundamental qualitative tools for singular p-Laplace equations to the dimension-independent range $\frac32<p<2$, improving many downstream results that relied on the strong comparison principle as a black box.

Abstract: We consider positive weak solutions of $-Δ_p u=f(u)$, with $f$ positive and locally Lipschitz continuous. In the singular case $1<p<2$, the Harnack-type inequalities, the strong maximum principle for the linearized operator, the description of the critical set and the strong comparison principle established by Damascelli and Sciunzi [Calc.\ Var.\ Partial Differential Equations \textbf{25} (2006), 139--159] have been available, for more than twenty years, only under the restriction $\frac{2N+2}{N+2}<p<2$, a range that shrinks to the empty set as $N\to\infty$. We remove the dependence on the dimension and prove all of these results for $\frac32<p<2$.
  Since the strong comparison principle is used as a black box throughout the qualitative theory of $p$-Laplace equations, the improvement propagates. We show in particular that the assumption $\frac{2N+2}{N+2}<p<2$ may be replaced by $\frac32<p<2$ in the resolution of Gibbons' conjecture for $-Δ_pu=f(u)$ by Esposito, Farina, Montoro and Sciunzi [Math.\ Ann.\ \textbf{382} (2022), 943--974] and in their monotonicity theorem in half-spaces for changing-sign nonlinearities [Calc.\ Var.\ Partial Differential Equations \textbf{61} (2022), art.\ 154]; further applications are given in the body of the paper.

</details>


### [124] [The Short Wave equation on the half-line by the Unified Transform Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07881)
*Iryna Karpenko,Dmitry Shepelsky,Ilona Tylevna*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the initial-boundary value problem for the Short Wave equation on the half-line, where the boundary's character (inflow/outflow) is determined dynamically by the sign of the unknown trace u(0,t). Using the Unified Transform Method (Fokas method), the authors formulate the solution via matrix Riemann-Hilbert problems, deriving spectral functions and global relations. When u(0,t)≤0, only initial data is needed; when u(0,t)≥0, compatible boundary data is also required.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Short Wave equation on the half-line presents a distinctive challenge: the boundary cannot be classified a priori as inflow or outflow since its character depends dynamically on the sign of the unknown boundary trace u(0,t). This leads to different analyticity properties of eigenfunctions and consequently different spectral formulations depending on the regime, requiring a novel analytical treatment.

Method: The authors employ the Unified Transform Method (Fokas method) to analyze the initial-boundary value problem. They formulate the solution in terms of matrix Riemann-Hilbert problems, construct the associated spectral functions, derive global relations, and show how the solution is reconstructed. The analysis is split into two regimes based on the sign of u(0,t) at the boundary.

Result: In the regime u(0,t)≤0, the solution is fully determined by the initial data alone (assuming appropriate decay as x→∞). In the regime u(0,t)≥0, compatible boundary data are additionally required for the construction of the solution.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete spectral analysis of the Short Wave equation on the half-line with a dynamically determined boundary character, demonstrating how the Unified Transform Method can be adapted to handle problems where the boundary type is not fixed a priori but depends on the solution's trace.

Abstract: We study the initial-boundary value problem for the Short Wave equation on the half-line $x\ge 0$. A distinctive feature of this problem is that the boundary $x=0$ cannot be characterized \emph{a priori} as an inflow or outflow boundary: its character is determined dynamically by the sign of the unknown trace $u(0,t)$. This leads to different analyticity properties of the associated eigenfunctions and, consequently, to different spectral formulations in the regimes $u(0,t)\le0$ and $u(0,t)\ge0$.
  Using the Unified Transform Method (aka the Fokas method), we formulate the solution in terms of matrix Riemann--Hilbert problems. We construct the associated spectral functions, derive the global relations, and show how the solution is reconstructed from the corresponding Riemann--Hilbert problem. In the case $u(0,t)\le0$, the solution is determined by the initial data alone (assuming an appropriate decay as $x\to \infty$), whereas for $u(0,t)\ge0$, compatible boundary data are also required for the construction.

</details>


### [125] [Rotating vortex patches: rigidity, bifurcation, and unified structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07895)
*Taoufik Hmidi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This monograph provides a systematic and unified account of rotating vortex patches (V-states) for the 2D Euler equations and related active scalar models, covering both classical foundations (Rankine vortices, Kirchhoff ellipses) and recent developments, with a unified bifurcation framework based on interaction kernel structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide both an expository and structural treatment of the theory of rotating vortex patches, revisiting foundational results with detailed proofs while framing recent advancements within a common analytical framework.

Method: Develops two complementary approaches to the V-state equation using Cauchy integrals and conformal mappings, connected to potential theory and Faber polynomials, and establishes a unified bifurcation framework via complete monotonicity and spectral factorization of interaction kernels applicable to Euler, generalized SQG, quasi-geostrophic shallow-water, and related models.

Result: Presents detailed proofs and alternative formulations of classical results (Rankine vortices, Kirchhoff ellipses, rigidity results), revisits Burbea's bifurcation construction of noncircular V-states, and provides a unified bifurcation theory for rotating patches across a broad class of active scalar equations.

Conclusion: The monograph demonstrates that structural properties of the interaction kernel yield a common bifurcation framework unifying rotating patch solutions across Euler, generalized surface quasi-geostrophic, quasi-geostrophic shallow-water, and related models.

Abstract: This monograph presents a systematic account of several classical and recent developments in the theory of rotating vortex patches, or V-states, for the two-dimensional Euler equations and related active scalar models. Its purpose is both expository and structural: we revisit some of the foundational results of the subject, provide detailed proofs and alternative formulations, and present more recent results within a common analytical framework. We begin with Euler vortex patches and the contour dynamics formulation of rigidly rotating solutions. We then develop two complementary approaches to the V-state equation, based on Cauchy integrals and conformal mappings, and discuss their connections with potential theory and Faber polynomials. These tools are used to revisit classical examples, including Rankine vortices and Kirchhoff ellipses, as well as rigidity and classification results. A substantial part is devoted to Burbea construction of noncircular V-states via bifurcation theory. The final part develops a unified approach to rotating patches for a broad class of incompressible active scalar equations. Structural properties of the interaction kernel, in particular complete monotonicity and the resulting spectral factorization, yield a common bifurcation framework encompassing the Euler, generalized surface quasi-geostrophic, quasi-geostrophic shallow-water, and related models.

</details>


### [126] [Completion of DNA replication is constrained by the spatiotemporal organisation of origin firing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07924)
*Ahmad Alkhaled,Francisco Berkemeier,Michael A. Boemo,Katerina Nik*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a population-level kinetic framework extending KJMA nucleation-and-growth models to quantitatively analyze how stochastic origin firing and heterogeneous fork speeds govern the persistence and completion of DNA replication across the genome.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: DNA replication completion is a stochastic process, yet must reliably duplicate the entire genome before cell division. While biological mechanisms have been proposed to resolve this classical "random completion problem," there is a lack of quantitative understanding of how heterogeneous initiation and fork speed govern the persistence of final unreplicated regions.

Method: The authors introduce a population-level kinetic framework that extends classical KJMA (Kolmogorov-Johnson-Mehl-Avrami) nucleation-and-growth models by tracking unreplicated intervals across three dimensions: size, genomic position, and time. They establish well-posedness of the resulting mean-field system and prove global existence for compatible data. They also introduce a local initiation mass function to quantify how origin firing density and spatial organization constrain replication completion.

Result: The framework yields novel and sharp upper bounds on both the worst-locus unreplicated fraction and locuswise near-completion time, providing rigorous quantitative constraints on replication completion dynamics.

Conclusion: These results offer a rigorous and computable foundation for mapping vulnerabilities in replication completion, and for relating persistent unreplicated regions to replication stress and genome instability.

Abstract: DNA replication requires the coordination of origin firing and fork progression to ensure the entire genome is timely duplicated before cell division. Yet origin firing is stochastic, giving rise to the classical random completion problem of how probabilistic local events can nevertheless ensure reliable genome duplication. Although several biological mechanisms have been proposed to resolve this problem, a quantitative account of how heterogeneous initiation and fork speed govern the persistence of the final unreplicated regions is still lacking. To address this gap, we introduce a population-level kinetic framework that extends KJMA nucleation-and-growth models by tracking unreplicated intervals over size, genomic position and time. We establish well-posedness of the resulting mean-field system and global existence for compatible data. Notably, by introducing a local initiation mass function, we quantify how the density and spatial organisation of origin firing constrain replication completion, yielding novel and sharp upper bounds on both the worst-locus unreplicated fraction and locuswise near-completion time. These results provide a rigorous and computable foundation for mapping vulnerabilities in replication completion and relating persistent unreplicated regions to replication stress and genome instability.

</details>


### [127] [A counterexample to Kenig's conjecture for the Laplace double-layer operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07947)
*Matthew J. Colbrook,Siavash Sadeghi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper disproves Kenig's 1994 spectral-radius conjecture by constructing a bounded simply connected planar Lipschitz domain whose double-layer operator has essential spectral radius strictly greater than 1/2 on L² arclength spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Layer potentials are a classical tool for boundary value problems on Lipschitz domains. Kenig's 1994 conjecture claimed that the double-layer operator's spectral radius would guarantee operator-norm convergence of Neumann series on mean-zero L² densities for connected boundaries. Disproving or proving this conjecture is fundamental to understanding convergence properties of iterative methods for such boundary integral equations.

Method: The construction starts from smooth graphs whose shapes repeat under translation. In the separated-scales limit, refinement makes adjoint resolvent equation solutions grow with fixed forcing while keeping graph slopes uniformly bounded. A computer-assisted certificate proves this growth via an inequality for Hermitian 2×2 matrices, with strict margins persisting at nearby spectral parameters. Normalization and Floquet transformation give compactly supported densities with small residuals. Rescaled segments of successive graphs are inserted into one bounded boundary, forming a weakly null sequence of approximate eigenvectors. The certificate combines continuous estimates, exact rational arithmetic, and rigorous interval enclosures.

Result: For every t>1/2 sufficiently close to 1/2, a bounded simply connected planar Lipschitz domain is constructed with ±it in the Fredholm essential spectrum of its double-layer operator, proving essential spectral radius strictly greater than 1/2. The same conclusion holds on a single periodic Lipschitz graph.

Conclusion: Kenig's 1994 spectral-radius conjecture is disproved. The double-layer operator on Lipschitz domains can have essential spectral radius exceeding 1/2 even on connected boundaries, which has implications for the convergence guarantees of Neumann series in boundary integral equation methods.

Abstract: Layer potentials provide a classical approach to boundary value problems for Laplace's equation on Lipschitz domains. Kenig's 1994 spectral-radius conjecture for the double-layer operator would ensure operator-norm convergence of the associated Neumann series on mean-zero $L^2$ densities when the boundary is connected. We disprove this conjecture by constructing a bounded simply connected planar Lipschitz domain whose double-layer operator on arclength $L^2$ has essential spectral radius strictly greater than $1/2$. More precisely, for every $t>1/2$ sufficiently close to $1/2$, we obtain such a domain with $\pm i t$ in its Fredholm essential spectrum. The construction starts from smooth graphs whose shapes repeat under translation. In the limit of separated scales, refinement makes solutions of adjoint resolvent equations grow with fixed forcing. The graph slopes remain uniformly bounded. A computer-assisted certificate proves this growth through an inequality for Hermitian $2\times2$ matrices. Its strict margin at $- i/2$ persists at nearby spectral parameters. Normalisation and a Floquet transform then give compactly supported densities with small residuals on the full graphs. We insert rescaled segments of successive graphs into one bounded boundary, where these densities form a weakly null sequence of approximate eigenvectors. The same spectral conclusion holds on a single periodic Lipschitz graph. The certificate combines continuous estimates, exact rational arithmetic and rigorous interval enclosures.

</details>


### [128] [Solvability of Semilinear Elliptic Equations on Infinite Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07994)
*Bobo Hua,Yong Lin,Haohang Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a constructive method for solving semilinear elliptic equations Δu(x)=f(x,u(x)) on locally finite, connected infinite graphs with layered structure, using Eidelheit's theorem to establish coupling criteria that guarantee arbitrary initial-layer data extend to global solutions. They apply combinatorial criteria to prove solvability on various infinite graphs including leafless infinite trees, integer/hexagonal/triangular lattices, and Cayley graphs of semidirect products, and extend the approach to bi-Laplacians, p-Laplacians, and magnetic Laplacians.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish general solvability criteria for semilinear elliptic equations on infinite graphs with layered structure, where traditional methods may fail, and to understand structural features of graphs that guarantee existence of solutions.

Method: The authors use Eidelheit's theorem (an infinite-dimensional Hahn-Banach type theorem) to derive coupling criteria ensuring that solutions on arbitrary initial layers extend to global solutions on the entire graph. They then employ combinatorial/graph-theoretic criteria to verify these coupling conditions on specific graph classes.

Result: Solvability is proven on leafless infinite trees, integer lattices (Z²), triangular and hexagonal lattices, and Cayley graphs of the discrete Heisenberg group, plus a broad class of Cayley graphs of semidirect products G≅Z⋉θH. Notably, Δu=e^u has infinitely many solutions on Z² but none of finite energy. The method extends to bi-Laplacians (two-step coupling), p-Laplacians (unique-neighbor condition), and magnetic Laplacians.

Conclusion: A general constructive framework based on Eidelheit's theorem yields broad solvability results for semilinear elliptic equations on infinite layered graphs, with applications across diverse graph families and generalizations to several nonlinear operators.

Abstract: We develop a constructive method for solving semilinear elliptic equations $Δu(x)=f(x,u(x))$ on locally finite, connected infinite graphs with layered structure. Using Eidelheit's theorem, we establish coupling criteria ensuring that arbitrary initial-layer data extend to global solutions for every $f$. We apply combinatorial criteria to prove solvability on leafless infinite trees, integer lattices, the triangular and hexagonal lattices, and a Cayley graph of the discrete Heisenberg group. We further establish solvability for a broad class of Cayley graphs of semidirect products $G\cong\mathbb Z\ltimes_θH$. In particular, $Δu=e^u$ has infinitely many solutions on $\mathbb Z^2$, but none of finite energy. We also extend the method to the bi-Laplacian under two-step coupling conditions, to the $p$-Laplacian under a unique-neighbor condition, and to magnetic Laplacians.

</details>


### [129] [Construction of three-solitons with logarithmic distance for the mass-critical gKdV equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08085)
*Yang Lan,Xu Yuan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves the existence of three-soliton solutions with logarithmic relative distances and specific sign choices (+, - , -) for the mass-critical generalized Korteweg-de Vries equation, showing that strong nonlinear interactions between solitons drive the dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the existence of multi-soliton solutions for the mass-critical gKdV equation where solitons interact strongly, going beyond the well-understood regime of asymptotically decoupled solitons.

Method: The proof relies on reducing the existence problem to the solvability of an ODE system generated by nonlinear interactions between three solitons and non-localized profiles, with specific choices of soliton number (three) and signs (+, -, -) determined by this ODE solvability.

Result: Existence of three-soliton solutions is proven with logarithmic relative distance between solitons, where the leading-order dynamics of each soliton is perturbed by the presence of the others due to strong interactions.

Conclusion: The special three-soliton behavior arises from strong soliton interactions in the mass-critical setting, and the number of solitons and their sign configuration are dictated by the underlying ODE system's solvability conditions.
