Abstract: For the mass-critical generalized Korteweg-de Vries equation, \begin{equation*}
  \partial_{t}u+\partial_{x}\left(
  \partial_{x}^{2}u+u^{5}\right)=0,\quad (t,x)\in [0,\infty)\times \mathbb{R}, \end{equation*} we prove the existence of three-soliton solutions with logarithmic relative distance and with the choice of signs $(+,-,-)$. The choice of the number three and the signs of solitons are related to the solvability of the ODE system generated by the nonlinear interactions between the three solitons and some non-localized profiles. In particular, these special behaviors are due to strong interactions between the three solitons. That is, the dynamics of each soliton is perturbed at leading order by the presence of other solitons.

</details>


### [130] [Global mild solutions and the semiclassical limit for the Fermi--Dirac BGK model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08142)
*Young-Pil Choi,Byung-Hoon Hwang,Sihyun Song*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global existence of mild solutions to the spatially inhomogeneous Fermi-Dirac BGK equation for arbitrary-size Pauli-admissible initial data with finite mass and kinetic energy, including vacuum and locally saturated zero-temperature states. It also establishes an entropy-gap H-theorem and a quantum-to-classical convergence result under appropriate assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a global well-posedness theory for the spatially inhomogeneous Fermi-Dirac BGK equation that handles arbitrary-size initial data, including challenging degenerate states at the saturation boundary where the Pauli exclusion principle becomes active. This extends prior results that were limited to small data or avoid saturation regimes.

Method: The construction uses a moment-compatible regularization of the local equilibrium that preserves the Pauli bound and maintains uniform moment control while remaining consistent up to the saturation boundary. This regularization is the key tool enabling global mild solution construction for arbitrary admissible data.

Result: Global mild solutions exist with mass, momentum, and energy conservation. Under an additional finite spatial second-moment assumption, an entropy-gap H-theorem holds on the full Pauli interval. Under uniform moment and entropy bounds, quantum mild solutions converge strongly in phase space, uniformly on bounded time intervals, to a mild solution of the classical BGK equation.

Conclusion: The Fermi-Dirac BGK equation admits global mild solutions for arbitrary-size Pauli-admissible data (including saturation states), satisfies conservation laws and entropy dissipation, and exhibits a rigorous quantum-to-classical limit recovery of the classical BGK equation.

Abstract: We prove global existence of mild solutions to the spatially inhomogeneous Fermi--Dirac BGK equation for arbitrary-size Pauli-admissible initial data with finite mass and kinetic energy, allowing both vacuum and locally saturated zero-temperature states. The construction relies on a moment-compatible regularization of the local equilibrium that preserves the Pauli bound and uniform moment control while remaining consistent up to the saturation boundary. The solutions satisfy mass, momentum, and energy conservation and, under an additional finite spatial second-moment assumption, an entropy-gap H-theorem on the full Pauli interval. Under uniform moment and entropy bounds, we further prove that, after extraction of a subsequence, quantum mild solutions converge strongly in phase space, uniformly on bounded time intervals, to a mild solution of the classical BGK equation.

</details>


### [131] [Asymmetric Periodic Water Waves in Finite Depth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08150)
*Yuchen Wang,Shusen Yan,Maolin Zhou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: First construction of asymmetric periodic solutions to the full 2D finite-depth water-wave problem for both pure-gravity and capillary-gravity cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Asymmetric periodic water-wave solutions have not been constructed before for the full finite-depth problem, despite interest in non-symmetric wave patterns.

Method: The paper constructs families of asymmetric periodic solutions via some analytical/numerical construction method (details not fully specified in abstract).

Result: Families of asymmetric periodic solutions are obtained for the first time in both pure-gravity and capillary-gravity settings.

Conclusion: Asymmetric periodic waves exist in the full two-dimensional finite-depth water-wave problem, extending the solution landscape beyond symmetric waves.

Abstract: In this paper, we construct, for the first time, families of asymmetric periodic solutions to the full two-dimensional finite-depth water-wave problem, in both the pure-gravity and capillary--gravity cases.

</details>


### [132] [Two global stability theorems for small-data $3D$ compressible Euler solutions: Unique maximal globally hyperbolic developments with gradient-blowup & smooth global existence in the entire spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08158)
*Leonardo Abbrescia,Jared Speck,Dongxiao Yu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves two complementary small-data stability theorems for the isentropic, spherically symmetric 3D compressible Euler equations with any equation of state having positive sound speed. The first theorem establishes existence, uniqueness, and stability of the maximal globally hyperbolic development (MGHD) for shock-forming solutions, marking the first such result for any multi-D quasilinear wave system. The second theorem proves global existence in both future and past for data with opposite-sign density tails, where the sound cones bend logarithmically, providing the first global existence result for a 3D quasilinear wave system violating both the null and weak null conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The fundamental questions of global existence versus shock formation for the compressible Euler equations depend critically on the structure of initial data. Prior work could only establish local portions of candidate MGHDs for shock-forming solutions, and no local analysis can determine whether an MGHD exists or is unique. For the global existence side, no prior result handled a 3D quasilinear wave system that violates both the null and weak null conditions.

Method: The paper analyzes spherically symmetric perturbations of trivial data (vanishing velocity, constant positive density) with finite kinetic energy, where the density differs from a positive constant by a signed asymptotically flat 1/r tail. Two regimes are studied based on the sign of this tail. For shock-formation (one sign), they describe the MGHD boundary structure with gradient-singularity hypersurfaces extending to spatial infinity. For the opposite sign, they show the 1/r tail produces a global rarefaction effect that logarithmically bends sound cones away from Minkowski ones, yielding logarithmically enhanced dispersion that stabilizes solutions.

Result: The paper achieves: (1) First MGHD existence-uniqueness-stability result for shock-forming solutions to any multi-D quasilinear wave system, with complete description of the MGHD boundary containing gradient-singularity hypersurfaces. (2) First small-data global existence result in the entire spacetime for any 3D quasilinear wave system that violates both the null and weak null conditions, with nonlinearly distorted asymptotics toward null infinity due to logarithmic sound-cone bending.

Conclusion: The sign of the 1/r density tail completely determines the fate of solutions: one sign leads to shock formation with a well-defined MGHD, while the opposite sign yields global existence for all time driven by a rarefaction mechanism with logarithmically enhanced dispersion. The results are not restricted to the Chaplygin gas (aside from the shock-formation theorem's exception).

Abstract: We prove a pair of complementary small-data stability theorems describing the global future and past structure of classical solutions to the isentropic, spherically symmetric $3D$ compressible Euler equations. We allow any equation of state with positive sound speed, except that our shock-formation results do not apply to the Chaplygin gas.
  Our first theorem concerns open sets of smooth Cauchy data that are perturbations of trivial data with vanishing velocity and constant positive density. The perturbed data have finite kinetic energy, and the perturbed density is equal to a positive constant plus a signed ``asymptotically flat'' tail. Our main theorem yields a complete description of the existence and uniqueness of the maximal globally hyperbolic development (MGHD) of the data. The boundary of the MGHD contains hypersurfaces that extend to spatial infinity on which our solutions develop gradient-singularities. This is the first MGHD existence-uniqueness-stability result for shock-forming solutions for any multi-$D$ quasilinear wave system. While prior works have yielded local portions of ``candidate'' MGHDs, neither the existence nor the uniqueness of an MGHD can be inferred from local considerations.
  Our second theorem considers analogous data with the opposite sign of the $1/r$ density tail. We prove global existence throughout spacetime, to both the future and past. The solution's asymptotic behavior towards null infinity is not linear, but rather is distorted by a logarithmic bending of the sound cones away from the flat Minkowski ones, leading to logarithmically enhanced dispersion. This is the first small-data global existence result in the entire spacetime for any $3D$ quasilinear wave system that fails to satisfy the null condition and the weak null condition. The main mechanism of stabilization is a global-in-spacetime rarefaction effect, tied to the $1/r$ tail.

</details>


### [133] [Fefferman--Stein type inequalities via area and maximal functions for Schrödinger operators with applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08193)
*Ji Li,Wanjun Li,Liang Song,Lixin Yan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves a Fefferman-Stein inequality involving area functions and non-tangential maximal functions for the Schrödinger operator L = -Δ+V on stratified Lie groups, extends it to tensor products of two such groups, develops atomic decompositions in Orlicz spaces L log⁺L, and establishes weak-type endpoint estimates for area integrals and Riesz transforms, generalizing the classical Fefferman-Stein result to singular integrals with non-smooth kernels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the classical Fefferman-Stein theory (originally for the Laplacian and smooth kernels) to Schrödinger operators with non-smooth potentials on stratified Lie groups and product settings, thereby broadening the applicability of endpoint estimates to singular integrals with non-smooth kernels.

Method: The authors first establish a Fefferman-Stein inequality relating area functions and non-tangential maximal functions for the Schrödinger operator on stratified Lie groups. They then extend this to tensor product domains G₁ × G₂ and construct atomic decompositions in the Orlicz space L log⁺L. These decompositions are used to prove weak-type endpoint estimates for the area integral operator and Riesz transforms.

Result: A Fefferman-Stein inequality is established for Schrödinger operators on stratified Lie groups and their tensor products. Atomic decompositions for L log⁺L are obtained, leading to weak-type (L log⁺L → weak-L¹) endpoint estimates for area integral operators and Riesz transforms associated with the Schrödinger operator.

Conclusion: The celebrated Fefferman-Stein result is successfully extended to the Schrödinger operator setting with non-smooth kernels on stratified Lie groups and product spaces, providing new weak-type endpoint estimates.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish a Fefferman--Stein inequality in terms of area function and non-tangential maximal function associated with the Schrödinger operator $\mathcal{L} = -Δ+ V$ on stratified Lie groups $\mathcal G$, where $Δ$ denotes the sub-Laplacian on $\mathcal G$ and $V$ is a nonnegative locally integrable function. As an application, we extend this inequality to the tensor product $\mathcal G_1 \times \mathcal G_2$ of two stratified Lie groups and develop atomic decompositions associated with the Schrödinger operator for functions in the Orlicz space $L\log^{+}L(\mathcal G_1 \times \mathcal G_2).$ Using these atomic decompositions, we further prove weak-type endpoint estimates for the area integral operator and the Riesz transforms associated with the Schrödinger operator on $L\log^{+}L(\mathcal G_1 \times \mathcal G_2),$ thereby extending the celebrated result of R.\,Fefferman and E.M.\,Stein \cite{FSt1982} to the setting of singular integrals with non-smooth kernels.

</details>


### [134] [On the singular limit of the Camassa-Holm equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08227)
*Billel Guelmame*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the singular limit of the Camassa-Holm equation as the length scale ℓ → 0. Despite the formal limit being the Burgers equation with flux 3u²/2, the authors prove that convergence to entropy solutions fails after shock formation, even along subsequences in local space-time L¹.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the Camassa-Holm equation, in the singular limit ℓ → 0, converges to the expected formal limit (Burgers equation), particularly after shock formation where dissipative effects are critical.

Method: The authors investigate the singular limit analytically, examining a class of smooth initial data and testing convergence to entropy solutions of the limiting Burgers equation via local space-time L¹ convergence (including subsequential convergence).

Result: The paper shows that convergence to entropy solutions of the Burgers equation fails after shock formation, even when allowing subsequences and weak local space-time L¹ convergence.

Conclusion: The formal limit of the Camassa-Holm equation as ℓ → 0 does not yield the expected entropy solutions after shock formation, revealing a fundamental breakdown of the singular limiting process.

Abstract: We study the singular limit of the Camassa--Holm equation as the length scale $\ell$ tends to zero. Although the formal limit is the Burgers equation with flux $3u^2/2$, we show that, for a class of smooth initial data, convergence to entropy solutions fails after the shock formation, even along subsequences in local space-time $L^1$.

</details>


### [135] [Necessary and Sufficient Condition of Existence for the Quadrature Surfaces Free Boundary Problem in Riemannian Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08245)
*Mohammed Barkatou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper generalizes Barkatou's Euclidean quadrature surface result to compact Riemannian manifolds, proving that the quadrature surface free boundary problem QS(f,k) admits a solution strictly containing the convex hull C of the support of f if and only if an integral condition involving f and the perimeter of C holds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to extend the classical Euclidean result on quadrature surfaces by Barkatou et al. (2005) to the more general geometric setting of compact Riemannian manifolds, following the variational and shape optimization framework developed by Djité and Seck.

Method: The paper formulates the quadrature surface problem as a shape optimization problem on a compact Riemannian manifold, employing the Riemannian RC-GNP condition and stability results from prior work (Djité & Seck 2026) to analyze existence of solutions strictly containing the totally convex hull of the support of the density function f.

Result: A sharp necessary and sufficient condition is established: the problem QS(f,k) admits a solution strictly containing the totally convex hull C of the support of f if and only if ∫_C f(x)dv(g) > k|∂C|_g, where the right-hand side involves the Riemannian perimeter of C. Explicit verifiable examples are provided on the round sphere.

Conclusion: This work complements the earlier papers by Djité and Seck by establishing a sharp necessary and sufficient existence condition for quadrature surface problems on Riemannian manifolds, in the spirit of the Euclidean result of Barkatou, thereby broadening the applicability of quadrature surface theory to curved geometries.

Abstract: We generalize to the setting of compact Riemannian manifolds a recent result of Barkatou on quadrature surfaces. Following the geometric and variational framework recently developed by Djité and Seck, we formulate the quadrature surface free boundary problem as a shape optimization problem on a compact Riemannian manifold. Using the Riemannian $RC$-GNP condition introduced in \cite{DjiteSeck2026} and the stability results established therein, we prove that the quadrature surface problem $QS(f,k)$ admits a solution strictly containing the totally convex hull $C$ of the support of $f$ if and only if the following integral condition holds: \[ \int_C f(x)\,dv(g) > k |\partial C|_g, \] where $dv(g)$ is the Riemannian volume element and $|\partial C|_g$ is the perimeter of $C$ with respect to the metric $g$. This work extends the results of Barkatou et al. (2005) by replacing the Euclidean space with a Riemannian manifold. We also provide explicit examples on the round sphere where the condition can be verified explicitly. This paper complements the recent works \cite{DjiteSeck2026} and \cite{DjiteSeck2026b} by establishing a sharp necessary and sufficient condition in the spirit of the Euclidean result of Barkatou.

</details>


### [136] [Existence and strong-field asymptotics of skyrmions in a fourth-order model of frustrated ferromagnets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08255)
*Xinye Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a fourth-order variational model for 2D frustrated ferromagnets under an applied magnetic field H>0. It proves existence and compactness of minimizers in topological classes Q=±1 for all H>1/4, shows H=1/4 is the sharp spectral threshold, and characterizes the strong-field limiting behavior of rescaled minimizers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The key challenge is that spectral coercivity degenerates as H↓1/4, making it difficult to guarantee compactness of minimizing sequences. The authors aim to establish threshold-stable compactness throughout the entire coercive regime despite this degeneracy.

Method: The authors use a Helmholtz circle-mean identity to prove that residual energy of nonzero-degree configurations has a uniform positive lower bound even at the degenerate endpoint. Combined with a sphere-valued H²-splitting construction, this yields a threshold-stable binding inequality and compactness results.

Result: For every H>1/4, energy admits minimizers in topological classes Q=±1 and minimizing sequences are precompact in H² modulo translations. H=1/4 is identified as the sharp spectral threshold: below it energy is unbounded from below; at the threshold, nonzero-degree configurations retain positive energy barrier while degree-zero Weyl sequences lose compactness. In the strong-field regime, rescaled minimizers approach Dirichlet-energy-maximizing minimizers of the limiting functional, with topological-charge and energy measures concentrating on the H^{-1/4} scale.

Conclusion: The paper resolves the compactness question across the full coercive regime for frustrated ferromagnet models with applied field, establishes the sharp nature of the spectral threshold at H=1/4, and provides a complete asymptotic characterization of minimizers in the strong-field limit.

Abstract: We study a fourth-order variational model for two-dimensional frustrated ferromagnets with competing exchange interactions and an applied magnetic field of strength $H>0$. For every $H>1/4$, we prove that the energy admits minimizers in the topological classes $Q=\pm1$ and that every minimizing sequence is precompact in $H^2$ modulo translations. The main difficulty is that spectral coercivity degenerates as $H\downarrow1/4$. Using a Helmholtz circle-mean identity, we prove that the residual energy of every nonzero-degree configuration has a uniform positive lower bound, even at the degenerate endpoint. Together with a sphere-valued $H^2$-splitting construction, this provides a threshold-stable binding inequality and yields compactness throughout the coercive regime. We also identify $H=1/4$ as the sharp spectral threshold. Below it the energy is unbounded from below, whereas at the threshold nonzero-degree configurations retain a positive energy barrier and degree-zero Weyl sequences lose compactness. Finally, in the strong-field regime, rescaled minimizers approach those minimizers of the limiting functional that maximize the Dirichlet energy, while topological-charge and normalized-energy measures concentrate on the $H^{-1/4}$-scale.

</details>


### [137] [Necessary and Sufficient Condition of Existence for the $p$-Laplacian Quadrature Surfaces Free Boundary Problem in Riemannian Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08260)
*Mohammed Barkatou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the existence conditions for quadrature surface free boundary problems to the p-Laplacian operator on compact Riemannian manifolds, proving a necessary and sufficient condition involving an integral inequality over the convex hull of the data's support.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the previously known results for the quadrature surface problem from the Euclidean p=2 case (Barkatou) and the Riemannian linear case (Djité and Seck) to the more general nonlinear p-Laplacian setting in Riemannian geometry.

Method: The paper employs a shape optimization approach on compact Riemannian manifolds, reformulating the p-Laplacian quadrature surface problem as a shape optimization problem. It leverages the Riemannian RC-GNP condition, stability results, and the p-Laplacian shape derivative formula.

Result: The paper proves that the p-Laplacian quadrature surface problem QS_p(f,k) admits a solution strictly containing the totally convex hull C of supp(f) if and only if ∫_C f(x) dv(g) > k^(p-1) |∂C|_g, for 1 < p < ∞, under suitable regularity and stability assumptions. Explicit examples on the round sphere are provided, including radial p-harmonic solutions and boundary flux computations.

Conclusion: The work successfully unifies and extends prior Euclidean and Riemannian results to the nonlinear p-Laplacian framework, providing both theoretical existence conditions and concrete worked examples on the round sphere.

Abstract: We generalize the necessary and sufficient condition of existence for the quadrature surfaces free boundary problem to the case of the $p$-Laplacian operator in Riemannian geometry. Following the shape optimization approach of Barkatou for the Euclidean case and the Riemannian framework recently developed by Djité and Seck, we formulate the $p$-Laplacian quadrature surface problem as a shape optimization problem on a compact Riemannian manifold. Using the Riemannian $RC$-GNP condition, the stability results, and the $p$-Laplacian shape derivative formula from \cite{DjiteSeck2026b}, we prove that the $p$-Laplacian quadrature surface problem $QS_p(f,k)$ admits a solution strictly containing the totally convex hull $C$ of the support of $f$ if and only if \[ \int_C f(x)\,dv(g) > k^{p-1} |\partial C|_g, \] for $1 < p < \infty$, under suitable regularity and stability assumptions. This extends the Euclidean result of Barkatou for $p=2$ and the Riemannian result of Djité and Seck to the nonlinear $p$-Laplacian setting. Explicit examples on the round sphere are provided, including the computation of the radial $p$-harmonic solution and the boundary flux.

</details>


### [138] [Interior $W^{2,p}$ regularity for the sigma-$2$ equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08297)
*Yasheng Lyu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves regularity results for continuous 2-convex solutions of the σ₂ equation with density bounded above and below by positive constants, showing they belong to W^{2,1}_{loc} in all dimensions, and with continuous density, to W^{2,p}_{loc} for all 1<p<∞.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish gradient/regularity estimates for solutions of the σ₂ equation (a fully nonlinear Hessian-type PDE) under fairly weak regularity assumptions on the solutions themselves and the density term.

Method: The paper uses analytic techniques for Hessian-type equations to derive second-order Sobolev regularity for continuous 2-convex solutions, likely relying on a priori estimates, approximation arguments, and stability properties of the σ₂ operator.

Result: Continuous 2-convex solutions belong to W^{2,1}_{loc} when the density is bounded above and below by positive constants. If the density is additionally continuous, solutions belong to W^{2,p}_{loc} for every 1<p<∞, in all dimensions.

Conclusion: The results improve or complement existing regularity theory for Hessian equations, showing local W^{2,p} integrability from merely continuous convex-type (2-convex) solutions without assuming higher initial smoothness.

Abstract: We prove that continuous $2$-convex solutions of the $σ_{2}$ equation with a density bounded above and below by positive constants belong to $W^{2,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ in every dimension. If, in addition, the density is continuous, the solutions belong to $W^{2,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ for every $1<p<\infty$.

</details>


### [139] [Second-order regularity for weighted widely degenerate problems with explicit $u$-dependence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08471)
*Miriam Piccirillo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a higher differentiability result for local weak solutions of widely degenerate elliptic PDEs with a double-weight structure, where the left-hand side carries a weight |x|^β and a degenerate part |Du|-1, while the right-hand side depends on the solution u itself with a mismatch in weight |x|^α.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study extends previous work on widely degenerate elliptic equations by introducing a right-hand side that explicitly depends on the solution u and considering a mismatch between the weights on the left and right sides, which is a new and more challenging scenario.

Method: The authors consider local weak solutions of a widely degenerate elliptic PDE of p-Laplacian type with double weights. They prove a higher differentiability result for the composition of the gradient with a suitable function that vanishes in the unit ball for the gradient, under appropriate assumptions on the datum.

Result: A higher differentiability regularity result is established for the composition of the gradient with a truncation-type function, given suitable assumptions on the data and the weight mismatch between the two sides of the equation.

Conclusion: The paper advances the regularity theory for widely degenerate elliptic equations with weighted structure and solution-dependent right-hand sides, addressing a previously unstudied configuration with mismatched weights.

Abstract: We consider local weak solutions of widely degenerate elliptic PDEs of the type \begin{equation}
  \label{equazione mia}
  \mathrm{div}\Biggl(|x|^β(|Du|-1)^{p-1}_+\frac{Du}{|Du|}\Biggr)=\frac{|u|^{q-2}u}{|x|^α} \ \ \text{ in }Ω,
  \end{equation} where $2\leq p<n$, $α,β>0$ are fixed exponents, $Ω$ is an open subset of $\mathbb{R}^n,$ that contains the origin, $n>2,$ and $( \ \cdot \ )_+$ stands for the positive part. We establish a higher differentiability result for the composition of the gradient with a suitable function that vanishes in the unit ball for the gradient, under appropriate assumptions on the datum. The novelty here with respect to previous papers on the subject is that the right-hand side explicitly depends on the solution $u$ and we have a mismatch between the weight on the left-hand side and the right-hand side.

</details>


### [140] [Dimension free weak-type endpoint estimates for the vectors of the Dunkl--Riesz transform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08492)
*Suman Mukherjee*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a Dunkl analogue of the obstacle/partial-balayage method for the fractional Laplacian, yielding a decomposition of functions driven by the Dunkl fractional Laplacian. This is applied to prove weak-type (1,1) estimates for vector Dunkl-Riesz and Dunkl-Schrödinger Riesz transforms with explicit constants depending on the multiplicity function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the obstacle/partial-balayage method of Ouyang, Spector, and Stockdale from the Euclidean fractional Laplacian to the Dunkl setting, and to use this framework to obtain quantitative weak-type (1,1) bounds for Dunkl-Riesz type operators, which are central objects in Dunkl harmonic analysis.

Method: The authors develop a Dunkl analogue of the obstacle/partial-balayage method. For a nonnegative function f in appropriate L^p spaces of the Dunkl measure, they construct a decomposition f = μ + (-Δ_k)^{s/2} u with 0 ≤ μ ≤ λ and support properties on the coincidence set {u>0}, yielding an explicit measure bound on the coincidence set. This decomposition is then used to analyze the behavior of the Dunkl gradient in relation to the Riesz potential, leading to weak-type estimates.

Result: The paper proves that the vector Dunkl-Riesz transform R_k is of weak type (1,1) with constant at most (M_k + 2), where M_k counts the positive roots with nonzero multiplicity. For G-invariant functions, the reflection terms vanish, yielding the universal constant 2. Additionally, a dimension-free weak-type (1,1) estimate is established for the Dunkl-Schrödinger Riesz transform.

Conclusion: The obstacle/partial-balayage method is successfully adapted to the Dunkl framework, providing new quantitative weak-type estimates for Dunkl-Riesz type transforms with explicit constants that depend on the multiplicity structure (number of active roots) of the underlying reflection group.

Abstract: Let $R\subset\mathbb{R}^d$ be a reduced root system, $G$ the associated finite reflection group, and $k\ge0$ a $G$-invariant multiplicity function. We develop a Dunkl analogue of the obstacle/partial-balayage method of Ouyang, Spector, and Stockdale (https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18068) for the Euclidean fractional Laplacian. For $0<s<2$, nonnegative $f\in L^1(ν_k)\cap L^2(ν_k)$, and $λ>0$, we obtain a decomposition \[ f=μ+(-Δ_k)^{s/2}u, \qquad 0\leμ\leλ, \] with $μ=λ$ on $Ω=\{u>0\}$ and \[ λν_k(Ω)\le\|f\|_{L^1(ν_k)}. \] As an application, we prove that the vector Dunkl--Riesz transform $\mathcal R_k=\nabla_k(-Δ_k)^{-1/2}$ is of weak type $(1,1)$ with constant at most $(M_k+2)$, where \[ M_k=\#\{α\in R_+:k(α)>0\}. \] For $G$-invariant functions, the reflection terms vanish and the same argument gives the universal constant $2$. We further establish a dimension-free weak-type $(1,1)$ estimate for the Dunkl--Schrödinger Riesz transform.

</details>


### [141] [Critical fractional $p$-Hardy Sobolev equations: Global compactness and multiplicity of positive solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08510)
*Nirjan Biswas,Souptik Chakraborty,Debangana Mukherjee*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the critical fractional p-Hardy-Sobolev equation with a nontrivial nonnegative functional f. The authors establish global compactness results for Palais-Smale sequences, characterizing loss of compactness via dilations of solutions of the Hardy-Sobolev limit problem (when α>0) and via both Hardy and pure Sobolev profiles (when α=0). They apply these results to obtain two distinct positive solutions under an explicit smallness assumption on f, using a minimax argument based on hidden convexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to understand the compactness properties of the fractional p-Hardy-Sobolev equation with a nontrivial source term f, which is critical in nature and thus suffers from lack of compactness. Establishing global compactness and multiplicity of positive solutions is challenging and requires careful analysis of concentration profiles, especially distinguishing the structural differences between the cases α>0 and α=0.

Method: The paper first establishes global compactness theorems for Palais-Smale sequences by performing a center-scale analysis of concentration regimes. For α>0, compactness loss is described by dilations of solutions to the Hardy-Sobolev limit problem. For α=0, a different structure emerges where pure Sobolev profiles may appear in addition to Hardy profiles when the concentration center escapes from the Hardy singularity. The authors then construct a nonlinear path exploiting hidden convexity to keep energy below the first bubbling threshold, and use a minimax argument combined with the global compactness theorem to produce two distinct positive solutions under an explicit smallness condition on f.

Result: The paper obtains an explicit smallness assumption on f under which a positive solution with negative energy exists. It further constructs a second distinct positive solution via a minimax argument, where the nonlinear path based on hidden convexity maintains energy strictly below the first bubbling threshold, ensuring the validity of the compactness argument.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete global compactness analysis for the critical fractional p-Hardy-Sobolev equation, revealing structurally different concentration phenomena for α>0 versus α=0, and demonstrates the applicability of these results by proving the existence of two distinct positive solutions under smallness conditions on the forcing term f.

Abstract: We study the critical fractional $p$-Hardy-Sobolev equation
  \begin{equation}\tag{$\mathcal{P}$}\label{a-main}
  (-Δ_p)^s u -μ\dfrac{|u|^{p-2}u}{|x|^{sp}}=\dfrac{|u|^{p^*_s(α)-2}u}{|x|^α}+f \;\mbox{ in }\,\mathbb{R}^d, \quad u\in \mathcal{D}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^d),
  \end{equation}
  where $1<p<\infty$, $0<s<1$, $0\leqα<sp<d$, $μ>0$, $p^*_s(α):= p(d-α)/(d-sp)$ is the critical Hardy-Sobolev exponent, and $f$ is a nontrivial nonnegative functional in $(\mathcal{D}^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^d))^*$. We first establish global compactness results for Palais-Smale sequences associated with the corresponding energy functional. When $α>0$, the loss of compactness is described by dilations of solutions of the Hardy-Sobolev limit problem. The case $α=0$ has a different structure: in addition to Hardy profiles, pure Sobolev profiles may occur when the centre of concentration escapes from the Hardy singularity relative to its scale. We give a direct centre-scale analysis of these two concentration regimes and obtain the corresponding energy decomposition and profile separation. As an application, under an explicit smallness assumption on $f$, we first obtain a positive solution for \eqref{a-main} with negative energy. We then construct a nonlinear path based on hidden convexity whose energy remains strictly below the first bubbling threshold. A minimax argument, combined with the global compactness theorem, then yields a second distinct positive solution for \eqref{a-main}.

</details>


### [142] [Global large solutions of the Cauchy problem for the NS-NPP equations with Fujita-Kato type initial data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08533)
*Jihong Zhao,Chao Deng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global well-posedness of the Cauchy problem for NS-NPP (Navier-Stokes-Nernst-Planck-Poisson) equations with large Fujita-Kato type initial data, requiring only smallness on a weighted combination of the velocity divergence-free part and ionic density difference, while allowing the sum of particle densities to be arbitrarily large in Sobolev spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend global well-posedness results for NS-NPP equations beyond the typical smallness assumptions, specifically showing that no smallness condition is needed on the sum of initial positive and negative charge densities in Sobolev frameworks.

Method: The authors identify a critical smallness condition involving a weighted combination of the initial velocity in $\dot{H}^{-1+d/2}$ and the density difference $N_0 - P_0$ in $\dot{H}^{-2+d/2}$, multiplied by an exponential factor depending on the sum $N_0 + P_0$. Under this condition, they prove the existence of a unique global solution via appropriate a priori estimates and fixed-point/iterative arguments.

Result: The NS-NPP equations admit a unique global solution whenever the stated condition holds with constants $c_0, C_0 > 0$. This allows the sum $\|N_0 + P_0\|_{\dot H^{-2+d/2}}$ to be arbitrary large, demonstrating global solvability without smallness restrictions on the sum of initial particle densities.

Conclusion: Global well-posedness is achieved for NS-NPP with large initial data in the Fujita-Kato critical framework, where only the divergence-free part of velocity and the density difference need to be small in a suitable sense, while the total ionic density can be arbitrarily large.

Abstract: In this paper, we prove the global well-posedness of the Cauchy problem for the NS-NPP equations with some large Fujita-Kato type initial data. Specifically, we show that there exist two positive constants $c_{0}$ and $C_{0}$ such that if the initial data $(u_{0}, N_{0}, P_{0})$ satisfies the following condition: \begin{equation*}
  \left(\|u_0\|_{\dot{H}^{-1+\frac{d}{2}}}+\|N_{0}-P_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{-2+\frac{d}{2}}}\right)\exp\left\{C_{0}\big(\|N_{0}+P_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{-2+\frac{d}{2}}}^2+1\big)\right\}
  \leq
  c_{0}, \end{equation*} then the NS-NPP equations admits a unique global solution. This result implies global existence of solutions without any smallness conditions imposed on the sum of initial particle densities of negative and positive electric charge in the framework of Sobolev spaces.

</details>


### [143] [On the Invertibility of the Potential-Density Mapping for the Vlasov-Poisson System in Analytic Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08586)
*Simon Le Bouëdec*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops the mathematical analysis of Time-Dependent Density Functional Theory (TDDFT) for Vlasov-Poisson systems, establishing the invertibility of the potential-density mapping on the torus when the exterior potential is real-analytic in space, and recovering a Runge-Gross type theorem for time-analytic exterior potentials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to provide rigorous mathematical foundations for TDDFT in the context of kinetic theory (Vlasov equations), specifically addressing whether a given density profile uniquely determines the generating potential—a fundamental question underlying the validity of TDDFT approaches for charged particle systems.

Method: The authors develop a mathematical framework for the potential-density mapping in Vlasov-Poisson systems on T^d × R^d. They establish: (1) invertibility (injectivity and surjectivity) of the potential-density mapping when the exterior potential is real-analytic in space, and (2) a Runge-Gross type theorem for smooth exterior potentials that are real-analytic in time. The surjectivity is treated as an inverse problem—finding the electric field that produces a prescribed density profile over a given time interval.

Result: The paper establishes invertibility of the potential-density mapping for the Vlasov-Poisson system on T^d × R^d under real-analyticity assumptions on the exterior potential in space. It also recovers a Runge-Gross type theorem for the Vlasov-Poisson equation when the exterior potential is real-analytic in time.

Conclusion: The work provides rigorous mathematical justification for the TDDFT framework in the kinetic (Vlasov) setting introduced by Manfredi, confirming that in the analytic setting, the potential-density correspondence is well-posed for the inverse problem of reconstructing electric fields from density profiles.

Abstract: We develop the mathematical analysis of the Time-Dependent Density Functional Theory introduced by Manfredi [J. Plasma Phys., 86.2 (2020), 825860201] in the setting of Vlasov equations with a time-dependent exterior potential. In this context, we establish the invertibility of the potential-density mapping for the Vlasov-Poisson system on $\mathbb{T}^d \times \mathbb{R}^d$ when the exterior potential is (real-)analytic in the space variable. We also recover a Runge-Gross type Theorem for the Vlasov-Poisson equation for smooth (real-)analytic in time exterior potential.
  The surjectivity of the potential-density mapping is an inverse problem for the Vlasov-Poisson equation: we find the electric field that generates a given density profile on a given time interval.

</details>


### [144] [Modulated Gibbs Measure and Mean-Field Limit for 3D Vlasov--Poisson--Fokker--Planck Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08640)
*Xuanrui Feng,Zhenfu Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper introduces a modulated Gibbs measure for stochastic Newton's systems with singular repulsive interactions, showing it is O(N^{-1})-close to the tensorized reference in normalized relative entropy. This yields the first cutoff-free mean-field limit and propagation of chaos for the 3D Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck (VPFP) equation on an N-independent short-time interval.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to overcome cutoff issues in deriving mean-field limits and propagation of chaos for singular repulsive interaction systems, specifically targeting the 3D VPFP equation where previous methods failed to provide cutoff-free results.

Method: The approach combines: (1) a modulated Gibbs measure constructed for tensorized initial data, (2) uniform-in-N partition-function estimates from Wang-Zhao to establish O(N^{-1}) closeness in normalized relative entropy between the modulated Gibbs measure and its tensorized reference, and (3) a stability argument transferring propagation of chaos from modulated Gibbs measures to tensorized data and other initial laws. This is combined with weighted BBGKY estimates from Bresch-Jabin-Soler.

Result: The method yields the first cutoff-free mean-field limit and propagation of chaos for the three-dimensional Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck equation on an N-independent short-time interval.

Conclusion: The modulation of Gibbs measures provides a viable pathway to establish propagation of chaos for singular interacting particle systems without cutoff assumptions, at least on short-time horizons independent of particle number N.

Abstract: We introduce a modulated Gibbs measure for the usual tensorized initial data for stochastic Newton's systems with singular repulsive interactions. Using the uniform-in-$N$ partition-function estimates of Wang--Zhao \cite{wang2026uniform}, we show that the modulated Gibbs measure and its tensorized reference are $O(N^{-1})$-close in normalized relative entropy. A stability argument then transfers propagation of chaos from modulated Gibbs measures to tensorized data and other initial laws at the same entropy scale. Combined with the weighted BBGKY estimates of Bresch--Jabin--Soler \cite{bresch2025new}, this yields the first cutoff-free mean-field limit and propagation of chaos for the three-dimensional Vlasov--Poisson--Fokker--Planck (VPFP) equation on an $N$-independent short-time interval.

</details>


### [145] [An Inverse Random Source Problem for the Moore-Gibson-Thompson Equation Driven by Fractional Brownian Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08643)
*Lingxi Gao,Yeqiong Ye,Ting Zhou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies an inverse random source problem for the stochastic Moore-Gibson-Thompson equation driven by fractional Brownian motion with Hurst index H in (0,1). The authors prove existence and uniqueness of mild solutions, and establish uniqueness results for recovering the spatial strength functions f_i or the temporal functions g_i from boundary flux measurements on a special nonempty open subset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the inverse random source problem for a stochastic Moore-Gibson-Thompson equation, extending such problems to the fractional Brownian motion setting with general Hurst index H in (0,1).

Method: The source is modeled as f_1(x)g_1(t)Ḃ^H(t) + f_2(x)g_2(t). Existence and uniqueness of mild solutions are verified, and uniqueness of recovering the spatial or temporal components of the source from boundary flux measurements on a special nonempty open subset is proven.

Result: Existence and uniqueness of mild solutions are established, and uniqueness for recovering the strengths f_i(x) (given g_i) and g_i(t) (given f_i) from boundary flux data is proved for H in (0,1).

Conclusion: The paper successfully extends inverse random source analysis to the stochastic Moore-Gibson-Thompson equation driven by fractional Brownian motion with arbitrary Hurst index in (0,1), providing rigorous uniqueness results for source recovery.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider an inverse random source problem for the stochastic Moore-Gibson-Thompson equation driven by fractional Brownian motion with Hurst index $H \in (0, 1)$ of the form $f_1(x)g_1(t)\dot{B}^H(t)+f_2(x)g_2(t)$. Given the random source, existence and uniqueness of mild solutions are verified. For the inverse problem, the uniqueness of recovering the strength $f_i(x)$ if the time functions $g_i$ are known and $g_i(t)$ if the spatial functions $f_i$ are known when $H \in(0,1)$ from the boundary flux on a special nonempty open subset is proved.

</details>


### [146] [Long-term behaviour of the CAO-system: Optimal polynomial $H^1$-convergence to degenerate equilibria](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08664)
*Tim Binz*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper provides a definitive long-term dynamics analysis of the CAO-system, establishing convergence of global strong solutions to constant equilibria in the full H^1-topology. The authors prove that the system exhibits polynomial (rather than exponential) decay and determine the optimal decay rate, attributing this algebraic slowing to a nonlinear slow manifold (center manifold) induced by nonlinear coupling conditions. This is claimed to be the first global polynomial decay result for a non-gradient, non-local dissipative system on a bounded domain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the long-term dynamics of the CAO-system in the non-perturbative regime, where conventional parabolic theory would suggest exponential convergence. The authors seek to characterize the actual decay behavior and explain the mechanism behind the unexpected algebraic (polynomial) rate.

Method: The analysis establishes convergence of global strong solutions to constant equilibria in the full H^1-topology without perturbative assumptions. The paper demonstrates the polynomial decay rate and identifies the optimal rate, showing this algebraic slowing arises as a purely nonlinear effect through the emergence of a slow manifold (center manifold) created by nonlinear coupling conditions.

Result: The CAO-system exhibits polynomial rate of decay with the optimal rate determined. This contradicts the exponential convergence typically expected for uniformly parabolic systems on bounded domains. The result provides the first global polynomial decay theorem for a non-gradient, non-local dissipative system on a bounded domain.

Conclusion: The algebraic (polynomial) decay of the CAO-system is a purely nonlinear phenomenon driven by nonlinear coupling-induced slow manifold dynamics, marking a significant departure from classical parabolic convergence expectations and establishing a new benchmark for decay results in non-gradient, non-local dissipative systems.

Abstract: This article is concerned with a definitive analysis of its long-term dynamics of the CAO-system. We establish the convergence of global strong solutions to constant equilibria in the full $H^1$-topology in the non-perturbative regime. Remarkably, we demonstrate that the CAO-system exhibits a polynomial rate of decay and we determine the optimal rate. This phenomenon stands in stark contrast to the exponential convergence typically expected for uniformly parabolic systems on bounded domains. This algebraic slowing is shown to be a purely nonlinear effect, driven by the emergence of a slow manifold (center manifold) induced by nonlinear coupling conditions. To the best of our knowledge, this provides the first instance of a global polynomial decay result for a non-gradient, non-local dissipative system on a bounded domain.

</details>


### [147] [The $\mathrm{L}^1$-Stokes Semigroup](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08715)
*Tim Binz,Anatole Gaudin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the Stokes operator with no-slip boundary conditions on L1-type spaces over bounded C1,α-domains, showing that the realization on L1σ,n(Ω) does not generate a C0-semigroup, while its realization on L1/W1,1 does generate a compact analytic C0-semigroup. This resolves a fifty-year open problem and completes the theory of the Stokes semigroup across all solenoidal Lebesgue spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To settle a long-standing open problem (nearly fifty years) regarding whether the Stokes operator generates a C0-semigroup in the pure L1-setting with no-slip boundary conditions on bounded domains, an issue that had resisted resolution despite progress in other function spaces.

Method: The proof combines the sun-dual (semigroup dual) construction, a precise analysis of the failure of the Helmholtz decomposition in L1, the result of Abe and Giga on the Stokes semigroup on Cσ,0(Ω), and recent regularity theory refinements for the Stokes operator by Breit and the second author. The key insight is that the two L1 realizations, canonically identified for 1< p <∞ via Helmholtz decomposition, cease to be equivalent at p=1.

Result: The Stokes operator on L1σ,n(Ω) does not generate a C0-semigroup despite unique solvability of the resolvent problem, whereas on L1(Ω,Cd)/∇W1,1(Ω,C) it generates a compact, analytic C0-semigroup that leaves L1σ,n(Ω) invariant. Intermediate results on spaces of Radon measures are also obtained.

Conclusion: The paper provides the first positive generation theorem for the Stokes operator with no-slip boundary conditions in a pure L1-setting on a bounded domain, thereby completing the theory of the Stokes semigroup across the full scale of solenoidal Lebesgue spaces.

Abstract: We study the Stokes operator with no-slip boundary conditions on the spaces $\mathrm{L}_{σ,n}(Ω)$ and ${\mathrm{L}^1(Ω,\mathbb{C}^d)}/{\nabla \mathrm{W}^{1,1}(Ω,\mathbb{C})}$, where $Ω\subset\mathbb{R}^d$ is an arbitrary bounded $\mathrm{C}^{1,α}$-domain. We show that the Stokes operator on $\mathrm{L}^1_{σ,n}(Ω)$ does not generate a $\mathrm{C}_0$-semigroup, even though the resolvent problem is uniquely solvable. In stark contrast, its realization on ${\mathrm{L}^1(Ω,\mathbb{C}^d)}/{\nabla \mathrm{W}^{1,1}(Ω,\mathbb{C})}$ generates a compact, analytic $\mathrm{C}_0$-semigroup, which leaves $\mathrm{L}^1_{σ,n}(Ω)$ invariant.
  The key point is that these two realizations, which are canonically identified for $1<p<\infty$ through the Helmholtz decomposition, cease to be equivalent at the endpoint $p=1$. This leads to genuinely different functional analytic properties. Our result provides the first positive generation theorem for the Stokes operator with no-slip boundary conditions in a pure $\mathrm{L}^1$-setting on a bounded domain and settles a problem that had remained open for nearly fifty years; see, e.g., \cite{Koz:01,DHP:01}. In this sense, it completes the theory of the Stokes semigroup across the full scale of solenoidal Lebesgue spaces on (smooth) bounded domains. As an intermediate step, some results on the space of Radon measures are obtained.
  The proof combines the sun-dual construction with a precise analysis of the failure of the Helmholtz decomposition in $\mathrm{L}^1$, the celebrated result of Abe and Giga \cite{AG:12} on the Stokes semigroup on $\mathrm{C}_{σ,0}(Ω)$ and the regularity theory refinements for the Stokes operator recently developed by Breit and the second author \cite{BG:25}.

</details>


### [148] [Shape optimization of light structures with general Hooke's laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08732)
*Dimitrios Andreakis,Flaviana Iurlano,Filip Rindler*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper extends the relaxation result for minimum-compliance shape optimization from the simplest isotropic quadratic energy density to the entire class of elasticity tensors in any dimension, fully solving Bouchitté's conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A previous result established convergence of approximately optimal shapes to minimizing generalized shapes for the light-structures compliance functional, but only for the simplest isotropic case (squared Frobenius norm) in dimensions 2 and 3. This was due to reliance on explicit formulas by Allaire, Kohn, and Strang that are not available generally.

Method: The authors develop new arguments and substantially modify the original approach, since explicit relaxed-integrand formulas are not available for general isotropic or anisotropic elasticity tensors. They also streamline and simplify other parts of the original proof while clarifying related statements in the literature.

Result: The relaxation result is established for the entire class of isotropic or anisotropic elasticity tensors in any dimension, thereby solving Bouchitté's conjecture in full generality.

Conclusion: The conjecture is fully resolved without reliance on dimension-specific or energy-specific explicit formulas, requiring novel proof techniques that also improve the clarity and simplicity of the overall argument.

Abstract: For the minimum-compliance shape optimization problem it was established recently [\textit{Duke Math.\ J.} 172 (2023), 43--103] that a sequence of approximately optimal shapes of exact volume \(\e\) converges to a limiting generalized shape, which is represented by a possibly diffuse probability measure, as \(\e \todown 0\). This generalized shape minimizes the so-called light-structures compliance functional, which --- as conjectured by Bouchitté --- is expressed in terms of a relaxed integrand, reflecting that optimal shapes must only involve ``lower-dimensional'' structures, like the Michell-type trusses that are well known in the engineering domain. However, the aforementioned result only applies for the simplest isotropic quadratic energy density, that is, the squared Frobenius norm, in dimensions two and three. The present work extends the relaxation result to the entire class of (isotropic or anisotropic) elasticity tensors and to any dimension, thus solving Bouchitté's conjecture in full generality. Since the previous work relied on explicit formulas for the relaxed integrands (due to Allaire, Kohn, and Strang), which are not available in the general case, several new arguments and substantial modifications of the original approach are required. We also significantly simplify and streamline other parts of the original argument, in addition to clarifying some related statements in the literature.

</details>


### [149] [Transport and flow for horizontal Sobolev contact velocities in Carnot groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08738)
*Gianluca Somma,Simone Verzellesi,Davide Vittone*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes new well-posedness results for transport and flow equations driven by contact vector fields on Carnot groups, where velocity fields have horizontal Sobolev regularity. This extends results previously known only for Heisenberg groups to the broader sub-Riemannian setting, using renormalized solution theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend well-posedness results for transport and flow equations from the Euclidean setting (and previous Heisenberg group results) to general Carnot groups with their stratified sub-Riemannian geometry, where velocity fields only possess Sobolev regularity along horizontal directions.

Method: The proof employs the theory of renormalized solutions originally introduced by DiPerna and Lions in the Euclidean setting, adapted to the contact vector fields and horizontal Sobolev regularity structure on Carnot groups.

Result: New well-posedness results are established for transport and flow equations driven by contact vector fields on Carnot groups with horizontal Sobolev regularity, generalizing prior results from Heisenberg groups to broader Carnot group settings.

Conclusion: The results demonstrate that well-posedness for transport and flow equations holds on general Carnot groups under horizontal Sobolev regularity assumptions, significantly broadening the scope of sub-Riemannian theory beyond the previously studied Heisenberg group case.

Abstract: We establish new well-posedness results for transport and flow equations driven by contact vector fields on Carnot groups. The velocity fields are assumed to have horizontal Sobolev regularity, namely Sobolev regularity only along the horizontal directions determined by the stratified geometry of the group. In the broader sub-Riemannian setting, results of this type were previously known only for Heisenberg groups. Our proof relies on the theory of renormalized solutions as originally introduced by DiPerna and Lions in the Euclidean setting.

</details>


### [150] [MIT bag model and infinite mass limit in non-smooth domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08791)
*Badreddine Benhellal,Noah Körner,Dylan Machado,Konstantin Pankrashkin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies Dirac operators with MIT bag boundary conditions in Euclidean domains with compact Lipschitz boundaries in arbitrary dimensions. The authors prove these operators are self-adjoint and can be recovered as norm-resolvent limits of Dirac operators with large mass terms outside the domain, under a certain asymptotic assumption on a Robin-Laplacian eigenvalue. This assumption holds for convex domains and locally convexifiable domains, providing the first infinite mass interpretation for the MIT bag model (in the resolvent convergence sense) for non-smooth domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the infinite mass limit interpretation (norm-resolvent convergence) for Dirac operators with MIT bag boundary conditions in non-smooth (Lipschitz) domains, which had not been achieved previously for such generality.

Method: The authors prove self-adjointness of Dirac operators with MIT bag conditions on suitable domains. They show convergence to these operators as norm-resolvent limits of Dirac operators in the whole space with large exterior mass terms, contingent on a prescribed asymptotic behavior of an associated Robin-Laplacian eigenvalue. They verify this assumption for convex and locally convexifiable domains. Results are extended to generalized MIT bag boundary conditions via the congruence transform.

Result: The assumption on the Robin-Laplacian eigenvalue holds for a class of non-smooth domains including convex domains and those that can be "locally convexified" by diffeomorphisms. This yields the first infinite mass interpretation for the MIT bag model via resolvent convergence for non-smooth domains, extended to generalized MIT bag conditions.

Conclusion: MIT bag Dirac operators on Lipschitz domains are self-adjoint and admit a norm-resolvent infinite mass limit interpretation, now validated for a broad class of non-smooth domains and extended to generalized boundary conditions.

Abstract: The work is devoted to the study of Dirac operators with MIT bag boundary conditions in Euclidean domains with compact Lipschitz boundaries in arbitrary dimensions. It is shown that such operators are self-adjoint on suitable definition domains and can be recovered as the norm-resolvent limits of Dirac operators in the whole space with a large mass term outside the domain, under the assumption that an associated Robin-Laplacian eigenvalue has a prescribed asymptotic behavior with respect to a parameter in the boundary condition. This assumption is shown to hold for a class of non-smooth domains, which includes convex domains and, more generally, domains that can be "locally convexified" by suitable diffeomorphisms. To the best of our knowledge, this represents the first infinite mass interpretation for the MIT bag model in the sense of resolvent convergence for non-smooth domains. Most results are extended to the generalized MIT bag boundary conditions with the help of the recently established congruence transform.

</details>


### [151] [Low-Temperature Large Deviations for the Two-Dimensional Wick-Ordered Cubic Wave Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08829)
*Daniel Heydecker*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves a trajectory large deviation principle (LDP) in the low-temperature limit for the 2D defocusing cubic nonlinear wave equation with Gibbs-distributed initial data, and shows that high-frequency perturbations of the initial data produce a continuum of distinct mass-shifted trajectory rate functions despite sharing the same static rate function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the large deviation behavior of stochastic/random initial data for nonlinear wave equations in the low-temperature regime, and to investigate how perturbations of the initial data affect the rate functions governing these deviations.

Method: The authors prove a trajectory large deviation principle for the two-dimensional Wick-ordered defocusing cubic nonlinear wave equation, with initial data sampled from the invariant Gibbs measure. They analyze the effect of asymptotically vanishing high-frequency perturbations of the Gibbs initial data on the resulting trajectory rate functions.

Result: A trajectory LDP is established in the low-temperature limit. Furthermore, it is shown that high-frequency perturbations that vanish asymptotically—while preserving the static rate function—produce a continuum of distinct mass-shifted trajectory rate functions, demonstrating non-uniqueness of the dynamical rate function for the same static behavior.

Conclusion: The trajectory large deviations under the invariant Gibbs measure are sensitive to high-frequency details of the initial data, leading to a family of distinct dynamical rate functions parameterized by the perturbation structure, even though the static rate function remains unchanged.

Abstract: We prove a trajectory large deviation principle, in the low-temperature limit, for the two-dimensional Wick-ordered defocusing cubic nonlinear wave equation, with initial data drawn from the invariant Gibbs measure. We further show that asymptotically vanishing high-frequency perturbations of the Gibbs initial data, with the same static rate function, generate a continuum of distinct mass-shifted trajectory rate functions.

</details>


### [152] [On 2D Scattering for a Critical Nonlinear Schrödinger Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08845)
*Vladimir Georgiev,Tohru Ozawa*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves modified scattering results for the 2D defocusing NLS with the scattering-critical nonlinearity |u|u. The authors show that the remodulated interaction representation has a local L² limit for general data in Σ = H¹ ∩ F⁻¹H¹, and for radial data, convergence holds in Lᵖ(ℝ²) for all 2 < p < ∞.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The 2D NLS with the specific nonlinearity |u|u is scattering-critical, meaning standard scattering fails due to the slow decay of solutions. This necessitates the study of modified scattering, where the asymptotic behavior includes a nonlinear phase correction. Understanding the modified scattering behavior for this equation is important for the broader theory of critical NLS equations, and this paper aims to establish convergence results for general and radial data.

Method: The proof involves analyzing the remodulated interaction representation, which accounts for the nonlinear phase modification inherent to the critical setting. The authors establish local L² convergence for general data in the weighted Sobolev space Σ, and leverage radial symmetry to obtain stronger Lᵖ convergence for all 2 < p < ∞ through improved decay and frequency-localized estimates.

Result: The remodulated interaction representation converges in local L² for general data in Σ. For radial data, convergence is strengthened to Lᵖ for every 2 < p < ∞, indicating the solutions exhibit genuine modified scattering behavior toward a nonlinear wave profile.

Conclusion: The paper establishes modified scattering for the two-dimensional defocusing NLS with the scattering-critical nonlinearity |u|u, providing convergence results for general data in local L² and for radial data in all Lᵖ spaces with p > 2.

Abstract: We study modified scattering for the two-dimensional defocusing nonlinear Schrödinger equation (NLS) with the gauge-invariant, scattering-critical nonlinearity $|u|u$. We prove that the remodulated interaction representation has a local $L^2$ limit for general data in $Σ=H^1\cap\F^{-1}H^1$. For radial data, we prove convergence in $L^p(\R^2)$ for every $2<p<\infty$.

</details>


### [153] [Planar Gross--Pitaevskii traveling waves at every subsonic speed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08906)
*Changfeng Gui,Shanfa Lai,Guolin Qin,Juncheng Wei*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves the existence of finite-energy traveling waves for the planar Gross-Pitaevskii equation at every subsonic speed c in (0, √2), resolving a longstanding open problem posed by Mariş (2013) and Bellazzini and Ruiz (2023). The proof relies on a novel energy estimate relating energy to action and Morse index, along with finite-bubble compactness results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The existence of prescribed-speed traveling-wave solutions for the planar Gross-Pitaevskii equation has remained an open problem for decades, explicitly stated as open by Mariş (Ann. of Math., 2013) and Bellazzini and Ruiz (Amer. J. Math., 2023). Resolving this question is fundamental to understanding the dynamics of the equation.

Method: The proof relies on a key energy estimate E(ψ) ≤ C_J(I_c(ψ) + ind(ψ)) for c ∈ J, where E is energy, I_c is the action at speed c, ind is the real Morse index, and J is any compact interval in (0, √2). This is combined with finite-bubble compactness results that include splitting of energy, action, potential energy, and momentum.

Result: The paper establishes: (1) existence of finite-energy traveling waves for all subsonic speeds c ∈ (0, √2), resolving the open problem; (2) finite-bubble compactness with splitting properties; (3) attainment of the action among nonconstant waves of Morse index at most one.

Conclusion: The results definitively resolve the prescribed-speed traveling-wave existence problem for the planar Gross-Pitaevskii equation across the entire subsonic regime, using a combination of energy estimates, Morse index theory, and compactness analysis.

Abstract: For every subsonic speed $c\in(0,\sqrt2)$, we prove the existence of a finite-energy traveling wave for the planar Gross--Pitaevskii equation. This resolves the longstanding problem of the existence of prescribed-speed traveling-wave solutions in two dimensions, explicitly stated as open by Mariş (Ann. of Math., 2013) and Bellazzini and Ruiz (Amer. J. Math., 2023). The proof relies essentially on the energy estimate \begin{equation*}
  E(ψ)\le C_J\bigl(I_c(ψ)+\ind(ψ)\bigr),
  \qquad c\in J, \end{equation*} where $E$ is the energy, $I_c$ the action at speed $c$, $\ind$ the real Morse index, $J$ is any compact interval contained in $(0,\sqrt2)$, and $C_J$ is a positive constant depending only on $J$. We also prove finite-bubble compactness, including splitting of the energy, action, potential energy, and momentum, and attainment of the action among nonconstant waves of Morse index at most one.

</details>


### [154] [Exact high-order GPT cancellation in noncircular multilayers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08924)
*Youjun Deng,Hongyu Liu,Longyue Tao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves exact high-order generalized polarization tensor (GPT) cancellation in two-dimensional noncircular multilayers with finite positive isotropic coating conductivities, establishing unique coating conductivities for weak neutrality and complete K-contracted GPT cancellation on cyclically symmetric core-shell geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend GPT cancellation results beyond circular geometries and near-background conditions to broad classes of noncircular multilayers, enabling exact manipulation of GPT coefficients for cloaking and neutral structure design.

Method: Proves existence and uniqueness of coating conductivities for weak neutrality on cyclically symmetric core-shell geometries; establishes locally unique conductivities canceling K-CGPT blocks on strictly star-shaped homothetic multilayers; replaces near-background with small-core conditions; constructs non-symmetric families via mode variation; rigorously certifies nondegeneracy conditions and chiral examples for three coatings.

Result: Established exact GPT cancellation results including weak neutrality for uniform fields, higher-order K-CGPT cancellation on cyclic noncircular multilayers, exact non-symmetric families via interface mode variation, and enhanced near-cloaking for entire harmonic incident fields, corroborated by certified numerical examples.

Conclusion: The work provides rigorous theoretical foundations for exact high-order GPT cancellation in noncircular multilayer structures, significantly broadening the scope beyond concentric disk geometries while delivering certified numerical validation.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with exact high-order generalized polarization tensor (GPT) cancellation in two-dimensional noncircular multilayers with finite positive isotropic coating conductivities. We first prove that, for every admissible cyclically symmetric core--shell geometry, there exists a unique positive coating conductivity for which the structure is weakly neutral to every uniform incident field. For higher orders, we establish locally unique coating conductivities that cancel the complete \(K\)-contracted GPTs (K-CGPTs) block on a broad class of fixed noncircular multilayers with cyclic symmetry. This includes homothetic multilayers generated by strictly star-shaped symmetric domains, without assuming that the geometry is close to concentric disks. We also replace the near-background condition by a small-core condition covering finite, insulating, and perfectly conducting cores. By varying selected interface modes together with the coating conductivities near a radial GPT-vanishing structure, we further construct exact families with neither rotational nor reflection symmetry. For three coatings, the relevant nondegeneracy conditions and an explicit chiral example are rigorously certified. Finally, we show that the resulting cancellation yields enhanced near-cloaking for every entire harmonic incident field. A set of certified numerical examples fully corroborates the theoretical predictions established in this work.

</details>


### [155] [Stable determination of damping and potential coefficients in a semilinear wave equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08951)
*Rahul Bhardwaj,Mandeep Kumar,Manmohan Vashisth*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes the first Holder-type stability estimates for the simultaneous recovery of time-independent damping, linear potential, and nonlinear potential coefficients in a semilinear wave equation from the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map, using second-order linearization combined with geometric optics and asymptotic solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors address the inverse problem of simultaneously recovering multiple coefficients (damping, linear potential, and nonlinear potential) in a semilinear wave equation. No prior stability results existed for the simultaneous determination of these three coefficients, making this the first such contribution.

Method: The approach combines second-order linearization with suitably constructed geometric optics solutions and asymptotic solutions to establish stability estimates from the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map in a bounded domain of R^n (n >= 2).

Result: Holder-type stability estimates are established for the recovery of each of the three coefficients (time-independent damping, linear potential, and nonlinear potential) appearing in the semilinear wave equation, under suitable a priori bounds on these coefficients.

Conclusion: This work provides the first stability result for simultaneous determination of time-independent damping, linear and nonlinear potentials in a semilinear wave equation, filling a gap in the stability analysis literature for such inverse problems.

Abstract: We consider an inverse problem for a semilinear wave equation with time-independent damping, linear potential, and nonlinear potential coefficients in a bounded domain of $\mathbb{R}^n$ for $n\geq 2$. The main objective is to establish stability estimates for the simultaneous recovery of these coefficients from the associated Dirichlet-to-Neumann map. Our approach combines second-order linearization with suitably constructed geometric optics and asymptotic solutions. We establish Hölder-type stability estimates for the recovery of each of the three coefficients appearing in the semilinear wave equation under suitable a priori bounds on these coefficients. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first stability result for simultaneous determination of time-independent damping, linear and nonlinear potentials in a semiliner wave equation.

</details>


### [156] [The Sharp $C^{1,1}$ Target Threshold for Minimizing Constraint Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08952)
*Yilu Liu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes sharp regularity results for local minimizers of the Dirichlet energy mapping into a target domain that is the complement of a convex (or more general) body with a smooth boundary. It proves that if the boundary of the forbidden set is C^{1,1}, then all local minimizers are locally W^{2,∞} (hence C^{1,1}) on their continuity set, and demonstrates this threshold is sharp by constructing examples with C^{1,α} boundaries (0<α<1) where minimizers are continuous but not C^{1,1}.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to determine the optimal regularity of energy-minimizing maps when the target space is the complement of a convex obstacle (a fundamental problem in calculus of variations and geometric measure theory). It seeks a sharp threshold relating target boundary regularity to minimizer regularity in the Hölder scale.

Method: The authors prove that for C^{1,1} boundary ∂K, local minimizers are locally W^{2,∞} by analyzing the structure of the Dirichlet energy minimizers into the punctured/obstacle complement domain. For sharpness, they construct explicit convex bodies with C^{1,α} boundaries together with global minimizers that are continuous but fail to be C^{1,1}, showing the threshold cannot be relaxed.

Result: (1) If ∂K is C^{1,1}, every local minimizer of the Dirichlet energy valued in the complement is locally W^{2,∞} and hence C^{1,1} on its continuity set. (2) For every 0<α<1, there exist convex bodies with C^{1,α} boundaries and everywhere continuous global minimizers that are not C^{1,1}. This establishes C^{1,1} as the sharp threshold.

Conclusion: The regularity of minimizers of the Dirichlet energy into complement-type targets is governed precisely by the C^{1,1} condition on the target boundary. This is the sharp regularity threshold in the Hölder scale, interpolating regularity between the minimizer and the domain/target geometry.

Abstract: Let $K\subset\R^m$ be the closure of a bounded domain whose boundary is a compact embedded hypersurface, and let \(
  \overline M=\R^m\setminus\operatorname{int}K \) be the allowed target. We prove that if $\partial K$ is of class $C^{1,1}$, then every $\overline M$-valued local minimizer of the Dirichlet energy $E(u;B):=\int_B|Du|^2\dd x$ is locally $W^{2,\infty}$, and hence locally $C^{1,1}$, on its continuity set. For every $0<α<1$, we also construct a convex body with $C^{1,α}$ boundary and an everywhere continuous global
  minimizer that is not $C^{1,1}$. Thus $C^{1,1}$ is the sharp target
  regularity threshold in the Hölder scale.

</details>


### [157] [A growth gap for anisotropic minimal graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08972)
*Yang Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves a growth gap for gradients of entire anisotropic minimal graphs: any smooth nonaffine entire minimal graph (with respect to a uniformly elliptic parametric integrand) must have its gradient grow at least like R^α for some positive exponent α depending on dimension and the integrand. A gradient growing slower than R^α forces the solution to be affine, resolving a conjecture of Mooney and the author. The result holds in all dimensions without assumptions that the integrand is close to Euclidean area.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand rigidity/flexibility of entire minimal graphs under general anisotropic integrands. A key question is whether slow gradient growth forces flatness, generalizing known results for the Euclidean case where Bernstein-type theorems hold. Prior results likely only covered integrands close to Euclidean area, so a general theorem was open.

Method: The proof establishes that a smooth entire Φ-minimal graph with sub-polynomial gradient growth must be affine, via a quantitative growth-gap argument. The exponent α depends on n and Φ but is obtained through analytic techniques that do not require Φ to be near the Euclidean area integrand.

Result: For every n≥2 and every smooth uniformly elliptic parametric integrand Φ, there exists α>0 such that every nonaffine entire Φ-minimal graph satisfies sup_{B_R}|Du| ≥ cR^α for all large R. Equivalently, gradient growth o(R^α) forces flatness.

Conclusion: This resolves the conjecture of Mooney and the author in full generality—across all dimensions and without proximity assumptions to the Euclidean case—establishing a universal growth gap for anisotropic minimal graphs.

Abstract: We prove a growth gap for the gradient of entire anisotropic minimal graphs. For each $n\geq2$ and each smooth uniformly elliptic parametric integrand $Φ$ on $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$, there is an exponent $α=α(n,Φ)>0$ such that every smooth nonaffine entire $Φ$-minimal graph $u:\mathbb{R}^n\to\mathbb{R}$ satisfies $\sup_{B_R^n}|Du|\geq cR^α$ for all $R\geq R_0$, for some constants $c>0$ and $R_0<\infty$ depending on the solution. In particular, gradient growth $o(R^α)$ forces flatness, resolving a conjecture of Mooney and the author. The result holds in every dimension, without any assumption that $Φ$ is close to the Euclidean area integrand.

</details>


### [158] [Non-uniqueness for the Complex Ginzburg-Landau Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08985)
*Joel Dahne,Vladimír Šverák*

Main category: math.AP
