# math.PR [[Back]](#toc)

### [195] [Efficient computation of the asymptotics of extensive-rank HCIZ integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07771)
*Antoine Maillard,Jean-Christophe Mourrat*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper introduces an efficient numerical scheme based on particle discretization to solve the Matytsin boundary-value problem governing the high-dimensional asymptotics of Harish-Chandra-Itzykson-Zuber (HCIZ) integrals in the extensive-rank regime, proving convergence to the continuous problem for generic boundary densities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The high-dimensional limit of HCIZ integrals is governed by a one-dimensional boundary-value hydrodynamical problem (derived by Matytsin, proved by Guionnet and Zeitouni), which has wide-ranging applications. However, explicit solutions are known only in a few special cases, limiting the practical utility of this theory.

Method: The authors introduce a numerical scheme based on particle discretization to approximate solutions to the Matytsin boundary-value problem, and prove its convergence to the continuous problem for generic boundary densities.

Result: The scheme is validated against known analytical solutions and applied to generic densities, revealing interesting dynamical phenomena that were previously inaccessible.

Conclusion: This work opens the way toward numerical exploration of a wide range of high-dimensional models—including large deviations of random matrix spectra, free energy of disordered systems, high-dimensional statistics, and machine learning—that were previously intractable.

Abstract: We study the high-dimensional asymptotics of Harish-Chandra-Itzykson-Zuber (HCIZ) integrals in the extensive-rank regime. The limit of these integrals is governed by a one-dimensional boundary-value hydrodynamical problem originally derived by Matytsin (1994) and rigorously proved by Guionnet and Zeitouni (2002). Despite its wide-ranging applications, explicit solutions to this problem are known only in a few specific cases. In this work, we introduce an efficient numerical scheme based on a particle discretization and prove its convergence to the continuous boundary-value problem for generic boundary densities. We validate our approach against known analytical solutions and apply it to generic densities, uncovering interesting dynamical phenomena. The high-dimensional limit of HCIZ integrals appears in various contexts, from the large deviations of random matrix spectra to the limiting free energy of disordered systems, high-dimensional statistics, and machine learning. As such, our contribution opens the way towards the numerical exploration of a wide range of high-dimensional models that were previously intractable.

</details>


### [196] [Fluctuating Kinetic Theory: A Poissonian Stochastic Boltzmann Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06482)
*Zhengyan Wu*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper introduces a nonlinear Poissonian fluctuating Boltzmann equation whose noise encodes both fluctuations and the path large-deviation rate function of the underlying hard-sphere gas, addressing the challenge of nonnegativity through a finite-dimensional coarse-grained jump process.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to develop a fluctuating Boltzmann equation that correctly captures both fluctuations and large-deviation behavior of hard-sphere gases. Standard Gaussian-noise fluctuating hydrodynamics equations are insufficient, but Poissonian noise introduces the technical difficulty that jumps can destroy solution nonnegativity, which must be addressed to build a well-posed model.

Method: The authors construct a finite-dimensional coarse-grained jump process to avoid the nonnegativity problem caused by Poisson random measure jumps. They prove its well-posedness and nonnegativity, establish a good path large-deviation principle for each fixed mesh, and then analyze the asymptotic behavior of discrete rate functions as the mesh is refined under a regularized velocity cutoff.

Result: On a class of biased regular paths, the limit of the discrete rate functions as the mesh is refined recovers the corresponding cutoff Boltzmann large-deviation rate function for the hard-sphere gas, demonstrating consistency with the underlying particle system at the level of path large deviations.

Conclusion: The coarse-grained fluctuating Boltzmann model is consistent with the underlying hard-sphere particle system in terms of path large deviations, validating the Poissonian noise formulation as a correct mesoscopic description.

Abstract: We introduce a nonlinear Poissonian fluctuating Boltzmann equation whose noise encodes both the fluctuations and the path large-deviation rate function of the underlying hard-sphere gas. Unlike the Gaussian-noise equations commonly studied in fluctuating hydrodynamics, the present equation raises a new difficulty: a Poisson random measure produces jumps that may destroy the nonnegativity of the solution. To address this problem, we construct a finite-dimensional coarse-grained jump process and prove its well-posedness and nonnegativity. For each fixed mesh, this process satisfies a good path large-deviation principle. We then fix a regularized velocity cutoff and study the asymptotic behavior of the discrete rate functions as the mesh is refined. On a class of biased regular paths, their limit is the corresponding cutoff Boltzmann large-deviation rate function associated with the hard-sphere gas. This establishes the consistency of the coarse-grained fluctuating Boltzmann model with the underlying particle system at the level of path large deviations.

</details>


### [197] [Strong Galerkin Approximation, Malliavin Regularity, and Blow-Up for a Mixed Local--Nonlocal Stochastic Wave Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06883)
*Francisco Delgado-Vences,Jose Julian Pavon-Español*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper analyzes semilinear stochastic wave equations on bounded domains driven by additive trace-class noise, where the elastic response involves a mixed local-nonlocal operator combining the Laplacian and fractional Laplacian. The authors establish strong convergence of dense Galerkin approximations, derive Itô energy identities, prove global well-posedness in the defocusing regime with Malliavin regularity and absolute continuity of laws, and establish a dichotomy (explosion vs. infinite second moment) in the focusing regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The local and nonlocal operators (-θΔ + β(-Δ)^s) do not commute on bounded domains, meaning the standard Dirichlet basis fails to diagonalize the restricted fractional Laplacian. This structural challenge complicates both numerical approximation and theoretical analysis of stochastic wave equations with mixed local-nonlocal operators.

Method: The authors establish strong convergence of a non-diagonal dense Galerkin approximation scheme, derive the Itô energy identity via uniform energy bounds, analyze Malliavin regularity (showcasing solutions in D^{1,2} spaces), and apply the Bouleau-Hirsch criterion with fractional Sobolev embeddings to prove absolute continuity of one-dimensional laws.

Result: In the defocusing case (ε=+1), global well-posedness holds on the energy-subcritical range with a unique probabilistically strong solution in V×H, plus Malliavin differentiability and absolute continuity of the law of u(t,x₀). In the focusing case (ε=-1), local well-posedness and a dichotomy are proven: either finite-time pathwise explosion with positive probability or infinite second moment of the energy norm before an explicit critical time T*.

Conclusion: The paper provides a comprehensive well-posedness and regularity theory for stochastic wave equations with mixed local-nonlocal operators, while also highlighting that dense Galerkin interaction matrices introduce unique structural challenges for spatial statistical inference.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical behavior of a class of semilinear stochastic wave equations on a bounded smooth domain $\Ocal\subset\R^d$ driven by additive trace-class noise, where the elastic response is governed by a \emph{mixed local--nonlocal} operator $\Acal=-θΔ+β(-Δ)^s$ with $s\in(0,1)$. A fundamental challenge in this setting is that the local and nonlocal operators do not commute on bounded domains: the natural Dirichlet basis fails to diagonalize the restricted fractional Laplacian. Consequently, we first establish the \emph{strong} convergence of the resulting non-diagonal, dense Galerkin approximation scheme. Leveraging these uniform energy bounds, we rigorously derive the associated Itô energy identity. In the defocusing regime ($\varepsilon = +1$), this strong approximation yields global well-posedness on the energy-subcritical range, providing a unique probabilistically strong solution in the energy space $V \times H$. Within this variational framework, we conduct an analysis of the Malliavin regularity, showing $(u(t), v(t)) \in \mathbb{D}^{1,2}(V) \times \mathbb{D}^{1,2}(H)$, and leverage fractional Sobolev embeddings to prove that the one-dimensional probability law of $u(t, x_0)$ is absolutely continuous via the Bouleau--Hirsch criterion. In stark contrast, for the focusing regime ($\varepsilon = -1$), we establish local well-posedness and prove a rigorous dichotomy: under a negativity condition on the initial energy, either pathwise explosion occurs with positive probability in finite time, or the energy norm possesses an infinite second moment before an explicit critical time $T^*$. Finally, we observe how the dense Galerkin interaction matrices pose unique structural challenges for spatial statistical inference.

</details>


### [198] [Chain rule symmetry for singular SPDEs from multi-indices to decorated trees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07676)
*Yvain Bruned*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: These lecture notes review recent results on the chain rule for generalised and geometric KPZ equations, introducing Regularity Structures via B-series expansions with decorated trees and multi-indices, and showing how the chain rule is characterized via covariant derivative iterations (using operadic and homological tools for decorated trees).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a coherent and accessible review of recent advances in understanding the chain rule for generalised and geometric KPZ equations, particularly how this is formalized within the framework of Regularity Structures.

Method: The notes introduce Regularity Structures through B-series type expansions encoded by two combinatorial sets (decorated trees and multi-indices). The chain rule is first derived via integration by parts and diagrammatic computations. For the generalised KPZ equation, multi-indices yield a characterisation via iterations of covariant derivatives. For decorated trees, the same characterisation requires operadic and homological tools, the main proof arguments of which are summarised.

Result: A unified exposition of how the chain rule for generalized and geometric KPZ equations is established within Regularity Structures, with explicit characterisations via covariant derivative iterations for both multi-indices and decorated trees (the latter requiring operadic and homological arguments).

Conclusion: The lecture notes consolidate the understanding of chain rules in Regularity Structures for KPZ-type equations, highlighting the connection between algebraic/combinatorial structures (multi-indices, decorated trees, operad theory) and analytic results.

Abstract: In these lecture notes, we review the recent results around the chain rule for the generalised KPZ equation and the geometric KPZ equation. We introduce the main ideas of Regularity Structures via a B-series type expansion and the two main combinatorial sets for encoding this expansion: decorated trees and multi-indices. Then, we explain how the chain rule was first understood via integration by parts and diagrammatic computations. For the generalised KPZ equation, a scalar-valued equation, we use multi-indices to derive a characterisation in terms of iterations of covariant derivatives. The same characterisation for decorated trees is more involved and requires the use of operadic and homological tools. We summarise the main arguments of this proof.

</details>


### [199] [The Fenchel Game of Underdamped Langevin Dynamics: Insights into Accelerated Convergence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07812)
*Alexandra Borkowski,Nikolas Nüsken*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper quantifies convergence rates in KL divergence for the positional marginal of underdamped Langevin dynamics to a target distribution. For strongly log-concave targets, they achieve an exponential rate of e^{-√σ t}, and for merely log-concave targets, a polynomial rate of τ²/t². Both rates match the canonical accelerated gradient flow rates in R^d.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish sharp, explicit convergence rates for underdamped Langevin dynamics in KL divergence, which are known to accelerate sampling compared to overdamped Langevin. Prior work lacked rates matching the canonical accelerated gradient descent rates (√σ and 1/t² in the respective regimes).

Method: The authors introduce a novel interpretation of underdamped Langevin dynamics as a combination of strategies in an online sampling game. KL divergence is estimated using a cost function informed by fictitious competitors, with appropriately selected reference measures Π₀ and Π_τ at different times.

Result: For σ-strongly log-concave targets, KL(Law(Q_t)||π) ≤ e^{-√σ t}·KL(Law(Q₀,P₀)||Π₀). For merely log-concave targets, KL(Law(Q_t)||π) ≤ (τ²/t²)·KL(Law(Q_τ,P_τ)||Π_τ). Both rates match the canonical accelerated gradient descent rates: √σ (continuous-time Nesterov) and 1/t².

Conclusion: The underdamped Langevin dynamics exhibits accelerated convergence rates in KL divergence that precisely match accelerated gradient flows, achieved through the online sampling game framework and fictitious competitor-based cost functions. This provides theoretical justification for the acceleration observed in underdamped Langevin-based sampling algorithms.

Abstract: For $(Q_t,P_t)$ governed by suitably damped underdamped Langevin dynamics, we quantify the convergence in KL divergence of the positional marginal to a $σ$-strongly log-concave target $π(dq) = \frac{1}{Z}e^{-V(q)}dq$ as \begin{align*} \operatorname{KL}(\operatorname{Law}(Q_t) \| π) \leq e^{-\sqrtσ t}\operatorname{KL}(\operatorname{Law}(Q_{0}, P_{0})\, \|\, Π_0), \end{align*} where $Π_0$ denotes an appropriately selected reference measure. When $π$ is merely log-concave, the estimate \begin{align*}
  \operatorname{KL}(\operatorname{Law}(Q_t) \| π) \leq \frac{τ^2}{t^2}\operatorname{KL}(\operatorname{Law}(Q_τ, P_τ)\, \|\, Π_τ) \end{align*} is derived, where $Π_τ$ denotes another correspondingly chosen reference measure at time $τ> 0$. Both rates match precisely the canonical rates of the corresponding accelerated gradient flows in $\mathbb{R}^d$. They are achieved by the novel interpretation of the underdamped Langevin dynamics as a combination of strategies in an online sampling game and by estimating the KL divergence using a cost function informed by fictitious competitors.

</details>


### [200] [Polynomial mixing for the 3D damped cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation with degenerate noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08645)
*Rongchang Liu,Kening Lu,Lin Shi*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper proves polynomial (non-exponential) mixing for the defocusing damped cubic stochastic nonlinear Schrödinger equation (SNLS) on the 3D torus with saturating smooth finite-rank Brownian forcing. The mixing rate is established in p-Wasserstein metrics induced by the H^1 distance for all 1≤p<∞, along with sharp geometric characterizations of the saturation condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishing quantitative mixing rates for stochastic PDEs in the hypoelliptic/singular regime is a challenging open problem. The damped cubic SNLS on the 3D torus is a key test case where standard exponential mixing results fail or remain elusive, especially under degenerate (finite-rank) noise. Understanding how damping and forcing balance to produce polynomial-in-time decorrelation is fundamentally important for the ergodic theory of dispersive SPDEs.

Method: The proof combines several analytic tools: (1) a polynomial mixing criterion based on a stable–compact decomposition of solution differences with polynomial moment control of logarithmic path amplification; (2) Malliavin calculus shows a dense range, allowing compact error terms to be compensated by finite-dimensional Cameron–Martin shifts, yielding a block multiplier with negative mean logarithm; (3) a logarithmic transportation gauge combined with a renewal–reset coupling scheme gives polynomial mixing in the weaker L^2 distance; (4) a stationary regularity gain to H^{2-} upgrades the convergence rate to the H^1 metric.

Result: Polynomial mixing is proven at every prescribed polynomial order in p-Wasserstein metrics induced by H^1 distance for all 1≤p<∞, together with sharp geometric characterizations of the saturation condition for the noise.

Conclusion: This work establishes polynomial—rather than exponential—mixing as the correct quantitative ergodic rate for damped cubic SNLS in the saturating finite-rank noise regime on the 3D torus, providing both the first such result at polynomial order in H^1-Wasserstein metrics and sharp geometric insight into when saturation occurs.

Abstract: We prove polynomial mixing for the defocusing damped cubic stochastic nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the three-dimensional torus under saturating smooth finite rank Brownian forcing. The mixing rate is measured in the $p$-Wasserstein metric induced by the $H^1$ distance for every $1\le p<\infty$. We also obtain sharp geometric characterizations of saturation.
  The proof is based on a polynomial mixing criterion built on a stable--compact decomposition of the exact solution differences with polynomial moment control of the logarithmic path amplification. Dense Malliavin range allows the compact defect to be compensated by finite-dimensional Cameron--Martin shifts, producing a block multiplier with negative mean logarithm. A logarithmic transportation gauge, combined with a renewal--reset coupling scheme, then yields mixing at every prescribed polynomial order in the weaker $L^2$ distance. A stationary regularity gain to $H^{2-}$ then enables us to upgrade the convergence to $H^1$.

</details>


### [201] [Exact Asymptotics for the 2D Euclidean Random Matching Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08717)
*Victor Armegioiu,Michael Goldman,Francesco Grotto,Dario Trevisan*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper determines the exact first-order asymptotics of expected optimal cost in two-dimensional random bipartite matching for all finite power costs q≥1 on the flat torus, resolving an open question by Talagrand for the case q=1. The proof connects this to the energy asymptotics of solutions to a p-Poisson equation with a regularized white-noise source, and in the limit identifies this energy as the solution of a Variational Martingale Problem governed by a parabolic Monge-Ampère flow.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous work on random bipartite matching asymptotics was incomplete: exact first-order constants were only known for specific cost exponents, and the case q=1 (linear cost / Talagrand's question) remained open. Understanding the sharp asymptotic constants for all q≥1 provides a more complete picture of the optimal transport problem between random point sets.

Method: The approach establishes a relationship between the bipartite matching cost and the energy of solutions to the p-Poisson equation driven by a regularized white-noise source. A crucial ingredient is identifying, in the vanishing regularization limit, the energy as the value of a Variational Martingale Problem on a limiting Gaussian filtration, whose characterization is given via a parabolic Monge-Ampère flow.

Result: Exact first-order asymptotic constants are obtained for the expected optimal cost of two-dimensional random bipartite matching for every finite q≥1 on the flat torus, including the previously open q=1 endpoint case posed by Talagrand.

Conclusion: The paper closes the gap in the asymptotic theory of random bipartite matching in two dimensions by providing sharp constants for all finite power costs, and introduces a novel variational martingale problem framework linked to parabolic Monge-Ampère flows that may be of broader significance.

Abstract: We determine the exact first-order asymptotics of the expected optimal cost in two-dimensional random bipartite matching, for every finite power cost $q \ge 1$, on the flat torus. In the endpoint case $q=1$, this answers a question by Talagrand, in the periodic case. The argument involves the closely related asymptotics of the energy of the solution of the $p$-Poisson equation with a regularized white-noise source. In the limit of vanishing regularization parameter, we identify this energy as the solution of a Variational Martingale Problem on a limiting Gaussian filtration, whose value is characterized by a parabolic Monge-Ampère flow.

</details>


### [202] [Polynomial mixing for the weakly damped stochastic nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the whole space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08935)
*Vahagn Nersesyan,Tianyi Pan*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper proves the existence and uniqueness of a stationary measure for a weakly damped stochastic nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the real line with smooth space-time noise, establishing polynomial mixing in the dual-Lipschitz metric over H^1, with no restriction on the damping size.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to establish ergodic properties (stationary measure existence/uniqueness and mixing rates) for the stochastic NLS equation on the non-compact domain R, where classical compactness arguments fail due to the loss of compactness on the whole real line.

Method: The proof uses a coupling argument built on a Foiaş-Prodi-type estimate in the H^1 norm, derived via a Lyapunov functional adapted to the linearized NLS dynamics. To address the lack of compactness inherent to the real line, the authors combine this estimate with a truncated Poincaré inequality and a space-time weight function that quantifies the spatial decay of solutions.

Result: The authors prove that the weakly damped stochastic NLS equation has a unique stationary measure within the class of probability measures concentrated on H^2, and establish polynomial mixing in the dual-Lipschitz metric over H^1, assuming sufficiently non-degenerate noise, without any restriction on the damping size.

Conclusion: The paper establishes ergodicity and polynomial mixing for the stochastic NLS equation on the real line under general damping conditions, overcoming the loss of compactness through novel weighted estimates and a coupling procedure.

Abstract: We consider the weakly damped stochastic nonlinear Schrödinger (NLS) equation on the real line, driven by a noise that is white in time and smooth in space. Assuming that the noise is sufficiently non-degenerate, we prove that the equation has a unique stationary measure in the class of probability measures concentrated on $H^2$, and establish polynomial mixing in the dual-Lipschitz metric over $H^1$. We do not impose any restriction on the size of the damping. The proof is based on a coupling argument, whose key ingredient is a Foiaş-Prodi-type estimate in the $H^1$-norm, derived by means of a Lyapunov functional adapted to the linearized NLS dynamics. To compensate for the loss of compactness, we combine this estimate with a truncated Poincaré inequality and a space-time weight function quantifying the spatial decay of solutions.

</details>


<div id='math.FA'></div>

# math.FA [[Back]](#toc)

### [203] [A class of port-Hamiltonian systems with general time delays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05726)
*Rogelio Arancibia Bustos,Bálint Farkas,Birgit Jacob,Merlin Schmitz*

Main category: math.FA

TL;DR: This paper defines a class of infinite-dimensional linear port-Hamiltonian delay systems, provides a characterization for such systems, and offers an easily verifiable sufficient condition for membership in this class, extending prior work by Breiten, Hinsen and Unger.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the study of port-Hamiltonian delay systems to the infinite-dimensional setting, building on prior finite-dimensional results by Breiten, Hinsen and Unger, and to provide tractable criteria for identifying such systems.

Method: The analysis relies on operator semigroup theory for linear delay PDEs to characterize infinite-dimensional linear port-Hamiltonian delay systems and derive a sufficient condition for belonging to this class.

Result: A formal characterization of the class of infinite-dimensional linear port-Hamiltonian delay systems is provided, along with an easily verifiable sufficient condition for such systems, extending the previous work to the infinite-dimensional case.

Conclusion: The paper successfully extends the theory of port-Hamiltonian delay systems to infinite dimensions using operator semigroup methods, offering both a characterization and a practical sufficient condition for identifying these systems.

Abstract: We define a class of infinite dimensional linear port-Hamiltonian delay systems, give a characterization for such systems, and provide an easily verifiable sufficient condition to belong to this class, thereby extending the study by Breiten, Hinsen and Unger in [IEEE Trans. Autom. Control vol. 69, no. 12 (2024), pp. 8924-8930, doi: 10.1109/TAC.2024.3464332]. The results rely on the operator semigroup theoretic treatment of linear delay PDEs.

</details>


### [204] [On Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces associated with the Grushin operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05940)
*Sayan Bagchi,Nishta Garg,Rahul Garg*

Main category: math.FA

TL;DR: The paper defines classical and non-classical Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces associated with the Grushin operator (with or without drift), establishes characterizations, and studies their complex interpolation, embeddings, and fractional Leibniz rules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize function space theory (Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces) to the setting of the Grushin operator, a degenerate subelliptic operator, both with and without drift, and to understand the structural properties of these spaces.

Method: The authors define classical and non-classical Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin spaces adapted to the Grushin operator, then systematically analyze their characterizations, interpolation behavior, embeddings, and fractional Leibniz-type product rules.

Result: The paper provides characterizations of the newly defined spaces, establishes complex interpolation results, derives embedding properties, and proves fractional Leibniz rules for these spaces.

Conclusion: The study extends classical Besov and Triebel-Lizorkin space theory to the degenerate Grushin setting, yielding a comprehensive framework with interpolation, embedding, and product rule results.

Abstract: In this article, we define classical and non-classical Besov and Triebel--Lizorkin spaces associated with the Grushin operator with or without drift. We establish various characterisations of these spaces, and study their complex interpolation, embedding properties, and fractional Leibniz rules.

</details>


<div id='astro-ph.IM'></div>

# astro-ph.IM [[Back]](#toc)

### [205] [Full Characterisation of the Polarisation Primary Beam of the GRAO 32-m Telescope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08700)
*Theophilus Ansah-Narh,Nia Imara,Benedicta Woode,Joseph Bremang Tandoh,Emmanuel Proven Adzri,Diana Klutse,Evaristus Uzochukwu Iyida*

Main category: astro-ph.IM

TL;DR: This paper develops a quantitative, direction-dependent polarimetric beam model for the GRAO 32-m telescope at 5.0 and 6.7 GHz using GRASP electromagnetic simulations, characterising beam shape, squint, instrumental Stokes leakage, and intrinsic cross-polarisation ratio (IXR) across the primary beam to establish a baseline for polarimetric calibration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direction-dependent instrumental polarisation is a major systematic limitation in high-fidelity single-dish radio polarimetry, yet no unified characterisation of beam leakage and polarisation recovery conditioning exists for the GRAO 32-m telescope.

Method: High-resolution GRASP electromagnetic simulations of the nominal telescope configuration are used to derive the full complex Jones response and corresponding Mueller matrices, from which beam shape, beam squint, instrumental Stokes leakage, and IXR are evaluated across the primary beam.

Result: Stokes I beams have HPBWs of 396.4 and 295.3 arcsec at 5.0 and 6.7 GHz with main-beam efficiencies of 56.4% and 55.6%. A circular-polarisation beam squint of 9.96 arcsec (2.5% of HPBW) is found at 5.0 GHz but none at 6.7 GHz. Stokes I leakage into linear polarisation is below 0.1% within the half-power beam but rises in sidelobes. IXR reaches ~80 dB on axis and decreases with angular offset.

Conclusion: The results establish the intrinsic electromagnetic polarimetric response of the GRAO 32-m telescope, providing a quantitative baseline for direction-dependent calibration and subsequent observational validation.

Abstract: Direction-dependent instrumental polarisation is a major systematic limitation in high-fidelity single-dish radio polarimetry, yet a unified characterisation of beam leakage and the conditioning of polarisation recovery is lacking for the Ghana Radio Astronomy Observatory (GRAO) 32-m telescope. We aim to establish a quantitative, direction-dependent polarimetric beam model at 5.0 and 6.7 GHz. High-resolution \texttt{GRASP} electromagnetic simulations of the nominal telescope configuration are used to derive the full complex Jones response and corresponding Mueller matrices, from which beam shape, beam squint, instrumental Stokes leakage, and intrinsic cross-polarisation ratio (IXR) are evaluated across the primary beam. The Stokes $I$ beams have half-power beam widths of $396.4$ and $295.3$ arcsec at 5.0 and 6.7 GHz, respectively, with main-beam efficiencies of $56.4$ and $55.6$ per cent. At 5.0 GHz, the circular-polarisation beam squint is $9.96$ arcsec ($2.5$ per cent of the HPBW), whereas no statistically significant squint is detected at 6.7 GHz. Leakage from Stokes $I$ into linear polarisation remains below $0.1$ per cent within the half-power beam but increases substantially towards the sidelobes. IXR reaches approximately $80$ dB on axis and decreases with angular offset. These results establish the intrinsic electromagnetic polarimetric response of the GRAO 32-m telescope and provide a quantitative baseline for direction-dependent calibration and subsequent observational validation.

</details>


<div id='cond-mat.mes-hall'></div>

# cond-mat.mes-hall [[Back]](#toc)

### [206] [One-dimensional quasicrystals with tensor-network finite-state automata](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06040)
*Milla Kolehmainen,Jose L. Lado,Anouar Moustaj*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: This paper develops a tensor-network framework for simulating quasicrystals by exploiting substitution rules to construct deterministic finite automata, which are then mapped to matrix product states/operators with system-size-independent bond dimension. This enables exact representation of tight-binding Hamiltonians and spectral density computation for systems exceeding 10^9 sites, resolving hierarchical spectral structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Simulating quasicrystals is computationally challenging because they lack the translational symmetry used for crystals and typically require costly diagonalization of large finite approximants.

Method: The authors leverage the equivalence between substitution rules (written in a numeration system) and deterministic finite automata with output. They further show that transition matrices correspond exactly to matrix product state tensors with bond dimension equal to the number of automaton states, independent of system size. This yields exact matrix product operators for quasicrystal Hamiltonians, combined with the kernel polynomial method for spectral computations.

Result: The framework is demonstrated for the metallic-mean and k-bonacci families, with explicit constructions for Fibonacci, silver-mean, and Tribonacci quasicrystals. Spectral densities are computed for chains with over 10^9 sites, directly resolving the fractal hierarchical structure of the spectrum.

Conclusion: The substitution-rule-to-automaton-to-tensor-network mapping provides an exact, efficient representation of quasicrystal Hamiltonians at arbitrary system size, enabling large-scale spectral computations that were previously intractable.

Abstract: Quasicrystals occupy a distinctive position between the translational order of crystals and the disordered amorphous matter. Simulating this physics has remained challenging, since quasiperiodic structures lack the translational symmetry exploited for crystals and generally require costly diagonalization of large finite approximants. Quasicrystalline order admits two equivalent descriptions, a cut-and-project scheme from a higher-dimensional periodic crystal, and a discrete set of substitution rules acting on a finite alphabet. We show that the latter, written in a numeration system adapted to the substitution, defines a deterministic finite automaton with output, the digits of a site index are fed, and the automaton returns the letter occupying that site. We further exploit another equivalence to a different construction, the transition matrices are exactly the tensors of a matrix product state, whose bond dimension is the number of automaton states and is independent of system size. This allows efficient representation of extremely large tight-binding Hamiltonians in the tensor-train language, thereby yielding an exact matrix product operator for the quasicrystal Hamiltonian at any system size. We show how this framework works for two families of one-dimensional quasicrystals, the metallic-mean and $k$-bonacci families and we explicitly construct the Fibonacci, silver-mean, and Tribonacci quasicrystals. By leveraging efficient tensor-network compression and the kernel polynomial method, we compute spectral densities for chains with more than $10^9$ sites and directly resolve the hierarchical structure of the spectrum.

</details>


### [207] [Continuum Limit of Nonlocal Electrostatics in Random Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06354)
*Prashant K. Jha,Kaushik Dayal*

Main category: cond-mat.mes-hall

TL;DR: This paper derives a two-scale continuum limit for the electrostatic energy of stationary, ergodic random charge density fields on lattices, proving almost sure convergence of local and nonlocal energies to deterministic limits, with applications to random-displacement models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how microscopic random charge fluctuations on a lattice give rise to macroscopic electrostatic energy behavior, particularly how cell-level charge neutrality with fluctuating dipole moments affects the continuum limit of Coulomb energies.

Method: The authors formulate charge density fields that are stationary and ergodic under lattice translations with cell charge neutrality and fluctuating dipole moments. They derive and prove (under assumptions on stationary microscopic potentials and finite-volume fields) that local and nonlocal electrostatic energies converge almost surely to deterministic two-scale limits, distinguishing ensemble-averaged microscopic energies, cell-depolarization terms, and effective polarization contributions. They also verify assumptions for an independent-cell random-displacement model.

Result: Local energy limits consist of ensemble-averaged microscopic Coulomb energy plus a cell-depolarization term; the nonlocal limit is governed by effective polarization. Cell-depolarization terms cancel in total energy, leaving ensemble-averaged microscopic cell energy plus macroscopic field energy. The deterministic case recovers the periodic two-scale limit. Centered microscopic fluctuations can affect local energy without altering effective polarization or nonlocal energy.

Conclusion: A general rigorous two-scale continuum limit framework is established for random electrostatic systems, unifying deterministic periodic results as a special case and revealing that only the macroscopic polarization (not the centered fluctuations) governs nonlocal energy, while local energy does depend on fluctuation statistics.

Abstract: We derive a two-scale continuum limit for the electrostatic energy of random charge density fields that are stationary and ergodic under lattice translations. Each microscopic cell is charge neutral in every realization, while its dipole moment may fluctuate and have a nonzero mean. Under assumptions on the stationary microscopic potential and the finite-volume fields, the local and nonlocal energies converge almost surely to deterministic limits. The local limit consists of the ensemble-averaged microscopic Coulomb energy and a cell-depolarization term, whereas the effective polarization determines the nonlocal limit. The cell-depolarization terms cancel in the total energy, which consists of the ensemble-averaged microscopic cell energy and the macroscopic electrostatic field energy. The deterministic specialization recovers the periodic two-scale limit. For an independent-cell random-displacement model, we verify the assumptions of the continuum-limit theorem and show that centered microscopic fluctuations can change the local energy without changing the effective polarization or the nonlocal energy.

</details>
